# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.
From 0.6.0 on, [release-please](https://github.com/googleapis/release-please) writes each release's
section from the titles of the pull requests merged into `main` (Conventional Commits:
CONTRIBUTING.md, "Pull request titles and commit messages"); optional hand-written notes go under
`## [Unreleased]` and move into the next release's section as "Details". The sections up to 0.5.0
were written by hand and follow [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/).

## [Unreleased]

- **The project page in Polish, for Polish airsoft (ASG) players: an AI draft awaiting a native review** (decisions SITE-PL-1 to SITE-PL-9; new `site/i18n/pl.yaml` and the generated `site/pl/` pages, `tools/build_site.py` `LANGUAGES`, `PAGES` and the new `mode_gifs`, `site/src/partials/head.html`, `header.html`, `site/src/index.html`, `modes.html`, `site/style.css` and Barlow's latin-ext files in `site/fonts/`, the flag font with 🇵🇱, `site/img/diagrams/*-pl.svg`, `site/sitemap.xml`, `tools/site_browser_test.py`, `docs/translations/status.yaml`, `glossary.md`).
- **The maintainer's review of the stuck-box recovery: every open STUCK-* decision confirmed, two changed** (decisions STUCK-62, STUCK-64, STUCK-68 to STUCK-70; RFC 0001 Amendment 28 no longer proposed). The phone controller has the Pi's whole clock guard (`app/lib/controller/controller_clock.dart` (new): the floor from the release date, the highest time seen and the store's newest record, jumps that hold the retention purges, the GM's forward-only correction through `GET`/`PUT /api/v1/clock` and `clock` in the status, so the console's banner and *Settings* card work on the phone; `controller_engine.dart`, `controller_api.dart`, `controller_store.dart`, `controller_checkin.dart`, `controller_branding.dart`, `controller_stuck.dart`; tests `controller_clock_test.dart`). The Pi's TIME beacons carry the GM-corrected clock as RELEASE does (`controller/atalaia_controller/engine.py`), so a box's join time and a RELEASE are on one clock. `GET /api/v1/boxes` and the stream's `boxes` message carry `radio_kind` on both controllers, and the console's *Stuck in an ended game* hint says to use a USB radio modem for an I/O pod instead of updating its firmware (`reinvite.py`, `controller_boxes.dart`, `stuck-boxes.js`, `app.js`, `sw.js`, new string `stuck.no_watch_pod` in en, pt-PT, de and zh-TW). Docs: field-frames.md, the amendment, the controller and app READMEs, the game-master and controller-setup manuals (en, pt), phase 5 STATUS and the decisions file.

- **A Field Box's `@wifi` takes a Wi-Fi name and password with spaces, in double quotes** (decisions WIFI-1 to WIFI-9; `firmware/esp32/main/wifi_args.c`, `wifi_args.h` (new), `shell.c`, `fw_update_esp.c`; `firmware/esp32/test/test_wifi_args.c` (new); the firmware README, BRING_UP.md, `protocol/firmware-update.md`, the firmware and Field Box manuals in English and Portuguese): `@wifi "My Field" "pass word"`, with `\"` and `\\` inside the quotes. The unquoted form works as before. An unclosed quote, an unknown escape or text after a closing quote is refused with its reason, and nothing is stored. The console now also refuses a 64-character password that is not 64 hex digits (WPA2 takes 8-63 characters or 64 hex digits).
- **The setup portal refuses a Wi-Fi password the box cannot use, as `@wifi` does** (decision WIFI-7, changed by the maintainer on 2026-11-05; `firmware/esp32/main/wifi_setup.c`, `wifi_setup.h`, `wifi_setup.html`; `firmware/esp32/test/test_wifi_setup.c`; `firmware/esp32/docs/BRING_UP.md`; `docs/manuals/en` and `pt`: `firmware.md`): the field Wi-Fi password is none, 8-63 characters or 64 hex digits (the console's `wifi_pass_valid`). Before, the portal took any 8-64 characters and cut a longer one to 64 without saying so. The page's label and the refusal in en, pt-PT, de and zh-TW say "8-63 characters or 64 hex digits".
- **The Android controller frees boxes stuck in an ended game, as the Raspberry Pi does** (decisions STUCK-60 to STUCK-67; `app/lib/controller/controller_stuck.dart` (new), `controller_boxes.dart`, `controller_boxes_api.dart`, `controller_engine.dart`, `controller_radio.dart`, `serial_modem.dart`, `controller_host.dart`, `controller_isolate.dart`, `controller_screen.dart`, `app/lib/pod/pod_codec.dart`, the four app language files; `app/test/controller/controller_stuck_test.dart` (new), `serial_modem_games_test.dart` (new), `serial_modem_language_test.dart`, `controller_screen_test.dart`, `boxes_e2e_test.dart`, `app/test/support/recording_modem.dart`; `protocol/field-frames.md`, `protocol/usb-modem.md`, `docs/rfcs/0001-skirmesh-rf-wire-format.md`, `controller/console/README.md` and two console comments; `app/README.md`; `docs/manuals/en` and `pt`: `game-master.md`): the phone controller sends WATCH_GAMES to a USB radio modem whose IDENT says `games`, keeps the last 50 ended games' key tags, and resends an ended game's GAME_OVER when the modem reports a box still in it (once a minute per game, never from a standby). `GET /api/v1/boxes` and the stream carry `stuck`, `stuck_reports` and `stuck_watch`, so the console it serves shows **Stuck in an ended game** with **Release** (`POST /api/v1/boxes/stuck/{game}/release`) and *Release every box not in this game* (`POST /api/v1/boxes/release`, RFC 0001 Amendment 28), with the Pi's confirmation, answers, rate limits and refusals (a standby, an implausible clock, a game live on another channel). With an older modem or an I/O pod, which cannot report such boxes, the phone's screen says so under *Radio* (new strings in en, pt-PT, de and zh-TW). Tested in Dart and end to end with the C host modem and boxes; not yet on a real phone.
- **RELEASE frees every box not in the live game, for a controller that lost the game** (RFC 0001 Amendment 28, proposed; decisions STUCK-50 to STUCK-59; `core/include/atalaia/field_frame.h`, `core/src/field_frame.c`, `core/tests/test_field_frame.c`; `simulator/atalaia_sim/field_frame.py`, `lobby.py`, `simulator/tests/test_reinvite_sim.py`; `app/lib/protocol/field_frame.dart` and its test; `protocol/vectors/field_frame.json`, `tools/field_frame_vectors.py`, `tools/protogen.py`; `firmware/esp32/main/box_lobby.c`, `box_game.c`, `box_game.h`, `radio/field_link.c`, `radio/field_link.h`, `radio/radio_nvs_esp.c`, `shell.c`, `firmware/esp32/test/test_box_game.c`, `box_host/box_host.c`; `controller/atalaia_controller/stuck_games.py`, `reinvite.py`, `api.py`; `controller/tests/test_reinvite.py`, `test_box_e2e.py`; `controller/console/src/components/release-boxes.js` (new), `app.js`, `sw.js`, the four language files, `controller/console/tests/test_browser.py`; `protocol/field-frames.md`, `docs/rfcs/0001-skirmesh-rf-wire-format.md`, `controller/README.md`, `controller/console/README.md`, `firmware/esp32/README.md`; `docs/manuals/en` and `pt`: `game-master.md`, `field-box.md`): a reinstalled controller, or one whose database was lost, has no key tag for the game a box is stuck in, so no GAME_OVER can name it. The GM's *Release every box not in this game* (Settings, behind a confirmation) sends field frame kind 6 (the live game's id or 0, and the wall clock), three times. A box in another game ends it as on GAME_OVER only if it joined that game before the RELEASE's time: the wall clock of the first TIME it took in that game, which it now keeps in NVS, so a recording played back once the box is in a newer game changes nothing. Refused from a standby, with an implausible clock, and more than once in 30 s. The modem is unchanged; a box from before ignores the kind. Tested in C, Python, Dart, end to end with the C host box and modem, and in Chromium; not yet on hardware.
- **The GM console lists the boxes stuck in an ended game, with a Release button** (decisions STUCK-40 to STUCK-47; `controller/console/src/components/stuck-boxes.js` (new), `box-invites.js`, `app.js`, `sw.js`, the four language files; `controller/atalaia_controller/stuck_games.py`, `reinvite.py`, `api.py`; `controller/tests/test_reinvite.py`, `controller/console/tests/test_browser.py`; `controller/README.md`, `controller/console/README.md`, `protocol/field-frames.md`; `docs/manuals/en` and `pt`: `game-master.md`, `field-box.md`): the *Boxes* tab (and the *Boxes for this game* dialog when it has one) shows **Stuck in an ended game**: each ended game a box was heard still in, with its name, the node ids heard, how long ago, the signal and the resends, live from the stream's `boxes` message, which now carries `stuck`. **Release** sends that game's GAME_OVER at once through the new `POST /api/v1/boxes/stuck/{game}/release` (GM; the usual channel rules, refused with a game live on another channel, one burst per game in 10 s; answers `game_over_frame` as End does). With a modem that cannot report stuck boxes the list says to update its firmware and to use the box's *Leave the game* or `@leave`. Tested against the C host modem in Chromium; not yet on hardware.
- **A Field Box stuck in a game that ended while it was off leaves it at the box, with the admin PIN** (decisions STUCK-20 to STUCK-33; `firmware/esp32/main/box_game.c`, `box_game.h`, `preset_ui.c`, `preset_ui.h`, `shell.c`, `box_text.c`, `box_text.h`, `screen_renderer.c`, `oled_view.c`; `firmware/esp32/test/test_box_game.c`, `test_preset_ui.c`, `test_screens.c`, `test_oled_screens.c`, `box_host/box_host.c`, new screen references; `docs/manuals/en` and `pt`: `field-box.md`, `firmware.md`, `game-master.md`; `firmware/esp32/docs/BRING_UP.md`; `protocol/field-frames.md`): holding `*` for 3 s during a field game opens the admin PIN, then **Leave the game** (pt **Sair do jogo**, de **Spiel verlassen**, zh-TW **離開遊戲**) and a confirmation; `@leave <admin PIN>` on the USB console does the same, gated as `@joinkey` (the PIN's count and waits apply; a bench build takes `@leave` alone). The box ends its game exactly as a GAME_OVER for it would (the mode's game over, then announcing itself and Ready), keeps it as played to its end so it never joins it again, and keeps its adoption, join key, Wi-Fi, admin PIN and modes; a new join is taken at once. It also works in a running game, so a GM can pull a box out. No wire change: the box's log says it left by hand, and the controller sees its STATE "ended". Before, the only way out was erasing NVS and the storage partition over USB. Tested on a computer only.
- **The controller frees a box still in a game that ended while it was off** (decisions STUCK-1 to STUCK-17, RFC 0001 Amendment 27, proposed; `firmware/esp32/main/modem/modem.c`, `modem.h`, `modem_framing.h`, `firmware/esp32/test/test_modem.c`; `controller/atalaia_controller/stuck_games.py` (new), `engine.py`, `reinvite.py`, `radio_modem.py`, `modem_framing.py`, `store.py`; `controller/tests/test_reinvite.py`, `test_radio_modem.py`, `test_modem_host.py`, `test_box_e2e.py`, `support.py`; `protocol/usb-modem.md`, `protocol/field-frames.md`; `controller/README.md`, `firmware/esp32/README.md`; `docs/manuals/en` and `pt`: `field-box.md`, `firmware.md`, `game-master.md`, `modem.md`): the USB modem reports another game's packets when asked (CMD WATCH_GAMES `0x0A`, frame OTHER_GAME `0x0C`: the header's game id and source, RSSI and SNR, at most once per game id in 10 s and 8 game ids per 10 s; IDENT `"games": true`, a development build's long version cut so IDENT still fits). The Raspberry Pi controller asks after every IDENT that says so, keeps each ended game's key tag (the last 50), and when it hears a box in one of its ended games it sends that game's GAME_OVER again by the usual channel rules, at most once a minute per game, also after a restart without the key; a standby never does. `GET /api/v1/boxes` adds `stuck`, `stuck_reports` and `stuck_watch` (no console UI yet). Nothing changes on air or in the boxes. The modem needs this firmware; with an older one the controller logs it once and the box is taken out with `@leave` or the admin menu. Tested on a computer only, end to end with the C host box and host modem.

### Details

- **`check_wiring.py` checks the I/O pod's pins on the project page's build page** (decisions WIRE-1 to WIRE-4, answering SITE9-2; `tools/check_wiring.py`, `site/src/build.html`, the generated build pages, `site/README.md`, `tools/README.md`): the cells of the page's pod section (`<section id="io-pod">`) are checked against the pod build's defaults, Kconfig with `firmware/esp32/sdkconfig.defaults` and `sdkconfig.pod` over it, as the pod guide's table already was (which now reads the same files instead of naming `ATALAIA_POD_MODE`); every other cell keeps the Field Box's. The pod's two simple outputs, GPIO 33 and 1, become marked cells (`data-kconfig`), and the section must keep a marked cell for each of its pins, so a wrong number or a dropped mark fails the check with the section named. The build page's target section no longer says the pod will lend a target "later": the pod already lends a target addon's hits to a phone's game (increment 9), tested on a computer only (pt, en, de and zh-TW; SITE9-4). The decisions file gains the rows the release key rotation's entries cite (KRT-1 to KRT-12, reconstructed from the sources; KRT-0 was already there).

- **The project page's I/O pod after increment 9: its simple outputs and its expansion port** (decisions SITE9-1 to SITE9-7; `site/src/build.html`, `site/i18n/pt.yaml`, `en.yaml`, `de.yaml` and `zh-tw.yaml`, the generated pages, `README.md`): the build page's I/O pod section says what the pod now drives: simple outputs on GPIO 33, then 1 (wired as the Field Box's, enabled by building them in, off when the phone goes) and the expansion port (a target's hits; an output module's relays only on a pod built to lend them, as many as fit in its description to the phone), with two parts bullets, two pin rows, the build options in the flash step, and the open item that said "do not wire one to a pod yet" (SITEB-2) replaced by what waits for the bench. The Field Box simple output's "the I/O pod gets it later", the pod's settings item (the GM console pushes four over the radio) and the Portuguese "(em inglês)" before a Portuguese guide are corrected; the roadmap's props list and README's phase 3 lines name the pod's outputs and port. In software and on a computer only, as the page says.
- **The I/O pod lends as many outputs as fit in IDENT, and keeps the others off** (decisions EXPI9-27 to EXPI9-33, answering EXPI9-23; `firmware/esp32/main/pod/pod.c`, `pod.h`, `pod_port.c`, `pod_port.h`, `pod_outputs.c`, `pod_outputs.h`, `pod_task.c`, `outputs.c`, `outputs.h`; `firmware/esp32/test/test_pod.c`, `test_ble_gatt_stub.c`, `pod_host/pod_host.c`; `protocol/vectors/capability_proxy.yaml` and `core/tests/proxy_vectors.h`; `protocol/capability-proxy.md`, `firmware/esp32/README.md`, `hardware/io-pod/BUILD.md`, `BUILD.pt.md`, `docs/rfcs/0000-expansion-port.md`): when IDENT's 256 bytes cannot hold every output, the pod no longer drops them all; it leaves them out one at a time, the last first (`POD_IDENT_TRIM`), and logs which. An output left out is withheld until an IDENT lists it again: turned off at once if it was on, its CMDs answered as for a capability the pod lacks, refused by the output layer, and left out of the EVT `pod.modules`. A pod with every part and an out4 module now lends one of the four channels (new IDENT vector `pod_full_out4_trimmed`); with a target too, the hit sensor still fits and no output does. Before, an output left out of IDENT could still be switched by its id.
- **The I/O pod's expansion port, and a phone prop's outputs and modules in the console (increment 9 of the draft RFC "The expansion port", its port half)** (decisions EXPI9-18 to EXPI9-26, following EXP-11, EXP-21, EXP-26 and EXP-28; draft RFC 0000 Amendment 7; `firmware/esp32/main/pod/pod_port.c`, `pod_port.h`, `pod.c`, `pod.h`, `pod_outputs.c`, `pod_task.c`, `Kconfig.projbuild`, `CMakeLists.txt`; `firmware/esp32/test/test_pod.c`, `pod_host/`; `app/lib/prop/prop_hits.dart`, `prop_outputs.dart`, `prop_session.dart`, `app/lib/pod/pod_client.dart`; `controller/atalaia_controller/prop_link.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_props.dart`; `protocol/vectors/capability_proxy.yaml`, `controller_api.json`; `tools/docker_fw.py`). The pod runs the box's port driver on its I2C bus (`CONFIG_ATALAIA_POD_PORT`, on by default): a target module's `hit` (and `hit.2`) is lent to the phone, armed by the phone's shell only while the game runs; an output module's channels are lent as `output` on a pod built with `CONFIG_ATALAIA_POD_MODULE_OUTPUTS` (`docker_fw.py pod --module-outputs=on`), the pod holding the module's lease and turning them off when the phone goes. The pod tells the phone a box's `node.modules` text and each output's longest on (EVT `pod.modules`), and the phone tells the controller, so a prop's outputs and modules show in the console and the phone uses the pod's longest on instead of 30 s (EXPI9-12 closed). Both controllers relay a CMD naming dest 65535 to every Wi-Fi prop (EXPI9-13 closed: All outputs off already reached one, since every-node CMDs go without dest). `pod_host --module`, `--module-outputs`, `--module-max-on`. Tested on a computer only.
- **The project page's build page after increments 8 and 11 of the expansion port: the target module's firmware and the I2C output module** (decisions SITE8-1 to SITE8-12, closing EXPI11-16; `site/src/build.html`, `site/i18n/pt.yaml`, `en.yaml`, `de.yaml` and `zh-tw.yaml`, the generated pages, `site/README.md`, `README.md`, `docs/translations/glossary.md`): the "Coming with the firmware (increment 8)" lists are replaced by what is built (the box reading the port, its log lines and `@modules`; the target module's pin table, GP4/GP5 I2C, GP26/GP27 ADC, GP2/GP3 jumpers, a GP14 LED; how to flash its UF2 file from a release after 0.7.0, checked against `SHA256SUMS`; hits reaching scripts as `hit.hit`), and what still needs real hardware is listed "After the bench" (the module's current and 3V3, the threshold and strength on real plates, the divider values, the port's tests with a module, Target Windows as a built-in preset). A new section for the I2C output module (the isolator, its own 9-15 V supply, high-level-trigger relay boards, the never-mains and smoke-remote-only rules, its pins, its UF2 file, what waits for the bench). The Target Windows card on the modes page, the roadmap's phase 4 and 5 items and phase 5's headline, and the README's status, roadmap row and folder table follow. In Portuguese, English, German and Traditional Chinese (German and Chinese AI drafts). Text only: nothing built on hardware.
- **The project page's build page: the expansion port, the target addon and the simple output** (decisions SITEB-1 to SITEB-9; `site/src/build.html`, `site/i18n/pt.yaml`, `en.yaml`, `de.yaml` and `zh-tw.yaml`, the generated pages, `site/README.md`, `README.md`, `docs/translations/glossary.md`): a new section for the expansion port (what it is, its parts and what each protects against, the M12 pin table with SDA and SCL checked against the Kconfig defaults, the addresses, the steps and checks, the bench tests a builder can run now, the rules, a photo placeholder), the target addon rewritten as buildable (its parts, steps and meter checks), and the simple output (an RC relay switch on one GPIO for a siren, a strobe or a smoke remote: what it needs, its pin per board revision, low-voltage loads only) in the Field Box's section, with a parts bullet and a step for the Satellite and the I/O pod's open item saying it waits for the pod firmware. What waits for increment 8 (the target module's firmware, its pin table and UF2 files, the box reading the port) is listed as coming, not filled in. The safety rule on GPIO 5 and 46 names the simple output's exception; the Target Windows card no longer says the target has a light ring; the build guides line on the roadmap, the page's description and the README's hardware row name the expansion port. In Portuguese, English, German and Traditional Chinese (German and Chinese AI drafts). Text only: nothing built on hardware.
- **The I2C output module (increment 11 of the draft RFC "The expansion port")** (decisions EXPI11-1 to EXPI11-19, following EXP-13, EXP-16, EXP-17 and EXP-31; `firmware/output-module/` (`src/output.c`, `output.h`, `main.c`, `board.h`, `test/test_output_module.c`, `CMakeLists.txt`, `README.md`), `hardware/output-module/BUILD.md`, `BUILD.pt.md` and `README.md`, `hardware/README.md`, `hardware/expansion-port/BUILD.md` and `BUILD.pt.md`, `tools/check_wiring.py`, `tools/docker_module.py`, `tools/release_package.py`, `tools/check.sh`, `tools/README.md`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`): the later, optional output path on the expansion port. Its firmware (model `out4`, an RP2040 board at 0x5C plus two jumpers) runs on the module core, with the module's own limits from a channel list set at build time (`OUTPUT_CHANNELS`: relay or solid-state, hint, max on, min off, supply sense; four generic relays at 30 s by default, refused by CMake and again at power-up), every channel pin driven low before anything else, a guard that turns every channel off for good if one overstays its max on (an independent check under the core's), a 500 ms hardware watchdog fed only while the core's timers run on time, and `SUPPLY_OK` from a divider on ADC0 with hysteresis (9.0 V on, 8.5 V off). 277 host checks, the `out4` reference module of `expansion.json` read back byte for byte; CI's new **Expansion output module host tests** job runs them with warnings as errors and under ASan and UBSan, and increment 8's module job (now **Target and output modules**) and the release build its UF2 (`atalaia-output-module-<version>.uf2`, the default list). The build guide, in English and Portuguese: the I2C isolator between the box side (powered from the port, no pull-ups) and the module side, the module's own 9-15 V supply (fuse, reverse diode, 5 V buck), relay boards with high-level trigger only and pull-downs, or a MOSFET board, dry contacts with one fuse per load and flyback diodes, **never mains** and **smoke machines only through their documented low-voltage remote input**, the pin table (checked by `check_wiring.py` against `board.h`), assembly, meter checks, flashing and first power-up, and what waits for the bench (the isolator at the port's 3.0 V, the bus's low level, the relays at 3.3 V, the lease with a stopwatch, noise). Not built or run on hardware.

- **The I/O pod's simple outputs, and the phone prop that uses them (increment 9 of the draft RFC "The expansion port", its simple-output half)** (decisions EXPI9-1 to EXPI9-17, following EXP-26, EXP-28, EXP-29 and EXP-34; `firmware/esp32/main/pod/pod_outputs.c`, `pod_outputs.h`, `pod.c`, `pod.h`, `pod_task.c`, `pod_json.c`, `simple_out_esp.c`, `Kconfig.projbuild`, `CMakeLists.txt`; `firmware/esp32/test/test_pod.c`, `test_ble_gatt_stub.c`, `pod_host/`; `app/lib/prop/prop_outputs.dart`, `box_outputs.dart` (moved from `app/test/support/`), `prop_session.dart`, `app/lib/radio/rf_codec.dart`; `app/test/prop/prop_outputs_test.dart`, `prop_pod_outputs_test.dart`; `protocol/vectors/capability_proxy.yaml`, `capability-proxy.md`, `prop-link.md`; `tools/docker_fw.py`, `tools/check_wiring.py`; `hardware/io-pod/BUILD.md` and `BUILD.pt.md`; the firmware, Field Box and GM manuals in en and pt): a pod built with `--simple-out=on` (`on2` for a second) drives an RC relay switch on GPIO 33 (and GPIO 1) with the Field Box's output layer and RMT bursts, its use and longest on set in its build (a pod has no admin PIN), and lends it to the phone as an `output` capability with `"feedback": false`. The pod keeps the output safe by itself: its build's longest on, a change at most every 500 ms, on only while a phone is on a link, every output off at once when the link goes and after 1.5 s without a word from the phone while one is on (the app pings it twice a second then), and pulses that stop within 0.5 s if the pod hangs. The app's prop maps the mode's `cap("siren")` calls and the controller's CMD 12 to the pod's outputs with a box's rules (on only while the game runs and in the end's 10 s grace, off at its start, pause, end and a halted script, 30 s at most, two starts a second from a script) and tells the controller `output.switched`. New vectors: a pod's IDENT with a simple output and the keepalive PING. The expansion port on the pod (a target's hits, the I2C lease) is the increment's other half, after the box's port driver (increments 6 to 8). Not tried on hardware.

- **Bluetooth pairing with the I/O pod is safe against a man in the middle** (PF11's follow-up; decisions PODPK-1 to PODPK-11; `firmware/esp32/main/pod/pod_pair.c`, `pod_display.c`, `pod_ble_nimble.c`, `oled_view.c`, `app/lib/pod/pod_pairing.dart`, `BlePodPlugin.kt`): the pod pairs with LE Secure Connections and passkey entry. It is DisplayOnly: while a phone pairs, its OLED (dark otherwise) shows a random 6-digit passkey, the seconds left and its name, and Android's own pairing dialog asks for it. Only a link encrypted with a key made so carries frames, and Pod RX takes only authenticated writes. A wrong passkey, a cancelled or too slow pairing drop the link at once with **FAILED** or **TOO SLOW** on the OLED, and a phone that connects without pairing goes after 60 s. A bond made without a passkey (a pod firmware before this, Just Works) or one the pod no longer has is forgotten by the pod, which drops the link with HCI reason 0x05; the app reads that, removes Android's side of the bond and pairs again once by itself, or asks to forget the pod in the Bluetooth settings. Holding the board's PRG button 5 s clears every bond. The app shows a hint and clear messages in pt, en, de and zh-TW. `CONFIG_ATALAIA_POD_BLE_PASSKEY` (on) and `CONFIG_ATALAIA_POD_BOND_CLEAR_PIN` (0) are new. Host tests (the state machine, the OLED's texts and reference images, the GATT stub) and Dart tests with a fake plugin; not tried on hardware (bench checklist in `firmware/esp32/docs/BRING_UP.md`, "The I/O pod", step 11).

- **Port integration and the target module (increment 8 of the draft RFC "The expansion port")** (decisions EXPI8-1 to EXPI8-19; `firmware/esp32/main/expansion_box.c`, `expansion_box.h`, `outputs.c`, `outputs.h`, `outputs_admin.c`, `box_game.c`, `box_game.h`, `node_runner.c`, `node_runner.h`, `shell.c`, `box_text.c`, `screen_renderer.c`, `expansion.c`; `firmware/esp32/test/module_sim.c`, `test_expansion_box.c`, `box_host/`; `firmware/target-module/`; `tools/docker_module.py`, `tools/dev/module/Dockerfile`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `release.yml`; `tools/release_package.py`; `controller/tests/test_box_e2e.py`; `simulator/atalaia_sim/expansion.py`): the box starts its expansion port driver at boot and runs it in the game task. A module's hits reach the mode's script as `hit.hit` (`{"strength"}`, plus `"channel"` with two hit channels) while a game runs, the script's `cap("hit"):arm()` arms the plate (kept by id, so a target plugged in mid-game is armed at once), HELLO lists `hit`, `node.modules` carries the simple outputs' and the modules' entries and is told again on every change, and a module lost during a game is `node.module_lost`. A module's output channels join the output layer, disabled until enabled at the box, named beside the simple outputs (`siren.2`), with their durations and patterns passed whole to the module. `@modules` on the serial console. The port driver's tests now run on the module core (increment 6's C model removed), and `box_host --module target|out4` simulates modules on it; an end-to-end test plays *Target Windows* with a target module. The target module's firmware on pico-sdk 2.2.0 (`firmware/target-module/`: I2C target on GP4/GP5, the piezo on GP26 by DMA at 20 ksample/s, a peak detector with the box's threshold and hold-off, address jumpers on GP2/GP3, `LOCATE` on GP14, 48 MHz, a watchdog), built in a pinned Docker image (`tools/docker_module.py`), by CI's new **Target module** job, and as two release assets (`atalaia-target-module-<version>.uf2`, `-2-` for two plates) covered by `SHA256SUMS`, not signed. The guides' "after increment 8" parts (pins, power entry, flashing, the box's log and console), BRING_UP's step 8c, the GM and Field Box manuals (en, pt). EXP-14 reworded after the maintainer's answer to EXPI6-4. *Target Windows* is not yet a Field Box preset (once bench-tested). Not tried on hardware.

- **Walk-ins as guests on the site server, opt-in** (`[sync] push_walkins`, decisions PWI-1 to PWI-10; `protocol/sync.md`, "POST /api/v1/sync/checkins"): off by default, as before the walk-ins of a game made from the schedule go as a count only. With `push_walkins = true` on the Pi (or *Send walk-ins to the site server as guests* on the phone controller), each walk-in who is a player also goes under the outcomes' new optional `guests`: player id, display name and the declared replicas' type, class and chrono state; never a federation number, a team, a make and model or a ticket. The site server stores them as guest registrations (`source` `walkin`, no account, `checked_in`), deletes them 30 days after the game (ADR-P4-8's guest rule) and at once with the controller's erasure of the player; an import can no longer use `walkin` as its source. A site server that refuses `guests` gets the count only. Both controllers build the body with one rule (`sync_format.checkin_outcomes`, the phone's `checkinOutcomes`) held to new shared vectors (`checkin_outcomes`, 1 valid and 12 invalid check-ins bodies). No migration. The privacy notice templates gain rows for it, marked as the field's choice and for the lawyer.

- **The expansion port's driver, the box's side (increment 6 of the draft RFC "The expansion port")** (decisions EXPI6-1 to EXPI6-19, following EXP-7 to EXP-14 and EXP-19; `firmware/esp32/main/expansion.c`, `expansion.h`, `expansion_esp.c`, `expansion_esp.h`, `hal/esp/hal_esp_i2c.c`, `CMakeLists.txt`; `firmware/esp32/test/test_expansion.c`, `expansion_model.c`, `expansion_model.h`, `expansion_cases.h`, `CMakeLists.txt`; `tools/protogen.py`, `tools/expansion_vectors.py`, `protocol/vectors/expansion.json`'s description, `protocol/expansion-port.md`, `protocol/README.md`, `docs/rfcs/0000-expansion-port.md`, `hardware/target-addon/BUILD.md` and `BUILD.pt.md`, `firmware/esp32/README.md`). The box now has a driver for modules on the expansion port, platform-free and tested on the host, **not yet called by the game task** (increment 8): it finds modules at 0x58-0x5F (at boot `ALL_OFF` to each before anything else, then each empty address probed every 2 s, one every 250 ms, so a module plugged in is found within 2 s; at most 4), reads and checks their IDENT and CHANNEL descriptors, polls STATUS (20 ms with inputs, 250 ms output-only, up to 4 reads while MORE), reports each input event once with its time on the box's clock (`t_read - age`) and acknowledges it (a lost ACK does not count a hit twice; events of channels it has not armed are dropped), handles OVERFLOW, CMD_ERROR, LEASE_EXPIRED and FRESH (the IDENT read again: a different module there is the old one lost, `bad_ident`), drops a module after 25 failed reads in a row (`no_answer`), keeps each output channel's desired state reconciled with STATUS (sent again with what is left, rounded up to 100 ms) and refreshes its 1.5 s lease every 500 ms while an output should be on, with the module's max on, min off and a relay's 100 ms kept on the box's side too. Its ESP32 bus goes through `hal_esp_i2c`, so modules share the bus's health records and back-off (its device table grows from 8 to 16 entries). It replaces the target addon's stub `target_addon.c/.h` and its draft report format of P5J-19 (removed with `test_target_addon.c`). `test_expansion.c` checks every case of `protocol/vectors/expansion.json` (through `expansion_vectors.h` and a new generated `expansion_cases.h` with the cases' fields, reasons, modules and timelines), a C model of a module in the test tree (`expansion_model.c`, which increment 7's module core will replace) against every command case and all seven lease timelines, and the driver against the model on a fake bus: discovery, hot-plug, refused frames, retries, events, loss, a swapped module, the reconciliation, the lease, and four timelines the driver writes byte for byte. Not tried on hardware.
- **The expansion port's module core (increment 7 of the draft RFC "The expansion port")** (decisions EXPI7-1 to EXPI7-20, following EXP-9 to EXP-15; `firmware/module-core/` (`include/module_core.h`, `src/module_core.c`, `test/test_module_core.c`, `CMakeLists.txt`, `README.md`), `tools/protogen.py` (the new `firmware/module-core/test/module_core_vectors.h`), `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `tools/check.sh`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `protocol/expansion-port.md`): the modules' side of the expansion protocol v1, portable C with no platform calls and no allocation, for the target module (RP2040, increment 8), the I2C output module (increment 11) and community modules. The module's firmware hands it the I2C target's four moments (address match, byte received, byte requested, stop), a millisecond clock and its input events; the core answers `IDENT` (from the module's model, revision, version and unique id; classes and channel count from its channels), `CHANNEL` and `STATUS` (made at the read's address match, the event's age saturating at 65535 ms), takes or refuses every `COMMAND` with the protocol's reasons (`CMD_ERROR` until the next accepted write; a register pointer, the box's STATUS poll included, leaves it alone), queues 16 events with a sequence number (`MORE`; a full queue drops the newest and sets `OVERFLOW` until a STATUS that carries it is read in full), and switches the outputs under the module's hard limits: the lease (every output off and `LEASE_EXPIRED` when it runs out while one is on; a duration or a max on ending at the same moment ends by itself), each channel's max on and min off, patterns at least 100 ms on and off on a relay and 10 ms on a solid-state channel, a relay never turned on within 100 ms of turning off (also after a pattern's on time, a little stricter than the Python reference), and every output off at power-up. Tests: every vector of `protocol/vectors/expansion.json` (3 IDENT, 7 CHANNEL, 8 STATUS, 42 COMMAND and the seven lease timelines, step by step with the pins the module drives) and the state machine's edges (power-up, bad configurations, pointers and repeated starts, overlong writes, the queue's overflow and wrap, the lease and limits to the millisecond, a clock that wraps after 49 days), 915 checks; CI's new **Expansion module core host tests** job builds them with warnings as errors and again under AddressSanitizer and UBSan, and `tools/check.sh` runs them. Not in this increment: the box's port driver (increment 6, built alongside, whose tests' C model of a module this core replaces when both are in), a module's firmware on the core (increment 8). Not tried on hardware.

- **The expansion port's build guide and a buildable target addon** (draft RFC "The expansion port", increment 10; decisions EXPI10-1 to EXPI10-14; `hardware/expansion-port/BUILD.md` and `BUILD.pt.md`, `hardware/expansion-port/README.md`, `hardware/target-addon/BUILD.md`, `BUILD.pt.md` and `README.md`, `hardware/README.md`, `docs/manuals/README.md`, `tools/check_wiring.py`, `tools/README.md`): the port board behind a female M12 A-coded socket (TCA4307 hot-swap buffer, a USBLC6-2SC6-class TVS array, 100 ohm series resistors, 2.2 kohm port-side pull-ups, a 50 mA PTC and a BAT85-class back-feed diode), each part with what it protects against, the pin table, the addresses, assembly, checks, the RFC's port bench tests and the safety rules, in English and Portuguese. The target addon guide now builds the target module (an RP2040 board at 0x58, the piezo's front end, a P-channel MOSFET against a reversed cable, a male M12 connector) and marks what waits for increment 8: the module's pins and UF2 file, the box reading the port, Target Windows as a preset. `check_wiring.py` checks the port's M12 pin-out (EXP-1) in both languages and that the keypad's address stays out of 0x58-0x5F. The project page's build page is left to the coordinator (the RFC's row 10). Not built on hardware.

- **The app shows the player's chrono records and asks for the sharing consent per field** (`app/lib/site/chrono_screens.dart`, `site_api.dart`; *My chrono checks and sharing* on the field screen): every field where the account holds a player id, with its replicas, readings, violations and refused readings, from `GET /api/v1/me/chrono`; one switch per field, off until the person turns it on and confirms the consent text (`PUT /api/v1/me/consents/chrono-sharing/{field}`), withdrawn after a confirmation (`DELETE`). A hosted hub's *Chrono sharing between fields* opens the same switch. Player manuals in English and Portuguese; decisions APPS-1 to APPS-8.
- **Club pages in the app** (`app/lib/site/clubs.dart`, `club_screens.dart`; *Clubs* on the field screen, S18-11): the account's clubs, a club's page with the fields where it plays and its members, memberships and invitations (accept, decline, leave), making a club, and its admin's actions (invite by username, change a role, remove, change the club and its public page, delete), through `api/clubs.py`. Logos, crests and moderation stay on the staff pages. Decisions APPS-9 to APPS-16.

- **Players register on the field's web pages, without the app** (`server/atalaia_server/web/player_pages.py`, `player_i18n.py`, `static/player/register.js`; decisions WREG-1 to WREG-12): the game's *Register* page offers **Register here**, `/f/<slug>/games/<id>/register/web`, next to the app's way. A player logs in with Google, Microsoft or Apple, a passkey, or the password when the server takes them; a first social login makes the account under the app's rules (the privacy notice opened and ticked, 16 or older, the notice's version); then the app's form, the same API, checks and records, and the check-in ticket as a QR code and text, shown once and printable. Back on the page later: see, change or cancel, a new ticket, the way into the app, and a passkey for next time. The page holds no data, runs no inline script (the CSP is unchanged) and no cookie authorises a change. A social login may now return to that page, and registrations and new tickets are limited to 20 an hour per account (app and web alike). Four languages; server and Chromium tests; the player and organiser manuals in English and Portuguese; the privacy notice templates gain a browser paragraph and two points for the lawyer (WREG-11).

- **The mode editor's blocks for outputs (increment 4's leftover of the draft RFC "The expansion port")** (decisions EXPL-1 to EXPL-4; `simulator/atalaia_sim/editor/blocks.js`, `generator.js`, its four languages, `examples/master_satellites.json`, `simulator/editor_tests/test_editor.py`): *Box hardware* gets "switch output … pulse/on/off for … ms", which writes `cap("siren"):pulse(ms)`, `:on()`, `:on(ms)` or `:off()`, and "flash output …", which writes `:pattern(on_ms, off_ms, ms)`; *Field* gets the same for one box from the controller, `field.send(node, "output", { capability, act, ms[, on_ms, off_ms] })` (CMD 12 with `src: field`; `*` only with off). The editor points at a block with a missing time, `*` switched on, or a function that no role lists in its `outputs:` on the Mode tab (the list the console and the start check read). The Master and Satellites example's siren now uses the field block, so the round trip runs it against the hand-written mode's siren tests. Texts in pt, en, de and zh-TW.
- **A scenario's outputs in the game form and the start check (increment 5's leftover)** (decisions EXPL-5 to EXPL-8; `core/scenario/package.lua`, `controller/atalaia_controller/outputs.py`, `engine.py`, `modes.py`, `scenario_service.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_outputs.dart`, `controller_engine.dart`, `controller_modes.dart`, `controller_scenarios.dart`, `controller/console/src/components/game-control.js`, `protocol/vectors/scenario_compile.json`, a new contract case "EXPL" in `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`): the scenario compiler writes the output functions a scenario's `triggers:` switch on each box into the compiled `mode.yaml` (`scenario.outputs`, only when there are triggers, `*` left out), `GET /api/v1/modes` gives them as the scenario's `outputs` on both controllers, the console's game form names them box by box (*Foxhound would switch these outputs on a box that has them: siren, strobe (master); horn (gate)*), and the start check treats them as the role's `outputs:` of the box the GM gave that name in the Join tab, with `output` optional: the same `outputs` and `optional_caps` warnings as for a mode's role, on the Pi and on the phone. Scenarios compiled before are unchanged until compiled again.
- **The remote GM's All outputs off (increment 3's leftover)** (decisions EXPL-9 to EXPL-11; `server/atalaia_server/web/static/staff/remote.js`, `staff_i18n.py`, `server/tests/test_remote_gm.py`, `server/tests_browser/test_staff_browser.py`): the site server's **Remote GM** page gets **All outputs off**, the one output command a remote GM has (`outputs_off`, `protocol/sync.md`, EXP-27): no parameters, no confirmation (as the console's own button), in any state of the live game until it has ended; like the page and the command API, only the field's GM has it. Texts in pt, en, de and zh-TW. The "outputs disabled on this box" note needed a protocol change (EXPL-12): see the next entry.
- **Outputs fitted, not enabled by the box's admin: the console says so (increment 3's last leftover)** (decisions EXPL-12, answered by the maintainer on 2026-11-04, and EXPL-13 to EXPL-22; draft RFC 0000 "Phase 3 events, tags and pods" Amendment 6; `protocol/schema/events.yaml`, `protocol/expansion-port.md` section 7, `protocol/vectors/events.yaml`, `controller_api.json`; `firmware/esp32/main/outputs.c`, `outputs.h`, `box_game.c`, `test/test_outputs.c`, `test/test_box_game.c`, `test/box_host/box_host.c`; `controller/atalaia_controller/outputs.py`, `engine.py`, `controller/tests/test_outputs.py`, `test_box_e2e.py`; `app/lib/controller/controller_outputs.dart`, `controller_engine.dart`, `app/lib/player/player_screens.dart`; `controller/console/src/components/node-list.js`, `box-invites.js`, `ui.js`, the console's four languages; the GM and Field Box manuals, en and pt): a box left an output its admin had not enabled out of both its HELLO and its `node.modules`, so a siren fitted but not enabled looked like no siren. A new standard event, **`node.outputs_disabled`** (60, normal, data `modules`), sent with each `node.modules` by a box with an output fitted, names the outputs not enabled in the same compact text (`g45:servo:horn`; the empty text when every one is). It is its own event rather than a key of `node.modules` because the decoders refuse an unknown data key: a 0.7.0 controller refuses event 60 alone and keeps the box's `node.modules` whole. Both controllers give it in `GET /api/v1/nodes` as `outputs_disabled` (null until the box says) and the start check adds `outputs_disabled`, a note and never a refusal or a warning, for a box whose role's `outputs:` (or its scenario's triggers) would switch one; a GM's output request for such an output is refused as before, saying it is not enabled. The console shows *horn · fitted, not enabled by the box's admin* under the box, the note in the start panel (*enable it at the box with the admin PIN, or play without it*) and the event in the feed, in pt, en, de and zh-TW; the phone's player feed hides it. `box_host --simple-out FUNCTION[@GPIO],disabled` fits an output its admin has not enabled. Field Box image 2,175,680 bytes, 0 warnings. Tests: the firmware host tests (the text, its limits, a field game with one output enabled and one not, a box with none), the event vectors on the C, Python and Dart codecs, the controllers' unit tests and a new contract case on both, an end-to-end test with the C host box, and a console browser test in four languages. Not tried on hardware. Merged after increment 8 (decisions EXPL-20 to EXPL-22): a module's output channels left disabled (EXPI8-7) are in `node.outputs_disabled` too, as the module's entry with only those ids (`5c:out4/1.0.0:siren.2,smoke`), whole entries within 100 bytes, told with each `node.modules` and again if the text changes while `node.modules` does not.

- **A phone prop's withheld outputs show in the console as a box's outputs fitted but not enabled** (decisions PROPC-6 to PROPC-12, closing EXPI9-29; `firmware/esp32/main/pod/pod_port.c`, `pod_port.h`, `outputs.c`, `outputs.h`, `expansion_box.c`, `expansion_box.h`, `firmware/esp32/test/test_pod.c`; `app/lib/prop/prop_session.dart`; `protocol/vectors/capability_proxy.yaml` (`pod_modules`, new `pod_modules_withheld`), `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`; `protocol/capability-proxy.md`, `prop-link.md`, `expansion-port.md`, draft RFC 0000 Amendment 7, the I/O pod guide and the GM manual (en, pt)): an I/O pod with an output fitted adds `withheld` to its EVT `pod.modules`, the outputs it has but does not lend (left out of IDENT for want of room, or a module's channels on a pod built without `--module-outputs`) in `node.outputs_disabled`'s text (`g33:servo:siren`, `5c:out4/1.0.0:strobe,out.4`), and sends it again whenever it changes; the phone sends it as `node.outputs_disabled` just before each `node.modules` and when it changes, the empty text when every output is lent, as a box does. The console's *fitted, not enabled* chip and the start check's note now cover a prop; no IDENT and no controller change (a contract case on both). An older pod sends no `withheld` and the phone then says nothing. Not tried on hardware.

- **A phone prop's capability types in the start check** (decisions PROPC-1 to PROPC-6, closing EXPI9-12's remaining note; `app/lib/prop/capability_mapper.dart`, `prop_session.dart`, `prop_game.dart`, `app/lib/controller/dual_link.dart`; `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`; `protocol/prop-link.md`, the GM manual (en, pt)): a phone prop's HELLO now lists the capability type ids of what it gives a mode, the phone's screen (team buttons, keypad, display, team light), speaker, NFC and GPS, and what its I/O pod lends in IDENT (its buttons, keypad, ring, buzzer and NFC, `hit` while it lends a target, `output` while it lends an output), so both controllers' start check takes a prop as a box: a required type it lacks blocks Start (unless forced), an optional one or a role's `outputs:` warns. The prop sends HELLO again whenever the list changes (its pod's IDENT, the pod's link breaking, the person unlinking it), so a prop that loses its pod before the game is checked without it. No controller code changed; two contract cases (`PROPC`) on both controllers, Dart tests against a simulated pod and the host pod. A pod's outputs withheld for lack of IDENT room show as a box's disabled outputs since PROPC-6 (below). Not tried on hardware.
- **The I/O pod no longer turns its outputs off as the phone speaks** (decision ROB-5; `firmware/esp32/main/pod/pod_outputs.c`, `firmware/esp32/test/test_pod.c`, `app/test/prop/prop_pod_outputs_test.dart`): the outputs' lease (every output off after 1.5 s without a frame from the phone) measured the silence as an unsigned difference, so a frame handled after the pod had read its clock counted as 49 days of silence and turned every output off at once. The host pod does it that way, and the app's radio test of a phone prop's siren failed under load for it (and for waiting on the pod's off rather than the phone's own); the ESP32 pod task reads its clock after its frames, so the board was not seen to do it. The silence is now a signed difference; a host test covers it.
- **The I/O pod's automatic reconnect pairs again once, then asks for the pod to be linked again** (decisions ROB-1 to ROB-4, following PODPK-4 and PODPK-8; `app/lib/pod/pod_reconnect.dart`, `app/lib/prop/prop_home.dart`, `prop_session.dart`, `app/lib/l10n/strings_*.dart`): a Bluetooth pod that refuses the phone's bond when the app opens the link again by itself (HCI 0x05: a bond made without a passkey, or one the pod has cleared) now gets the phone's bond removed and one more try at once, so Android asks for the new passkey on the pod's screen, as when the pod is linked by hand. A wrong passkey, a cancelled or too slow pairing, a second refusal or an Android that will not remove the bond stop the tries, so the pairing dialog is not asked for again and again; the prop's screen says "The app could not link the pod again by itself" and why, in pt, en, de and zh-TW. A pod out of reach is still tried with the growing wait, and USB is unchanged. The game screen says it too (the entry below). Dart tests with a fake Bluetooth plugin; not tried with a real pod.
- **The phone prop's game screen says when its I/O pod pairs again, and when it has to be linked again** (ROB-4's follow-up; decisions ROB-7 to ROB-10; `app/lib/prop/prop_game.dart`, `app/test/prop/prop_pod_reconnect_test.dart`, `app/test/goldens/screens_goldens_test.dart`, `hardware/io-pod/BUILD.md` and `BUILD.pt.md`): during a game, while a Bluetooth pod that refused the phone's bond pairs anew by itself, an amber banner says "This pod needs pairing again ... type the new code from the pod's screen" in place of "Reconnecting (try n)"; when the tries stop, a red banner beside "A different pod answered" says "The app could not link the pod again by itself." and why (a wrong or cancelled code, too slow, or forget the pod in the Bluetooth settings). The first goes once the pod is linked again, the second when the admin unlinks the pod or the game is left. The texts the home screen already had, in pt, en, de and zh-TW; nothing new to translate. Widget tests and a new golden image per language (`prop_game_pod_relink_*.png`); not tried with a real pod.
- **The phone prop's admin links the I/O pod again during a game** (decision ROB-9, the maintainer's yes of 2026-11-04; decisions ROB-11 to ROB-17; `app/lib/pod/pod_reconnect.dart`, `app/lib/prop/prop_session.dart`, `prop_game.dart`, the four `app/lib/l10n/strings_*.dart`, `app/test/pod/pod_reconnect_pairing_test.dart`, `app/test/prop/prop_pod_reconnect_test.dart`, `hardware/io-pod/BUILD.md` and `BUILD.pt.md`, `docs/manuals/en/field-box.md` and `pt/field-box.md`): once the pod's automatic tries stop or a different pod answered, the game's admin menu (behind the PIN) offers **Link the pod again**. It starts the reconnect again at once for the same pod: Android's pairing dialog comes back, a pod that refuses the bond is paired again once more by itself, and a pod with other capabilities is still refused. Once linked, the banners go and the game goes on (not from the start), the pod's capabilities and radio taken again as on a reconnect; if it fails, the red banner comes back with the new reason. The "A different pod answered" banner now names the pod the game expects (its name, Bluetooth address, firmware and number of capabilities). pt, en, de and zh-TW; widget tests with the fake Bluetooth plugin; not tried with a real pod.
- **The fields of a hosted hub can opt in to share their game history** (decisions HUBH-1 to HUBH-16; the maintainer's decision of 2026-11-04, "fields can choose to share their games or not, should be an opt-in", answering S20-8; `server/atalaia_server/history_share.py`, `api/hub_history.py`, `hosted.py`, migration `0031_hub_history`, the staff page `/staff/fields/<field>/hub-history`): a per-field switch, off by default, that only the field's admin turns on. While it is on, the field's ended games are shared with the staff of the hub's other fields that share theirs (a field that does not share sees nothing: reciprocity), as game-level results only: mode, start and end, each team's final score, the winner and the field's name; never a player id, an event, a member list, a squad, or a game's or team's name. Turning it off withdraws every game the field shared, at once; a shared game never outlives the field's own results (365 days). The copies live in two hub tables under the same row-level security as the chrono sharing, read only through two `SECURITY DEFINER` functions; a field that never opted in has nothing there. The hub's operator lists what is shared with `python -m atalaia_server hosted shared-history` (audited). A single-field server is unchanged. A hub needs `hosted migrate` before the new server. The privacy notice templates (2.7) gain a paragraph marked for the lawyer. In four languages (German and Traditional Chinese AI-drafted).

- **Firmware and controllers trust only the root and the next CI release keys; the old CI key `32b3c815ac30ad43` is retired** (step 3 of the CI key rotation, decision KRT-0; `firmware/esp32/main/Kconfig.projbuild`, `tools/docker_fw.py`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `controller/atalaia_controller/config.py`, `controller/controller.example.toml`, `controller/tests/test_firmware_updates.py`, `app/lib/protocol/firmware_release.dart`, `app/test/controller/boxes_screen_test.dart`, `tools/release_package.py`, `firmware/esp32/docs/RELEASE_SIGNING.md`, `docs/community/keys.md`, `docs/manuals/{en,pt}/firmware.md`, `firmware/esp32/README.md`, `protocol/firmware-update.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`): `CONFIG_ATALAIA_OTA_RELEASE_KEYS`, `docker_fw.py --release-keys`, the Pi's and the phone's default `[firmware] release_keys` and the example `controller.toml` list root `424d053af5cdbc35` and the CI key `a4f6002b0122b131` only. **Boxes on v0.7.0 take this release over the air**: it is signed with the next CI key, which v0.7.0 trusts. **Boxes still on v0.6.1 or older must install v0.7.0 first** (over the air or by USB), since they trust only root and the old CI key. Boxes on this release refuse what the old key signed. A phone that stored exactly the v0.7.0 three-key default takes the new two-key default when it reads its settings (as it did with the two-key default before); an edited list is kept. The Pi stores no default: a `controller.toml` without `release_keys` takes the new default at its next start, but **a `controller.toml` that lists `release_keys` by hand (the example did) should drop `rIO8ogMU3LRLLtnt6Rys+URdKYfmNv5mxvuRJQsX9Tw=`**, and so should a site server's `ATALAIA_FIRMWARE_KEYS` that lists it; nothing removes it for you. `release.yml` drops the `current` branch, `CI_KEY_ID` and the old secrets' names: it always signs with the `release` environment's `ATALAIA_RELEASE_CI_KEY_NEXT` secrets and checks the key id `a4f6002b0122b131` before signing; the variable `ATALAIA_RELEASE_SIGNING_KEY` unset or `next` both mean that key, any other value (`current` included) fails the release, and the signature is still verified against the full trusted list. The old repository secrets are no longer used by anything and are deleted once this release is checked. A bench build with the bench release key now carries 3 of the 4 release keys a box takes.
- **CI on a pull request runs only the jobs whose parts changed** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, "What CI runs"): a first job matches the pull request's files against one filter per job (dorny/paths-filter v4.0.3, pinned by commit), each read from what the job builds, tests and copies in; a job left out shows as skipped. `ci.yml`, `core/` and `protocol/`'s folders are in every filter; the protocol, formatting, REUSE and DCO checks always run; pushes to `main`, release-please's pull requests and a failed filter run everything. The emulator still boots cold (`-no-snapshot`): a cached quick-boot snapshot, tried in #56, came up before Android's input service and failed every pull request's emulator run, so it was taken out.
- **The drawn field image comes fully into view** (`controller/console/src/field-map.js`, *Load the field from a map*): MF-59 scrolls the preview to the top of the window once it is drawn, but it scrolled before the image had loaded; with the image still taking no room the page was too short, so the scroll stopped early and left the image below the fold (at 1280 × 900 the heading stayed near the bottom of the window). The page now waits for the image to be decoded before it scrolls and focuses the heading. The browser test that put a grid over the preview also clicked while that scroll was still moving and failed under load (9 of 30 runs); it now waits for the preview to be in place first.
- **Orthophotos from WMTS tiles, and Austria's basemap.at** (`controller/console/src/components/field-render.js`, `ortho-sources.js`, *Load the field from a map*; decisions MF-94 to MF-99): a source with only a WMTS can now be the drawn image's base. The console fetches its Web Mercator tiles over the area (at the zoom nearest the image's scale, at most 256 tiles, coarser for a large area), stitches them and crops them to the image row by row, so it lines up as a WMS picture does. A tile with no picture stays white and the GM is told; a tile that does not arrive drops the photo (OpenStreetMap alone, said). The first such source is **basemap.at** in Austria (CC BY 4.0, "Datenquelle: basemap.at"), for both the map's *Ortofoto* layer and the image; its origin is added to the field-map page's Content-Security-Policy on the Pi and the phone controller. Baden-Württemberg's WMTS was left for its licence to be checked (MF-80, MF-98; added in the next entry).
- **Baden-Württemberg's orthophotos from LGL's WMTS** (`controller/console/src/components/ortho-sources.js`, `field-render.js`, `ortho-coverage.js` and `tools/ortho_coverage.py`, *Load the field from a map*; decisions MF-100 to MF-105): its licence checked on 2026-11-04 (dl-de/by-2-0, as the service's capabilities, its metadata and LGL's open-data page say: any use, commercial included, changes allowed, with the credit), Baden-Württemberg now has LGL's DOP 20 cm for the map's *Ortofoto* layer and the drawn image, stitched from its GoogleMapsCompatible WMTS (CORS `*`) as basemap.at's, with the credit "LGL-BW (<year>) dl-de/by-2-0, www.lgl-bw.de" ("Daten geändert" on the image) linked to the licence. Tiles outside the service's box are not asked (it answers 400 there); they stay white as a missing tile does. Its origin `https://owsproxy.lgl-bw.de` is added to the field-map page's Content-Security-Policy on the Pi and the phone controller. Twelve German Länder now have an orthophoto.

- **The simulator's web view draws each box's screen from its template** (`simulator/atalaia_sim/screens.py` `render_screen`, `web/app.js`, decisions SIMS-1 to SIMS-8): a node's card showed only the screen's name and data; it now draws the mode's `assets/screens/<name>.yaml` at the Field Box's 320x240, laid out with the numbers of the box's LVGL renderer (a centred column 4 px apart, rows 6 px apart, `fill`, the 14/20/28/48 fonts, a 200x12 bar), with every text, `visible:`, bar value and colour worked out by the core's own parser and evaluator through ctypes, and the data a box adds (`lang`, and `held`/`age`/`live` from `since`). *Box language* in the top bar shows the templates in English, Portuguese, German or Chinese. Screens with no template (`code_entry`, a built-in `final_score`) still show as their name and data; the outputs of increment 4 are drawn beside the screen as before. New tests draw every built-in mode's screens with the data its own tests send, in every language, without a failed expression (`test_screen_render.py`), and check the page in Chromium (`editor_tests/test_web_view.py`).

- **"Not built" lines the code has since built are corrected** (decisions SD-4 to SD-16 in `docs/review/decisions-to-review.md`; `docs/phase-1-single-box/SRE.md`, `docs/phase-2-field-network/STATUS.md`, `docs/phase-3-android-players/{ADR,PLAN,STATUS}.md`, `docs/phase-4-site-server/{PLAN,STATUS}.md`, `docs/phase-5-community/ADR.md`, `modes/README.md`, `site/README.md`, `site/i18n/*.yaml`): each line was checked against the code, and what is built is struck through or annotated with its date and commit, never deleted; among them the GM console's remote GM log (built 2026-10-26), the I/O pod's settings over the radio (PF15, built 2026-10-28), the editor's *Test in the simulator* (2026-10-22), phase 1's SRE defects B-1 to B-4 (fixed in the firmware's first commit) and hosted mode now waiting for its go-live checklist rather than the legal review. The project page in four languages no longer calls the site server, the phone controller and sending a box a new mode "still to come".

## [0.7.0](https://github.com/vakaobr/atalaia-project/compare/v0.6.1...v0.7.0) (2026-11-03)
