* the maintainer's review of the open P5F/P5J rows, httpx2 and the release key rotation ([#35](https://github.com/vakaobr/atalaia-project/issues/35)) ([16abd9f](https://github.com/vakaobr/atalaia-project/commit/16abd9f4b4a91165e385c45f0ffcdf4a718648f4))

### Details

- **Scenario triggers switch the boxes' outputs (increment 5 of the draft RFC "The expansion port")** (decisions EXPI5-1 to EXPI5-15, following EXP-25, EXP-29 and EXP-30; `core/scenario/manifest.lua`, `validator.lua`, `luagen.lua`, `runtime.lua`, `controller/atalaia_controller/engine.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_engine.dart`, `controller/console/src/components/scenario-blocks.js`, the console's four languages, `protocol/vectors/scenario.json`, `protocol/vectors/scenario_compile.json`, `tools/scenario_compile_vectors.py`): a scenario manifest's new optional `triggers:` list switches an output of a box when every objective of a list is completed (`completed`), the first of them is (`any_completed`), one fails (`failed`), or the game starts or ends (`game: start`, `game: end`): `output: { node, capability, act, seconds }` with the act `on`, `pulse`, `pattern` (with `on_ms` and `off_ms`, 500-10000) or `off`, the box one of the scenario's `nodes`, the function `siren`, `strobe`, `smoke`, `light`, `horn` (`siren.2`), `out.1`-`out.9` or `*` with `off`, and 1-600 s (10 s at most for `game: end`, the box's end grace). The validator says what is wrong in the style of the other rules (a bare `on` or `off`, which YAML 1.1 reads as a boolean, asks for quotes); the compiler writes `SCENARIO.triggers` into `field.lua` only when there are triggers; the runtime fires each trigger once a game as CMD 12 with `src: field` (the same path as `field.send(node, "output", ...)` and the controller's one start a box every 2 s), tries again every second for 10 s what that limit holds back, drops what waits on a pause and does not sound the start again after a restart mid-game. Both controllers now hold back the outputs a field script switches after its own `game.end` (an objective that wins the game) until the end's CMD has gone, so they come within the boxes' 10 s grace instead of being switched off by the end. The GM console's scenario blocks gain a **Triggers** category ("when" blocks holding an output block) in pt, en, de and zh-TW, with the YAML round trip (what the blocks cannot hold as it is stays in the YAML view). Tests: the shared vectors (C core, Python and the phone's Dart), a scenario game through the Pi's engine (`controller/tests/test_scenario.py`), the phone's end-of-game hold (`app/test/controller/controller_engine_test.dart`) and the blocks in Chromium. Not tried on hardware.
- **The GM console forgets a saved code the controller now refuses** (`controller/console/src/app.js`): reopened with a code kept in the browser that has since been revoked or has expired, the console said "Wrong access code" and kept the code, sending it again at every start. It now forgets it and says the code is no longer accepted, as when a live stream is closed for it; a code just typed and refused still says it is wrong, and a controller that does not answer keeps the code. Service worker cache v19.
- **CI's app job runs the host box again** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `tools/docker_app.py`): since the host box's HTTPS mode fetch (#32) `box_host` also links libmbedtls and libmbedx509, but the app job linked only mbedcrypto statically, so in the Flutter image (which has no mbedtls) the host box did not start and every phone-controller test that uses it timed out; main's CI app job had failed on every commit since. All three are now linked statically.
- **The live demo starts again** (`server/preview/demo/Dockerfile`): since `box_host` fetches modes over HTTPS (#32) it links libmbedtls and libmbedx509, which the demo image's runtime stage did not install, so its simulated boxes did not start and the demo restarted in a loop. The runtime stage now installs `libmbedtls14t64` and `libmbedx509-1t64`.
- **The live demo shows the players' positions again** (`server/preview/demo/field/director.py`): since position sharing became each player's own opt-in at check-in (LEG-1 to LEG-19, `share_position`, off by default), the controller refused every position the demo's made-up players sent (`409 position_sharing_off`), so the player's view showed the boxes and events but no players. The demo's players now opt in when they check in.
- **Bomb's smoke and siren, the master's siren, and a role's `outputs:` (increment 4 of the draft RFC "The expansion port")** (decisions EXPI4-1 to EXPI4-12, following EXP-19, EXP-22 and EXP-29; `modes/bomb/`, `modes/master_satellites/`, `modes/README.md`, `simulator/atalaia_sim/box_outputs.py`, `sim.py`, `testing.py`, `mode.py`, `live.py`, `web/`, `controller/atalaia_controller/outputs.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_modes.dart`, `controller_outputs.dart`, `app/lib/modes/builtin_modes.g.dart`, `app/test/support/box_outputs.dart`, `mode_runner.dart`, `controller/console/src/components/game-control.js`, `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`, the GM and Field Box manuals in en and pt): a role may name the output functions its mode switches (`outputs: [smoke, siren]`, with `output` in its `requires:` or `optional:`), which the simulator's, the Pi's and the phone's validators check; the start check on both controllers notes a box with outputs but none of those (`outputs`), and the console's game form says which outputs a mode would switch, in four languages. *Bomb / Rush* (0.4.0) gets optional **Smoke when it explodes** and **Siren when it explodes** (`smoke_s`, `siren_s`, up to 10 s: the explosion ends the game and a box allows outputs for 10 s after the end) and *Master and Satellites* (0.2.0) an optional **Siren on the master when one team holds every satellite** (`siren_all_s`, from `field.lua` through CMD 12). Left empty they are off, so existing games, presets and joins are unchanged, and a box without the output plays the same. The simulator and the app's Dart mode runner model a box's outputs (the output layer's rules, `output.switched`, a field script's CMD 12), mode tests take `outputs:` and `expect_output` (9 new tests), and the simulator's web view draws each box's outputs. The mode editor holds both too (`simulator/atalaia_sim/editor/`: a role's *outputs it switches* and *it needs one*, a setting that *may be left empty*, in four languages), and its Master and Satellites example makes the new manifest and passes the new tests. Not tried on hardware.
- **The controllers and the console switch the boxes' outputs (increment 3 of the draft RFC "The expansion port")** (decisions EXPI3-1 to EXPI3-14, following EXP-21, EXP-23, EXP-27, EXP-30 and EXP-36; `controller/atalaia_controller/outputs.py`, `engine.py`, `api.py`, `remote_gm.py`, `sync_format.py`, `simulator/atalaia_sim/field_controller.py`, `protocol/schema/capabilities.yaml`, `tools/protogen.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_outputs.dart`, `controller_engine.dart`, `controller_api.dart`, `controller_remote_gm.dart`, `controller_sync_format.dart`, `field_controller.dart`, `app/lib/player/player_screens.dart`, `controller/console/src/components/outputs-panel.js`, `node-list.js`, `box-invites.js`, `game-control.js`, `ui.js`, the console's four languages, `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`, `protocol/vectors/sync.json`, `protocol/sync.md`): both controllers keep each box's last `node.modules` and give it in `GET /api/v1/nodes` as `modules` (a simple output with `"feedback": false`, a module `node.module_lost` names marked lost); the start check refuses a joined box that reports its capabilities and lacks one its role requires (`missing_caps`, `force` overrides; `team_input` and `team_display` met by the types now listed under `role_names` in `capabilities.yaml`), warns about optional ones and names each simple output "not checked"; the GM switches an output with `POST /api/v1/games/{id}/nodes/{node}/output` (on, pulse or pattern only while the game runs, 5 s by default; off always) and turns every output off with `POST /api/v1/games/{id}/outputs/off`, and a field script with `field.send(node, "output", {...})`, all as CMD 12 with the controller's own `src` and at most one start a box every 2 s (429), off never held back; GM token only; the remote GM gets `outputs_off` and nothing that turns an output on. The console shows a chip per module under each box, the start check's new messages, an **Outputs** panel (seconds, *On for N s* after a confirmation, *Off*, **All outputs off**) and the three events in words, in pt, en, de and zh-TW; the app's player feed hides them. A new contract case runs on both controllers; an end-to-end test drives `box_host --simple-out` through the GM's output API. The "outputs disabled on this box" note is left for the field the RFC does not put in this increment.
- **The simple output and the box's output layer (increment 2 of the draft RFC "The expansion port")** (decisions EXPI2-1 to EXPI2-16, following EXP-27 to EXP-37, whose open questions Q1-Q21 the maintainer approved on 2026-11-03; `firmware/esp32/main/outputs.c`, `outputs.h`, `outputs_admin.c`, `outputs_admin.h`, `simple_out.c`, `simple_out.h`, `simple_out_esp.c`, `simple_out_esp.h`, `command_dispatch.c`, `node_runner.c`, `box_game.c`, `radio/field_link.c`, `preset_ui.c`, `box_text.c`, `screen_renderer.c`, `oled_view.c`, `screen_json.c`, `shell.c`, `Kconfig.projbuild`, `CMakeLists.txt`; `firmware/esp32/test/test_outputs.c`, `test_simple_out.c`, `test_box_game.c`, `box_host/box_host.c`; `controller/tests/test_box_e2e.py`; `tools/check_wiring.py`, `tools/docker_fw.py`; `hardware/field-box/BUILD.md` and `BUILD.pt.md` section 9.10, `hardware/satellite/BUILD.md` and `BUILD.pt.md`, `hardware/io-pod/BUILD.md` and `BUILD.pt.md`, the hardware READMEs, `firmware/esp32/README.md`, `firmware/esp32/docs/BRING_UP.md` step 8b, `docs/manuals/{en,pt}/field-box.md` and `firmware.md`). A box can now switch a siren, a strobe, a light or a smoke machine's remote through an off-the-shelf **RC relay switch**: new Kconfig option `CONFIG_ATALAIA_SIMPLE_OUT` (off by default) sends an RC-servo signal (50 Hz, 2.0 ms on, 1.0 ms off) on GPIO 46 (V4.3) or 5 (V4.2), following the board revision, through the 74AHCT125's spare gate to an M8 socket; `CONFIG_ATALAIA_SIMPLE_OUT2` adds a second one on GPIO 45, offered only without the piezo buzzer, and the build refuses a pin another part, the other revision's front-end line or the buzzer has. The pulses come from the RMT peripheral in bursts that the game task renews every 200 ms with never more than 0.5 s queued, so a hung, reset or unpowered box stops them within 0.5 s and the switch opens on the lost signal (not LEDC, which keeps pulsing after a hang). A new platform-free **output layer** holds each output's settings and rules, shared with the I2C output module of a later increment: outputs stay **disabled until the field's admin enables them at the box** (the picker's new **Outputs** entry behind the admin PIN, in en, pt, de and zh-tw, or `@outputs` / `@output <n> ...` on the serial console with the admin PIN), with a function (`siren`, `strobe`, `smoke`, `light`, `horn`, else `out.<n>`) and a max on (1-600 s); outputs switch only while a game runs, go off at boot, pause, script halt and game end (with a 10 s grace for an end-of-game horn from the mode's `game.end` handler or the field), change state at most every 500 ms (patterns at least 500 ms on and off), and a script starts one at most twice a second. A mode switches one with `cap("siren"):pulse(ms)`, `:on()`, `:off()`, `:pattern(on_ms, off_ms, ms)` (a call to an output the box lacks does nothing); the field with **CMD opcode 12**, handled by the firmware whatever the mode, its params checked against the shared vectors. Each start and each early off sends `output.switched`; HELLO's capabilities now list what the box has (button, keypad, display, led, audio, nfc, battery, and `output` while one is enabled), and once the HELLO is acknowledged the box sends `node.modules` (`g46:servo:siren`). `box_host --simple-out siren[@GPIO]` runs the same code and prints the pulse train for the host and end-to-end tests; `tools/docker_fw.py` gains `--simple-out=on|on2|<GPIO>` and `--buzzer=on`; `tools/check_wiring.py` allows GPIO 46 for this pin on a V4.3 build and 5 on a V4.2 build only, and 45 for a second output without the buzzer. The build guides' new section (wiring, the switch that must open on signal loss, the M8 socket, the 10 kohm and 330 ohm, power from the load's side, low-voltage loads only, the switch's bench check), BRING_UP's bench tests 11-13 (board 5 on GPIO 15, `@hang` in bench builds) and the operator manuals' **Outputs** section (en, pt). Field Box image: +11,440 bytes with the option off (2,174,736) and +14,192 with it on (2,177,488); static RAM left for the heap 192,005 (off) and 191,821 (on), against 192,757; 0 warnings in every build. Tests: `test_outputs.c` (settings, limits, game-state rules, the grace, the rate limit, CMD 12 against `cmd.json`'s `output_params`, the admin screen and console, a mode's calls through the node runner and the core), `test_simple_out.c` (the burst scheduler against a fake RMT, the pins), a field game in `test_box_game.c` (HELLO, `node.modules`, CMD 12, pause, end), and an end-to-end test with the real controller and the C host box. Not in this increment: the controllers sending CMD 12 and their output API, the console's outputs panel, the modes using outputs, scenario triggers, the I2C port and the pod's outputs. **Not tried on hardware** (BRING_UP.md step 8b).
- **The expansion port's protocol, its vectors and the capability type ids (increment 1 of the draft RFC "The expansion port")** (decisions EXPI1-1 to EXPI1-12, following EXP-7 to EXP-14, EXP-18 to EXP-23 and EXP-36, whose open questions Q1-Q16 the maintainer answered with the recommendations on 2026-11-03; `protocol/schema/capabilities.yaml`, `tools/protogen.py`, `protocol/expansion-port.md`, `tools/expansion_vectors.py`, `protocol/vectors/expansion.json`, `simulator/atalaia_sim/expansion.py`, `protocol/schema/events.yaml`, `protocol/vectors/cmd.json`, `capability_proxy.yaml`, `hello.json`, `events.yaml`, RFC 0001 Amendment 26, draft RFC 0000 Amendment 5, `docs/design-spec.md` sections 6, 7 and 17): HELLO's key 6 has always been "capability type ids" with no table; the table now exists, in one file generated into the C, Python and Dart tables (`button` 1 to `tamper` 13, `hit` 10, `output` 11, `contact` 14 reserved; a receiver ignores an id it does not know). The expansion port's register protocol v1 is specified: modules at 0x58-0x5F on the box's I2C bus, `IDENT`, `CHANNEL`, `STATUS` and `COMMAND` with a CRC-8 that covers a write's address byte, what a box refuses and why, and the **output lease** (a module turns its outputs off 1.5 s after the box stops refreshing them), with shared vectors for the CRC, every frame good and refused, seven lease timelines (the lease running out, a lost write repaired, a module restart, a pulse across missed polls, max on and min off, the relay's 100 ms floor) and `node.modules`' compact text, checked by a Python reference model and by the box's C CRC-8 (every frame). The `output` capability (`on`, `off`, `pulse`, `pattern`) with the simple output's `"feedback": false`, a second hit sensor's `channel`, CMD opcode 12 `output` (with the params a box takes and refuses) and the events `node.modules` (57), `node.module_lost` (58) and `output.switched` (59) are in the schema and the C, Python and Dart codecs' vectors. Specified, not built: nothing sends or handles them yet (the simple output and the box's output layer, the controllers and the console, the port driver and the modules are the RFC's later increments; the simple output's signal, pins and socket wait for its questions 17-21). Tests: the protocol checks (`protogen --check`, `expansion_vectors.py --check`, in CI's protocol job and `tools/check.sh`), the core's table lookups, the firmware host tests, the simulator's `test_expansion.py`, and the app's codec and capability table tests.
- **A piezo buzzer as an option, and the speaker no longer silent on clips** (decisions BZR-1 to BZR-8; `firmware/esp32/main/sound.c`, `sound.h`, `sound_esp.c`, `sound_esp.h`, `hal/hal_buzzer.h`, `hal/esp/hal_esp_buzzer.c`, `hal/hal.h`, `hal/esp/hal_esp.c`, `hal/stub/hal_stub.c`, `command_dispatch.c`, `shell.c`, `pod/pod_caps.c`, `pod/pod_task.c`, `pod/pod_main.c`, `Kconfig.projbuild`, `CMakeLists.txt`; `firmware/esp32/test/test_sound.c`, `test_command_dispatch.c`; `tools/check_wiring.py`; `hardware/field-box/BUILD.md` and `BUILD.pt.md`, `hardware/satellite/BUILD.md` and `BUILD.pt.md`, `hardware/io-pod/BUILD.md` and `BUILD.pt.md`, the hardware READMEs, `firmware/esp32/README.md`, `firmware/esp32/docs/BRING_UP.md`, `docs/manuals/{en,pt}/firmware.md` and `field-box.md`, `site/src/build.html`, `site/i18n/{pt,en,de,zh-tw}.yaml`). The maintainer reversed P5J-4 on 2026-11-03. New Kconfig option `CONFIG_ATALAIA_BUZZER` (off by default; the Field Box and the I/O pod) drives a **passive** piezo on `ATALAIA_BUZZER_PIN` (GPIO 45, the only header pin no variant uses; a strapping pin that the buzzer's circuit keeps low at reset) with LEDC square-wave tones; the volume setting sets the duty. New platform-free `sound.c` decides where sounds play: `audio.tone(freq, ms)` on every part fitted, together; `audio.play(clip)` plays `/littlefs/audio/<clip>.wav` on the speaker when the box has it, otherwise the clip's tone pattern (the pod's PF7 table, moved here, plus `hit`, `window`, `collected`, `deposited` and `healed`, so every clip a mode plays has one) on every part fitted. Before this change the Field Box gave the clip's bare name to the WAV player, which found no file, so a box with a speaker stayed silent, and `audio.tone` was never routed on the Field Box. `check_wiring.py` allows GPIO 45 for the buzzer pin only; the build guides, pin tables and project page list the part (a 220 ohm resistor for direct drive, at most 15 mA for an instant at each edge, or an NPN stage, about 2.6 mA from the pin), marked **not yet built on hardware**. Field Box image: +1,872 bytes with the option off and +8,352 with it on (2,169,520 bytes); static RAM left for the heap 192,757 (off) and 192,277 (on), against 192,765. Host tests: sound and tone-routing tests in `test_sound.c` with fake outputs, plus a check that every clip in `modes/` has a pattern, and `audio.tone` dispatch tests. **Not tried on hardware** (BRING_UP.md step 8).

- **Firmware trusts a third release key, the next CI key `a4f6002b0122b131`, and the release workflow can switch to it** (the maintainer's decision of 2026-11-03 to rotate the CI release key over the air, decisions KRT-1 to KRT-9; `firmware/esp32/main/Kconfig.projbuild`, `tools/docker_fw.py`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `controller/atalaia_controller/config.py`, `controller/controller.example.toml`, `app/lib/protocol/firmware_release.dart`, `app/lib/storage/app_store.dart`, `tools/release_package.py`, `firmware/esp32/docs/RELEASE_SIGNING.md`, `docs/community/keys.md`, `docs/manuals/{en,pt}/firmware.md`, `firmware/esp32/README.md`, `protocol/firmware-update.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`): the CI key `32b3c815ac30ad43` lives only in repository secrets, which cannot be read back to move them into the protected `release` environment, so it is replaced by a new key whose private half is a secret of that environment only (`ATALAIA_RELEASE_CI_KEY_NEXT`, `_NEXT_PASSPHRASE`; public key `76eY5BoMQ4RHohhX/8iSUlIUA5o5Entfk7EFBUxpbSY=`). This is step 1 of 3: `CONFIG_ATALAIA_OTA_RELEASE_KEYS`, `docker_fw.py --release-keys`, the Pi's and the phone's default `[firmware] release_keys` and the example `controller.toml` list root, CI and next CI, and the next release is still signed with the old CI key, so boxes on v0.6.1 take it over the air and learn the new key. `release.yml` signs with the key the variable `ATALAIA_RELEASE_SIGNING_KEY` chooses: unset or `current` the old secrets, `next` the `_NEXT` secrets; only the chosen secret reaches the step, a missing one fails the release instead of falling back, another value fails it too, the key's id is checked before signing and the signature is verified against all three trusted keys (the list the build checked in the image) as the chosen key's. A phone that stored exactly the earlier two-key default takes the new default when it reads its settings; an edited list is kept. A Pi whose `controller.toml` lists `release_keys` by hand must add the new key before step 2. RELEASE_SIGNING.md, "Rotating the CI key", says what the maintainer does at step 2 (set the variable to `next`, release, then delete the old repository secrets) and step 3 (drop the old key in a later release).
- **Design: one expansion port for inputs and outputs** (draft RFC `docs/rfcs/0000-expansion-port.md`, decisions EXP-1 to EXP-38; nothing built yet): the M12 port of P5J-15 to P5J-21 kept, with a hot-swap I2C buffer and ESD protection on the box; modules with their own processor (an RP2040 board) at 0x58-0x5F answering a small register map (identify, channels, one status read with the oldest input event and the outputs' state, commands), polled every 20 ms; every output on a lease that turns it off within 1.5 s when the box stops refreshing it; loads always on their own supply, mains out of scope; the `output` capability (`cap("siren"):pulse(ms)`), a CMD opcode 12 for field scripts and scenario triggers, the GM's "All outputs off", never players; the target addon as the first module; and, by the maintainer's decision (EXP-31), a **simple output** as the preferred way to switch a load: one free GPIO (46 on a V4.3, 5 on a V4.2, 33 on the I/O pod; GPIO 45 stays the piezo buzzer's) sending an RC-servo signal in RMT bursts through a spare 74AHCT125 gate to an off-the-shelf RC relay switch that opens when the pulses stop; a 4-channel I2C output module stays as a later option; a test plan, twelve implementation increments and twenty-one open questions with recommended answers.
- **The host box fetches mode packages from the real controller over HTTPS** (decisions MFH-1 to MFH-7; `firmware/esp32/main/field_ca_pin.c`, `firmware/esp32/main/fw_update_esp.c`, `firmware/esp32/test/box_host/box_host_https.c`, `firmware/esp32/test/box_host/box_host.c`, `controller/tests/test_box_mode_fetch_e2e.py`): the Field Box's check of the controller's certificate against the join's field CA (key 13) moves out of `fw_update_esp.c` into a platform-free file that builds on the firmware's mbedTLS 3.6 and the host's 2.28; the Field Box attaches it as before. `box_host --mode-source https` runs the firmware's own fetch (`mode_fetch.c`) over mbedTLS sockets with that same check, to the join's address (key 14), and posts its reports to the controller; `--mode-source DIR` (files standing for the controller) is unchanged. A new end-to-end test starts the controller as `run` does (the field CA made on its first start, HTTPS on its TLS port, the C host modem), uploads a package signed with a test registry key, and the C host box fetches, verifies, keeps and reports it, then plays the game; it refuses a join pinning another CA (`unreachable`, said over LoRa), a package signed by a key it does not carry (`bad_signature`) and an archive changed after signing (`bad_hash`). The same fetch on a real box is still to do (HW3).
- **The guides point to v0.6.1, and the release docs say how releases are guarded today** (`docs/manuals/{en,pt}/firmware.md`, `docs/manuals/{en,pt}/modem.md`, `README.md`, `firmware/esp32/docs/RELEASE_SIGNING.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`): the download and checksum examples use the v0.6.1 file names, the first release made through release-please with the signed firmware attached (v0.6.0's firmware build failed, #26, so it has no files). The `release` environment has no required reviewers, by the maintainer's choice while the only developer: merging the release pull request is the human gate, and the signing and publishing run by themselves. Required reviewers are to be turned on when other collaborators join; the steps stay in RELEASE_SIGNING.md.
- **The controller's pinned lock has httpx2 instead of httpx** (decisions HX2-1 to HX2-6, PLK-3; `controller/requirements.in`, `controller/requirements.txt`, `controller/tests/test_api.py`, `controller/tests/test_tls.py`, `controller/tests/test_main.py`, `controller/tests/test_box_mode_fetch_e2e.py`, `controller/tests/test_scenarios.py`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` comment): Starlette 1.x's test client asked for `httpx2` (Pydantic's fork of httpx 0.28) on every run of the `controller-pinned` job. Whoever installs the controller with pip from `controller/requirements.txt` now gets httpx2 2.13.1, httpcore2 2.13.1 and truststore 0.10.5 in place of httpx 0.28.1, httpcore 1.0.9 and certifi (all only for the tests: the controller itself makes no HTTP requests with them); every other pin is unchanged. Ubuntu's packages keep python3-httpx (their Starlette 0.31 knows no httpx2), so the tests that open their own HTTP client take httpx2 where it is installed and httpx otherwise, as Starlette's test client does; a new test checks which one it uses.

## [0.6.1](https://github.com/vakaobr/atalaia-project/compare/v0.6.0...v0.6.1) (2026-11-02)

### Bug Fixes

* **controller:** a scan ending as the region changes or the controller stops no longer writes after it ([#29](https://github.com/vakaobr/atalaia-project/issues/29)) ([4813977](https://github.com/vakaobr/atalaia-project/commit/48139774889f8d70e5a6e8a74aaf6324e9d8e615))
* **controller:** close a dropped stream's socket and wake every live-feed client; test the pinned lock in CI ([#28](https://github.com/vakaobr/atalaia-project/issues/28)) ([6ecf830](https://github.com/vakaobr/atalaia-project/commit/6ecf830b2ffdd1cfea9f86c826e016afb50c4415))
* **release:** the I/O pod's package passes the boot-splash check ([#26](https://github.com/vakaobr/atalaia-project/issues/26)) ([d82d481](https://github.com/vakaobr/atalaia-project/commit/d82d481c61cabcf02d109daa43c72e4de2d4020f))

### Details

- **The field controller's tests run on the hash-pinned lock too, in a new CI job** (decisions PLK-1 to PLK-6; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` job `controller-pinned`, `controller/tests/test_scenario_api.py`, `controller/atalaia_controller/ws_client.py`, `controller/tests/test_remote_gm.py`, `controller/atalaia_controller/ws.py`, `controller/tests/test_api.py`, `controller/README.md`, `controller/requirements.txt` header): the controller installs from Ubuntu's packages (FastAPI 0.101, pydantic 1.10) or with pip from `controller/requirements.txt` (FastAPI 0.143, Starlette 1.8, pydantic 2.14, httpx 0.28; decision D15), but its tests had only ever run on the first. On the lock, 1 of 1132 tests failed: `test_check_reads_surrogate_pairs_and_refuses_half_of_one` let the test client encode a lone surrogate, and httpx 0.28 writes JSON as UTF-8 itself and refuses one before the request leaves, so the controller was never reached. The test now makes the request bodies itself and checks the controller's answer (`bad_manifest`) to half of a surrogate pair sent three ways a client can: as an escape inside the manifest, as JSON's own `\udf09` escape, and as the raw bytes ED BC 89 (not UTF-8; Python's JSON reader takes them as the surrogate on both stacks). No bug specific to the newer stack was found, and it shows no deprecation warning from FastAPI or pydantic; Starlette 1.x asks for `httpx2` in its test client (left: the lock keeps httpx). A `ResourceWarning` seen on both stacks was a real leak, fixed: the remote GM's WebSocket client (`controller/atalaia_controller/ws_client.py`) marked a link that was already down (the site server closed it, a send or a receive failed) as closed, and `close()` then returned without closing the socket, so every reconnect after such a drop left one socket to the garbage collector; it now always closes the socket (still sending the close frame only on a live link), with a test, and the tests' fake site server closes its accepted sockets too. One run on the lock hung in `test_map_features`' stream test, a race in the live feed's hub (`controller/atalaia_controller/ws.py`) on both stacks: it filled every client's queue on the event loop of the latest connection, but FastAPI's TestClient runs each WebSocket on a loop of its own, and an `asyncio.Queue` filled from another loop's thread can leave its reader asleep for good; the hub now fills each queue on its own loop (one loop on a real server, so nothing changes there), with a test that failed before (`HubLoopTests`), and the new job stops after 30 minutes. The new job (ubuntu-24.04, `actions/setup-python` pinned by SHA, Python 3.12, `pip install --require-hashes --no-deps --only-binary :all:`, the core and the host modem, box and pod built as in the `controller` job, read-only permissions) runs the same tests with every `DeprecationWarning` an error.
- **The I/O pod's release package no longer fails the boot-splash check** (`tools/release_package.py`): the check (LOGO-7) wanted exactly one `splash_logo.c` in every build, but the I/O pod has no screen and its build embeds none, so the v0.6.0 release run stopped on the io-pod package and published nothing. A build with `CONFIG_ATALAIA_POD_MODE=y` now passes when it embeds no splash, and is refused if it does.
- **A noise scan that ends as the field's region changes or the controller stops no longer writes after it** (`controller/atalaia_controller/engine.py`): a scan reads "done" a moment before it reports, and in that moment the region could change (the scan no longer counted as running) and drop it without waiting for its thread. Its late report then saved the old region's result as the last scan, sent an EU868 modem the new region's channel, and after `stop()` wrote to the closed database (`scan listener failed ... Cannot operate on a closed database`). Only the current scan's reports count now (a new scan likewise ignores the one before), and `stop()` waits for a dropped scan's thread too.

## [0.6.0](https://github.com/vakaobr/atalaia-project/compare/v0.5.0...v0.6.0) (2026-11-02)

### Features

* **app:** say plainly what position sharing shares; offer indoor beacons as optional ([#23](https://github.com/vakaobr/atalaia-project/issues/23)) ([a7329a4](https://github.com/vakaobr/atalaia-project/commit/a7329a419ba8c1ff22f7f20162b7265a77c1892a))

### Continuous Integration

* release with release-please and check pull request titles as Conventional Commits ([#12](https://github.com/vakaobr/atalaia-project/issues/12)) ([31f216c](https://github.com/vakaobr/atalaia-project/commit/31f216c85d2132c0e0be827d2e7f19e1736bcc7a))

### Details

- **`app/tool/check.sh` can work in a folder of its own** (`ATALAIA_APP_WORK`, default `/tmp/atalaia-app` as before): two app checks run natively on the same Mac staged into the same folder and overwrote each other's sources mid-run (2026-11-02); the merge queue's native app job now gives every run its own folder.
- **The beacon scanner's error codes are the same on Android and iOS for the same cause** (`app/ios/Runner/BeaconScanPlugin.swift`, `app/android/app/src/main/kotlin/org/atalaia/app/BeaconScanPlugin.kt`, `app/lib/platform/beacon_scan.dart`): the sightings stream failed with `unavailable` on iOS when the location permission was refused (at the start, or taken away while a game was sharing), where Android's scan refused by a `SecurityException` says `permission`; and Android's own check before the scan said `unavailable` for a missing permission and for Bluetooth off alike. Both plugins now say `unavailable` when the phone has no Bluetooth LE or cannot range beacons (or, on Android, Bluetooth is off), checked first, then `permission` when a permission is refused; `no_beacons` and `scan_failed` are unchanged. The Dart doc comment of `BeaconScanService.windows` lists the four codes, and its stale "iOS has no plugin" paragraph now names the iOS plugin. The app still stops listening and stays on GPS on any error without reading the code, so nothing changes on screen. The Swift was type-checked against the iOS simulator SDK and Flutter's framework (`swiftc -typecheck`), not run on an iPhone.
- **The app says plainly what it shares, and the indoor beacons are offered as optional** (decisions LSW-1 to LSW-3, approved by the maintainer on 2026-11-02 after the iPhone beacon walk; `app/lib/player/player_screens.dart`, `app/lib/l10n/strings_{pt,en,de,zh_tw}.dart`, `app/test/position/beacon_scanner_test.dart`, `docs/manuals/{en,pt}/player.md` and `game-master.md`, `docs/runbooks/ios-beacons-iphone.md`, `app/docs/ios-on-a-mac.md`, `docs/translations/glossary.md`): turning on *Share my position* on a field with beacons asked about the beacons with *Continue* / *Not now*, and *Not now* left the switch on with the note "Indoor positions are off. Your GPS position is still shared.", which players read as "sharing anyway". The dialog is now **Indoor positions (optional)** (pt *Posições no interior (opcional)*): GPS is shared either way, the beacons only add the floor and the room indoors, with **GPS only** / **Use the beacons** (pt **Só GPS** / **Usar as balizas**). While sharing, a line under the switch always says what is shared: **Sharing by GPS only** or **Sharing by GPS and beacons** (pt *A partilhar só por GPS* / *A partilhar por GPS e balizas*), following the scan itself (an iPhone with the app in the background, a refused permission or Bluetooth off say GPS only). *GPS only* shows no note any more; the notes for a refused permission and a phone without Bluetooth LE keep their reason and drop "GPS is still shared". The same Dart screen on Android and iPhone; German and Traditional Chinese (Taiwan) drafted by an AI model like the rest, awaiting a native-speaking reviewer. Widget tests cover both buttons and the line in pt, en, de and zh-TW at 320 dp with text at 130 %, with no overflow.
- **Docs pass of 2 November** (decisions DOCS-10 to DOCS-13; `docs/review/decisions-to-review.md`, `README.md`, `site/i18n/*.yaml`, `protocol/firmware-update.md`, `firmware/esp32/README.md`, `firmware/esp32/docs/BRING_UP.md`, `firmware/esp32/docs/RELEASE_SIGNING.md`, `controller/README.md`, `docs/phase-2-field-network/STATUS.md`, `docs/phase-4-site-server/STATUS.md`): the decision rows of 1 and 2 November filed (EPW-2b to EPW-4b, IOSK, MACT, TFT-8 to TFT-10, AT, FLU, CLK, RP, DEP, MQF, CKF, MAC); the controller's clock handling and short-lived access tokens on the README and the roadmap in four languages; the docs no longer say an update over the air has not run on a real board (it has, on the bench, since 2026-10-22, and on 2026-11-02 with the bench release key); the controller README's slot size is 4 MB.
- **Privacy notice §9: the iOS Keychain wording is confirmed** (`docs/templates/privacy-notice-{en,pt}.md`): the maintainer confirmed the sentence on the app's secrets on an iPhone (sealed with a key in the iOS Keychain, kept on that phone only and never in a backup) on 2026-11-02, so its confirm-the-wording marker is removed in both languages.
- **Docs: flashing on macOS, and the modem with a controller in Docker on a Mac** (decisions MDM-M1 to MDM-M5; `docs/manuals/{en,pt}/modem.md`, `docs/manuals/{en,pt}/firmware.md`): the modem guide said macOS was not tried. On 2026-11-02, on a Mac mini (Apple silicon, macOS 27), the modem image and box images were written with esptool 4.12.0 from a venv on macOS's own `python3`, and a controller in Docker (Colima) reached the modem through a bridge run natively on the Mac (the repository's `tools/serial_bridge.py`, which the bench now runs; `socket://host.docker.internal:7600`, `--add-host host.docker.internal:host-gateway`) and logged `modem link up`. The guides now give the `/dev/cu.usbmodem...` port (no driver, `cu.` not `tty.`), how to tell boards apart (`ioreg`, `esptool read_mac`), the Mac bridge, one program per port, and that opening the port on macOS can restart the board. A controller run natively on macOS is still not tried.
- **release-please keeps `app/pubspec.yaml` as it is apart from the version** (`release-please-config.json`): listed as a plain path, the `.yaml` file got release-please's YAML updater, which parses and rewrites the whole file: the first release PR (v0.6.0) dropped all its comments, the SPDX header included (`reuse lint` would fail), and re-quoted values. It is now a `generic` extra-file, so only the version on the `x-release-please-version` line changes.
- **GM console: a change made on a block is no longer lost when the GM leaves the block view or presses Install at once; the keyboard test of the blocks no longer fails on a busy machine** (decisions CKF-1 to CKF-5; `controller/console/src/components/scenario-block-view.js` new `flush()`, `scenario-builder.js` `install()`, `controller/console/tests/test_scenario_blocks.py`): Blockly fires a change's events a frame later (a `requestAnimationFrame`) and the block view regenerated the YAML one turn after that, so a block changed and the YAML view (or a language change) opened before that frame lost the change, and Install pressed in that moment sent the old text. The view now hands the builder any change it has not sent yet when it goes away and before Install (and does not install when the blocks have an error). This was one of the two ways `test_keyboard_reaches_and_changes_the_blocks` failed in the merge queue, where the console's tests run next to a dozen other Docker jobs: the other was the test pressing the next key before Blockly had finished the last one: Blockly moves the focus a frame late, and a block it focuses is brought to the front in the next frame by moving its SVG siblings, which takes the focus away from a field focused in between (Blockly 13.3.0's `moveSvgRootToFront`; on a busy machine that frame comes after the next key). The test now lets two frames pass after every key and waits (up to 20 s, by polling: `page.wait_for_function` compiles its string in the page, which the console's CSP refuses) for where the focus and the blocks must be, and for the name in the YAML; the "Show" focus check waits too. New test `test_a_change_just_made_survives_leaving_the_view_and_install` changes a block and leaves the view (then installs) in one JavaScript task: it failed before the fix.
- **Releases by release-please, with pull request titles as Conventional Commits** (decisions RP-1 to RP-12; `.github/workflows/release-please.yml`, `release-please-config.json`, `.release-please-manifest.json`, `.github/workflows/pr-title.yml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `.github/dependabot.yml`, `tools/prepare_release.py`, `controller/tests/test_prepare_release.py`, the `x-release-please-version` markers in `app/pubspec.yaml`, `app/lib/version.dart` and the controller's and the server's `__init__.py`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `firmware/esp32/docs/RELEASE_SIGNING.md`, `tools/README.md`): every change reaches `main` as a squash-merged pull request titled `type(scope): summary`, checked by the new **Pull request title** workflow (amannn/action-semantic-pull-request v6.1.1, pinned by SHA; the repository's scopes; Dependabot's prefixes changed to `ci` and `chore(<scope>)` so its titles comply). On each push to `main`, release-please (googleapis/release-please-action v5.0.0, pinned by SHA; release type `simple`, tags `vX.Y.Z`, feat a minor and breaking changes a minor too while 0.x, every GitHub release a pre-release) keeps the pull request **chore(release): vX.Y.Z** open with the next version, its CHANGELOG section (Features, Bug Fixes, Performance, Reverts, Documentation) and the marked version files; a second job commits on it what a marker cannot do, `tools/prepare_release.py release-pr`: `version.h` with its `dev` emptied (or the version's own pre-release label) and `core/CMakeLists.txt`, the hand-written Unreleased notes moved under the section as "Details" (nothing hand-written is lost; this section becomes 0.6.0's Details), and the check that every version file agrees. Merging it tags and makes the release; the workflow then turns the release back into a draft and starts `release.yml` on the tag with `gh workflow run` (the workflow token's tags start no workflow, a dispatch does: no new secret), which builds, signs after the `release` environment's approval and attaches the same nine files as before, and publishes it; it also opens "chore(core): open X.Y.Z-dev" as `tag-release.yml` did. `release.yml` now uploads into an existing draft (release-please's) or creates the release (a tag pushed by hand), publishes only when every file is up, and still refuses a release that is published already. `prepare-release.yml` and `tag-release.yml` are removed, and with them `prepare_release.py`'s `prepare` and `notes`; releasing is "Releases" in `CONTRIBUTING.md`.
- **The preview runs on the maintainer's Mac mini, and `preview.sh restore` brings a backup back without Caddy and with a live demo that syncs** (`server/preview/preview.sh`, `server/restore.sh`, `server/docker-compose.preview.yml`, `tools/server_preview_check.py`, `docs/runbooks/preview-server.md`): the test site and the live demo moved on 2026-11-02 from WSL on the Windows PC to the Mac mini (Docker in Colima, cloudflared a macOS LaunchDaemon on the same remotely managed tunnel). The move showed two traps in restoring a backup into the preview, both fixed in code. restore.sh used `docker-compose.yml` alone, so it brought the server back without the preview's settings and started Caddy on 127.0.0.1:80/443; with `ATALAIA_PREVIEW=on` in the env file it now goes through `docker-compose.preview.yml` and starts the server alone (an env file that is both a preview and a hosted hub is refused before anything changes). And the live demo's API key is kept in a volume with only its hash in the database, so after a restore the two no longer matched and the demo's sync was refused (`invalid_api_key`) until `demo-refresh`'s next ten-minute round: the new `preview.sh restore FILE --yes` stops `demo-field` and `demo-refresh`, runs restore.sh, runs `demo live --key-out` once on the restored database and starts the two again. `preview-check` runs `preview.sh restore` and restore.sh against a stand-in `docker` that records its calls (no container): the preview's Compose file on every call, no Caddy, the key step after the database is replaced and before the demo starts, plain restore.sh unchanged; against main's restore.sh it fails. The runbook describes the Mac host (Colima starts at login, not power-on: automatic login, or log in after a power cut; the same for a cron backup), the shared tunnel (every connector gets a share of the visitors: stop the old host's), a section on moving the preview to another host, and keeps Windows with WSL as the alternative host; `check`'s "from Windows" line is said to be WSL-only, its public hint no longer names Windows, and `up` prints the demo's port (8001 by default) when `preview.env` does not set it. ShellCheck (CI's pinned v0.11.0) is clean on both scripts.
- **The app's NDK and APK checks run on a Mac too** (`app/tool/check.sh`): `check_android_lib` looked for `llvm-readelf` and `llvm-nm` under the NDK's `prebuilt/linux-x86_64`, which exists only on Linux, so `check.sh ndk` and `check.sh apk` built the libraries and then reported every one as "not built for" its ABI on macOS (the NDK there ships `prebuilt/darwin-x86_64`). It now takes the NDK's one host folder, and stops with a clear message if it has no `llvm-readelf`. The merge queue now runs on the maintainer's Mac mini, where the Flutter image cannot run in Colima's arm64 VM, so the app job runs natively there (`check.sh check --exclude-tags golden`, `ndk`, `apk`); checked on main 63b9739c: 2752 tests, three ABIs, the APK's libraries, assets and no Play services.
- **Access tokens on the field controller: short-lived, scoped, revocable** (maintainer decision of 2026-11-01, decisions AT-1 to AT-10; new `controller/atalaia_controller/access_tokens.py`, `api.py` `gate`, `store.py` schema 20, `main.py` `tokens --create/--list/--revoke`, console Settings panel `access-tokens.js` in pt, en, de and zh-tw, `controller/README.md`, `docs/manuals/{en,pt}/game-master.md`): the GM, and only the main GM token, makes a token with a label, a scope (`read`, `gm`, or `marshals` for adding and removing marshals), a lifetime (8 h by default, 7 days at most) and optionally one game, for a referee's phone, a visiting GM, an automated test run or a spectator screen. It is shown once (QR code in the console), only its SHA-256 is stored and compared in constant time, it is listed with its expiry and last use, revoked at once (an open stream ends too), refused when expired and purged, and cannot make other tokens. One `gate` in front of every route enforces scope and game, so a new route is covered; created, used (rate-limited), refused and revoked go to the audit log; wrong tokens are limited per address; the GM and read tokens behave as before. Tests: `controller/tests/test_access_tokens.py` (every route of the router against every scope) and a console browser test.
- **Trial: the app on Flutter 3.47.9 with every dependency at its latest** (branch `mac/flutter-3.47-trial`, a proposal for the repo-wide pin; `app/pubspec.yaml` `sdk: ^3.13.0`, `flutter: ">=3.47.0"`, `qr` 4.0.1, `pubspec.lock`; `lib/modes/prop_mode_fetch.dart`, `lib/branding/field_branding.dart`; 70 files reformatted by Dart 3.13's formatter): `qr` 4.0.1, `webview_flutter_wkwebview` 3.27.2 (no more `SecTrustGetCertificateAtIndex` deprecation warning on iOS), `vector_math` 2.4.4, `meta` 1.19.0 and the other SDK-pinned packages move with the SDK; only `material_color_utilities` and `test_api` stay behind, pinned by Flutter. Two warnings of the newer analyzer (`unawaited_return_in_try_block`) are fixed: the team crests' fetch is awaited inside its `try`, so its failure is caught as "no branding" as intended; a mode package's address loop narrows its `try` to the request, so a refused package is still reported once. The CI workflows, the Docker image and the docs still name Flutter 3.44 and are left to the pin change.
- **App dependencies: `crypto` 3.0.7 to 3.0.8** (`app/pubspec.yaml`, `pubspec.lock`, `app/README.md`): the only direct dependency with a newer release that Flutter 3.44 can resolve. The rest are either current (`cryptography` 2.9.0, `ffi` 2.2.0, `pointycastle` 4.0.0, `webview_flutter` 4.14.1, `yaml` 3.1.4, `flutter_lints` 6.0.0) or need a newer SDK: `qr` 4.0.1 needs Dart 3.13, `webview_flutter_wkwebview` 3.27 needs Flutter 3.47, and the transitive `meta`, `test_api`, `matcher`, `vector_math` and others are pinned by the Flutter SDK.
- **iOS: the app's own Swift builds without warnings on Xcode 27.2** (`app/ios/Runner/BrowserPlugin.swift`, `PasskeyPlugin.swift`, `app/docs/ios-on-a-mac.md`): the first local Mac build of the social-login and passkey plugins (until now compiled only by CI's `ios-build` job) gave three warnings, `'@preconcurrency' on conformance to 'ASWebAuthenticationPresentationContextProviding'` (and `ASAuthorizationControllerDelegate`, `ASAuthorizationControllerPresentationContextProviding`) `has no effect`: the iOS SDK already declares these protocols for the main actor, so the attribute is dropped, as for the QR scanner's delegate on 2026-10-26. No behaviour change. The one warning left is in the `webview_flutter_wkwebview` package (`SecTrustGetCertificateAtIndex` deprecated), not in the app.
- **iOS: the app's secrets are sealed with a key in the Keychain, no longer kept in a readable file** (decisions IOSK-1 to IOSK-6; new `app/ios/Runner/KeychainVaultPlugin.swift`, `AppDelegate.swift`, `lib/storage/secret_vault.dart`, `app_store.dart`, `lib/platform/channels.dart`, `integration_test/keystore_test.dart`, `app/docs/ios-on-a-mac.md`, `app/README.md`, `docs/templates/privacy-notice-{en,pt}.md`): a legal and privacy review found that on an iPhone the field join key, the marshal's private key, the read code, the player's secret at each field, the site refresh token and the scoreboard link tokens stayed in `settings.json`, protected only by the app sandbox. The iOS app now answers Android's `org.atalaia.app/keystore` channel with the same methods, errors and sealed format: an AES-256-GCM key (CryptoKit) in one Keychain item with `kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly` (never in a backup, never synced, never moved to another phone; no Face ID or passcode prompt), so `secrets.enc` and the store's existing migration work unchanged: the first start of this version seals the plaintext from an earlier install, reads it back and only then removes it from `settings.json`; a Keychain failure loses nothing and is retried on the next start. The on-device vault test now runs on iOS too and passes in the iOS 27.2 simulator against the real Keychain (5/5). The privacy notice templates say the secrets are in the Keychain on an iPhone, and that iOS may keep the key (no personal data) after an uninstall. No Dart behaviour change on Android.
- **Docs: where Xcode 27.2 puts the iOS release build** (`app/docs/ios-on-a-mac.md`): `flutter build ios --release` on Xcode 27.2 says "Build succeeded but the expected app at build/ios/iphoneos/Runner.app not found" and the app is in `build/ios/Release-iphoneos/Runner.app`; the guide's reinstall step names both folders, says the message is harmless and that installing over the test build (not uninstalling first) keeps the data, the permissions and the developer trust.
- **Docs pass of 1 November 2026: decision rows, the project page's roadmap texts, the phase status files, the README and the Field Box manuals** (documentation only): `docs/review/decisions-to-review.md` gains the rows of 28 October to 1 November that were missing (S2113, HLC, NV, the board 5 finding, TZ, FB, FBM, TS, I2C, PLU, MSC, MSF, MDM, TFT, TPL, TI, EPW-1 to EPW-4); `site/i18n/{pt,en,de,zh-tw}.yaml` roadmap phases 1 and 2 list the big screen's hold bar, held time and corner points, the other modes' screens, the boot splash, the I2C fault handling, the screen-text size check, the singular counts, the modem guide and the controllers' lost-epoch draw, each with what has been tried; `docs/phase-{1,2,5}-*/STATUS.md`; `README.md` (status, phase rows, the modem guide); `docs/manuals/{en,pt}/field-box.md` (the boot screen on both screens, the reboot reason and the console's crash wording).
- **The iPhone checklist test's marshal checks work against a real controller, and leave nothing behind** (`app/integration_test/ios_checklist_test.dart`, `app/docs/ios-on-a-mac.md`): item 9 checked a test player in at the controller's plain `http://` address with a throwaway CA, but the app's field client has spoken only TLS with the field's own CA since check-R1 (`ioProbe`, `SecureSocket`), so both marshal checks failed against any controller ("HandshakeException ... WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER"), on Android too. The check-in now fetches the field's CA from `GET /api/v1/controller/ca` on the plain HTTP address, as the app does on a first scan, and uses the HTTPS address (the same host on port 8443, or `ATALAIA_TEST_CONTROLLER_HTTPS`). It also stored the pin with an all-zero `ca_sha256`, which the app drops when it reads a trusted field back (APP-1), so the marshal screen said "check in first"; the pin now carries the CA's real hash. On the iPhone 15 Pro Max against the bench controller: 11 passed, 2 skipped (the camera and the location prompt, both left to a person). A run cut short on the bench iPhone left its marshal `itest-ios` on the controller: the test now also removes every marshal and every check-in named `itest-ios` in a game that has not ended, before and after the run (GM token, best effort).
- **The field controller survives a wrong clock: it says so, keeps its certificates valid, deletes nothing, and a GM sets the time from the console** (decisions CLK-1 to CLK-9; new `controller/atalaia_controller/wallclock.py`, `tls.py`, `engine.py`, `checkin.py`, `branding.py`, `positions.py`, `pod_config.py`, `firmware_updates.py`, `mode_fetch.py`, `store.py`, `api.py`, console `components/clock-settings.js` and `i18n/{pt,en,de,zh-tw}.json`, `controller/README.md` "The wall clock" with the audit table, `docs/manuals/{en,pt}/controller-setup.md` "The clock" and "Using a travel router"): a Raspberry Pi 4 has no real-time clock, so after a power cycle in a field with no internet it comes up at the last save or at 1970 (a Pi 5 only has a clock with its optional battery), and nobody had checked what the controller did then. The audit found: the field CA and server certificate were issued from `now - 1 hour` for 10 years / 365 days, so a CA made at 1970 expired in 1980 and was refused by every phone, and one made days behind was not yet valid for them (and the CA is never replaced); the I/O pods' settings `rev` is the clock in seconds and a pod keeps the last `rev` in its flash, so after a restart at 1970 the pods ignored every push; a clock that jumps years forward would have run the retention purge over everything; visibility grants and the firmware / mode-fetch timeouts ran on the wall clock (a jump ended or extended them). Fixed: certificates are issued from the day before the release date to a year or ten after the later of the clock and the release, and a CA already expired before the release (made at 1970) is replaced; the `rev` is kept in the store; the purge waits while the clock is below the release date, the highest time the controller saw (stored every 5 minutes, so a Pi 4 that comes up days behind is caught) or the newest record in the store, and for an hour after a forward jump of a day or more, and its throttle runs on monotonic time; grants and the firmware and mode-fetch timeouts use monotonic time. The status (`GET /api/v1/controller`, `GET /api/v1/clock`) says whether the clock is plausible; the console shows a banner (pt, en, de, zh-tw) and a Settings card with the controller's time and the device's, and the GM can **set the controller's clock from this device** (`PUT /api/v1/clock`, GM token): forward only, never below what the controller has seen, at most 14 days ahead of a plausible clock (5 years above the release for an implausible one); a backward move is refused so that expired player codes and certificates cannot be revived. The service has no right to set the system clock, so the correction lives in the process; the manual says `sudo timedatectl set-time` keeps it across restarts. Manuals (en, pt): a "The clock" section (Pi 4 vs Pi 5 with its battery, what the controller does, how to set it) and a "Using a travel router" section (a fixed address by DHCP reservation, a router that works with no internet uplink, no AP / client isolation or guest network, the poster's address). Tests: `test_wallclock.py` (guard, forward-only set and every refusal, the API and tokens, the purge that waits, a jump, certificates for a 1970 and a days-behind clock, a 1970 CA replaced, pod revs, grants), a browser test of the banner and the set. Not tried on a real Pi 4 or 5.
- **Flutter pinned at 3.44.9, not 3.44.0** (decisions FL-1 to FL-4; `tools/dev/flutter/Dockerfile` new, `tools/docker_app.py`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `app/tool/check.sh`, `app/pubspec.yaml` and `dart_test.yaml` comments, `app/test/goldens/flutter_test_config.dart`, `app/README.md`, `app/docs/ios-on-a-mac.md`, `tools/README.md`, phase 3 PLAN and STATUS): Flutter 3.44.0 cannot build iOS with Xcode 27, because its `thinFramework` runs `lipo -verify_arch arm64 x86_64` and Xcode 27's `lipo` refuses more than one architecture; 3.44.9 checks each architecture on its own. cirruslabs publishes no 3.44.9 image (its tags end at 3.44.0 and `stable` is 3.44.0), so the app image `atalaia-flutter:3.44.9` is now built from its 3.44.0 image (still pinned by digest) with the SDK replaced by Google's official 3.44.9 Linux archive, checked against SHA-256 `a9120fa4...efcb` from `releases_linux.json`; `docker_app.py` builds it on first use and when the Dockerfile changes, and CI's three Docker jobs build it first. CI's iOS and emulator jobs use `flutter-version: 3.44.9`. Dart 3.12.2 satisfies `sdk: ^3.12.0`; `pubspec.lock` is unchanged.
- **iOS: the app's own Swift builds without warnings on Xcode 27.2** (`app/ios/Runner/BrowserPlugin.swift`, `PasskeyPlugin.swift`, `app/docs/ios-on-a-mac.md`): the first local Mac build of the social-login and passkey plugins (until now compiled only by CI's `ios-build` job) gave three warnings, `'@preconcurrency' on conformance to 'ASWebAuthenticationPresentationContextProviding'` (and `ASAuthorizationControllerDelegate`, `ASAuthorizationControllerPresentationContextProviding`) `has no effect`: the iOS SDK already declares these protocols for the main actor, so the attribute is dropped, as for the QR scanner's delegate on 2026-10-26. No behaviour change. The one warning left is in the `webview_flutter_wkwebview` package (`SecTrustGetCertificateAtIndex` deprecated), not in the app.
- **iOS: the app's secrets are sealed with a key in the Keychain, no longer kept in a readable file** (decisions IOSK-1 to IOSK-6; new `app/ios/Runner/KeychainVaultPlugin.swift`, `AppDelegate.swift`, `lib/storage/secret_vault.dart`, `app_store.dart`, `lib/platform/channels.dart`, `integration_test/keystore_test.dart`, `app/docs/ios-on-a-mac.md`, `app/README.md`, `docs/templates/privacy-notice-{en,pt}.md`): a legal and privacy review found that on an iPhone the field join key, the marshal's private key, the read code, the player's secret at each field, the site refresh token and the scoreboard link tokens stayed in `settings.json`, protected only by the app sandbox. The iOS app now answers Android's `org.atalaia.app/keystore` channel with the same methods, errors and sealed format: an AES-256-GCM key (CryptoKit) in one Keychain item with `kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly` (never in a backup, never synced, never moved to another phone; no Face ID or passcode prompt), so `secrets.enc` and the store's existing migration work unchanged: the first start of this version seals the plaintext from an earlier install, reads it back and only then removes it from `settings.json`; a Keychain failure loses nothing and is retried on the next start. The on-device vault test now runs on iOS too and passes in the iOS 27.2 simulator against the real Keychain (5/5). The privacy notice templates say the secrets are in the Keychain on an iPhone, and that iOS may keep the key (no personal data) after an uninstall. No Dart behaviour change on Android.
- **Flutter pinned at 3.47.9 (Dart 3.13.7) and the app's dependencies brought to their latest** (decisions FLU-1 to FLU-6; new `tools/dev/flutter/Dockerfile`; `tools/docker_app.py`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `app/tool/check.sh`, `app/pubspec.yaml`, `app/pubspec.lock`, `app/dart_test.yaml`, `app/README.md`, `app/docs/ios-on-a-mac.md`, `tools/README.md`, phase 3 PLAN and STATUS; `lib/modes/prop_mode_fetch.dart`, `lib/branding/field_branding.dart`; 70 files reformatted by Dart 3.13's formatter): Flutter 3.44.0 cannot build iOS with Xcode 27, because its `thinFramework` runs `lipo -verify_arch arm64 x86_64` and Xcode 27's `lipo` refuses more than one architecture; 3.44.9 and later check each architecture on its own, and the Mac session tested 3.47.9 on iOS. cirruslabs publishes no 3.44.9 or 3.47.9 image (its tags end at 3.44.0 and `stable` is 3.44.0), so the app image `atalaia-flutter:3.47.9` is built from its 3.44.0 image (still pinned by digest) with the SDK replaced by Google's official 3.47.9 Linux archive, checked against SHA-256 `25ed8c3c...4c5a` from `releases_linux.json`; `docker_app.py` builds it on first use and when the Dockerfile changes, and CI's three Docker jobs build it first. CI's iOS and emulator jobs use `flutter-version: 3.47.9`. `app/pubspec.yaml` asks `sdk: ^3.13.0` and `flutter: ">=3.47.0"`. Dependencies: `crypto` 3.0.7 to 3.0.8, `qr` 4.0.1 (needs Dart 3.13), `webview_flutter_wkwebview` 3.27.2 (no more `SecTrustGetCertificateAtIndex` deprecation warning on iOS), and `vector_math`, `meta` and the other SDK-pinned packages move with the SDK; only `material_color_utilities` and `test_api` stay behind, pinned by Flutter. Two warnings of the newer analyzer (`unawaited_return_in_try_block`) are fixed: the team crests' fetch is awaited inside its `try`, so its failure is caught as "no branding" as intended; a mode package's address loop narrows its `try` to the request, so a refused package is still reported once. Goldens: 24 images (chrono, settings, prop setup, player views) redrawn, see FLU-4.
- **Project page: the Master and Satellites test-run caption says the three findings are fixed, and the mode's GIF description matches the new screens** (decisions MSF-1 to MSF-3; `site/i18n/{pt,en,de,zh-tw}.yaml` `modes.master_satellites.real_caption` and `gifs.master_satellites.alt`): the clip's caption keeps the story (the 1 November 2026 run found "MASTER" for "MESTRE", "ZONA" before the first capture, and "desde" instead of the held time) and now says all three are fixed in the firmware after this run, that the clip still shows the old screens and that a new recording will follow; it does not claim a hardware test of the fixes. The animation's description said a satellite box turned red, but the animation shows the master: it now names the MESTRE / MESTRE OCUPADA header, the held time and the team total in the corner (regenerated GIFs of `5e32a100`).
- **A screen expression whose texts do not fit is refused at check time, and a render that fails shows `?`, never a blank** (decisions TPL-1 to TPL-6; `core/src/screen_template.c`, `core/include/atalaia/screen_template.h`, `app/lib/ui/template/expression.dart`, `simulator/atalaia_sim/screens.py`, `modes/README.md` "Authoring limits of a screen expression"): the evaluator copied every `'text'` literal and every string it read from the data into one 256-byte scratch, and an expression that ran out of it rendered an empty text without a word: a header with a `lang == 'pt' && ... || ...` chain for four languages and a role, each `lang` read taking its bytes too, went blank on a box and passed every check (the Master and Satellites header, worked around with three labels and a Python guard test). Now the literals have their own 256 bytes (each takes its length + 1, every branch of `&&` and `||` counts, so the size is a property of the expression and never of the data or the language) and the data strings their own 160, so the load-time check, which every route runs (the simulator's and the registry's `atalaia_sim test`, the Field Box's compile of a built-in or fetched template, the app's Dart port), is exact: an expression over the limit is refused with its size, `<mode> / <screen> / layout[2] label text: the expression {...} has 'text' literals of 281 bytes in all ...; the limit is 256`, and the core's own message says `its 'texts' need 281 bytes of 256`. The test run prints a `warning:` for an expression above 230 bytes. If a render fails anyway (a template that skipped the check, three long data strings), the text shows `?` in its place and the Field Box's log names the expression once (`atalaia_tpl_set_log`, `ESP_LOGW`); in the app the same through `templateLogHook`; a data string with no room left reads `?` and is cut when it only partly fits. Error messages repeat at most the first 40 bytes of an expression (cut at a character) so the reason always fits. `atalaia_tpl_expr_literal_bytes` (core, Dart binding, simulator) reports the size. Tests: C (the edge at 256, all branches counted, the message through a template, data strings not stealing the literals' room, the `?` and the single log line), Dart against the core (check results, sizes, texts, the cut echo), Python (the named message, the exact edge, the warning, and every built-in mode and registry example under the limit). No shipped template changes: the largest uses 186 of 256 literal bytes (Bomb's and Sabotage's standby label). The evaluator's scratch grows by 160 bytes on the stack of whoever renders a text (the box's LVGL task, 8 KB, one expression at a time).
- **Tests: the project page's browser test runs in one command and can no longer pass by skipping; the console's Check-in 409 is explained and pinned** (decisions TI-1 to TI-6; new `tools/docker_site_browser.py`, `tools/dev/site-browser/` (`Dockerfile`, `requirements.in`, hashed `requirements.txt`), `tools/site_browser_test.py`, `controller/atalaia_controller/engine.py`, `controller/tests/test_checkin.py`, `controller/console/tests/test_browser.py`): `tools/site_browser_test.py`, run as its docstring said in Playwright's Docker image, printed "OK (skipped=1)" because that image's `python3` has no `playwright` module, so three agents installed it by hand. `python tools/docker_site_browser.py` builds an image with the client pinned by hash (1.49.0, like the other browser images), runs the test against the read-only repository and removes its container; the test now fails with a message when Playwright is missing, and only `ATALAIA_SKIP_SITE_BROWSER=1` skips it. Console: `test_checkin_tab_follows_the_live_game` failed now and then with `HTTP 409 GET .../chrono-list`. Cause: the tab reloads its chrono list on every stream message, and a reload that reaches the controller while the GM ends the game made `public_game` answer 409 "no live game" (the live check and the start check are two reads); main already catches it since be9321a8 (2026-10-26), after the one recorded failure, but nothing tested it. `public_game` now reads the live game and its engine once, and `GameEndsDuringAGetTests` forces the interleaving and hammers GETs against 40 game ends (409 on every run without the catch, 200 with it); the browser test's failed-request report now includes the start of the answer.
- **VIP Payload follows the box's language, and the OLED shows the held time on every held zone** (decisions TFT-8 and TFT-9; `modes/vip_payload/assets/screens/*.yaml`, `modes/vip_payload/mode.yaml`, `firmware/esp32/main/oled_view.c`, `docs/manuals/{en,pt}/field-box.md`, `modes/README.md`): VIP's three templates read the mode parameter `lang` (`params.lang`, filled with the game's language, decision P5H-23, there because the Medic Kit's script writes text and a script cannot read the box's language; VIP's script writes none), so a box whose language was changed after the game began, or a game saved with another `lang`, drew its checkpoints in the old language, and the default without one was Portuguese. They now read the box's `lang` like every other mode (English when the box has none). The `lang` parameter stays declared, so a game or scenario saved with it still loads, but nothing reads it (its label says so in four languages); the Medic Kit and the Hacking Terminal keep theirs. On the OLED, a held Master status and a held VIP checkpoint that carry `since` now read like Domination's captured screen: the team big and `held for 2:15` below (`ocupada 2:15`, `gehalten 2:15`, `已佔領 2:15`) from the game clock, the hold bar of a team taking the zone still replacing it; they showed the mode's name and the team before. Progressive Domination's OLED already showed it (its `captured` screen is Domination's), now pinned by reference images in pt, de and zh-TW. Nothing checked needed more than 230 literal bytes (VIP's longest expression is about 140, far under the warning). Tests: a Python test renders every VIP text in four languages (none empty, none reading `params.lang`, a saved `lang: en` ignored), OLED mapping and 9 new OLED reference images (3 master_status images changed), a TFT reference image of a game saved with `lang: en` on a Portuguese box, three VIP images renamed `_pt` (same pixels). Regenerated `builtin_modes.g.dart` and the Master and Satellites and VIP Payload GIFs; the app's VIP golden data carries `"lang":"pt"` as a phone's does.

- **The Field Box's big screen shows a boot splash: the tower and the firmware version** (decisions TS-1 to TS-5; `firmware/esp32/main/screen_renderer.c` template `splash`, new `tft_splash.c/h`, `shell.c`, Kconfig `ATALAIA_TFT_SPLASH_MS`; docs in `firmware/esp32/README.md`, `docs/BRING_UP.md`, `docs/manuals/{en,pt}/field-box.md`): until now the TFT stayed blank until the first game screen. The screen template language already compares numbers, so the templates now pick the form with `points == 1 && ...` in front of the plural in the same chain (no new helper, so the core, the Field Box and the app stay the same code; `modes/README.md` and `docs/translations/README.md` say how); fixed: Domination, the King of the Hill example, Progressive Domination, Master and Satellites (points, satellites), Target Windows (hits), Respawn ("1 vida restante" / "1 life left" / "vida de EQUIPA") and the Codes prompt ("+50 points for RED" became "Points for RED: +50", which has no noun to agree). The app's `Strings.t` and the console's `t()` take a singular text `<key>_one` for `n: 1` (pt-PT, English, German; Chinese has no plural) and 18 app texts and 17 console texts have one now; the "(s)" spellings ("série(s)", "error(s)", "port(s)") became real plurals with a singular form. New tests: the simulator checks each fixed template's rendered text in every language, the firmware has six singular reference images, the app's template and strings tests and the console's browser test check the singular. The site server's staff pages (`staff_i18n.py`) and the controller's English log texts still have a few "(s)" and count texts, listed in the decision rows.
- **A flaky I2C bus can no longer reboot a Field Box, and a crash is reported as a crash** (decisions I2C-1 to I2C-6; `firmware/esp32/main/hal/esp/hal_esp_i2c.c`, `hal_esp_keypad.c`, `hal_esp_oled.c`, `hal_esp_nfc.c`, `hal_esp_battery.c`, new `main/i2c_health.c`, `main/reset_reason.c`, `reset_reason_esp.c`, `box_game.c`, `radio/field_link.c`, `shell.c`; `controller/console/src/ui.js` and `i18n/*.json`; `protocol/schema/events.yaml`; `docs/runbooks/i2c-fault-bench.md`). Bench, 2026-10-31: a keypad with bad solder joints rebooted board 5 six times in three minutes ("Interrupt wdt timeout on CPU0" in `i2c_isr_handler_default`), and the controller logged each restart as `node.rebooted` with reason `power`. The legacy I2C driver leaves an interrupt it does not own uncleared, so a noisy bus made the handler spin until the interrupt watchdog fired. All four parts on the bus (OLED, PCF8574 keypad, PN532, MAX17048) now use ESP-IDF's `i2c_master` driver (its handler clears first, every transaction has a timeout and a controller reset), with a glitch filter, a per-device health record (3 failures in a row suspend a device, a trial after 0.5 s doubling to 30 s, one success restores it, a run of failures resets the bus) and error counters; a failing keypad reads as "no key" and the game runs on. `node.rebooted`'s `reason` was a constant `power`: the box now sends why the chip came up (`esp_reset_reason()`: `power`, `brownout`, `software`, `panic`, `watchdog`, `deepsleep`, `unknown`), the controllers pass it through unchanged, and the console's feed says "A box crashed and restarted (program crash)" for a panic or a watchdog reset, in pt, en, de and zh-tw. Host tests: 8 for the back-off and bus-reset logic, 3 for the reset words, a box test that each reason reaches the event, a controller test that `panic` and `watchdog` are logged as sent, a console test for the words. **Not tried on real hardware**: `docs/runbooks/i2c-fault-bench.md`.
- **The rest of the singulars: "(s)" spellings and "1 dias" gone from the staff pages, the controller, the editor and the manuals** (decisions PLU-6 to PLU-10; `server/atalaia_server/web/staff_i18n.py` and `static/staff/staff.js`, `controller/atalaia_controller/{engine,mode_fetch,firmware_updates}.py` and their Dart twins in `app/lib/controller/`, `simulator/atalaia_sim/editor/`, `app/lib/l10n/`, `docs/manuals/`): the staff pages' `t()` now takes `<key>_one` when `n` is 1 (and `staff_i18n.count_text()` does the same on the server, for the page's own selects), like the app's `Strings.t` and the console's `t()` since PLU-2; `problema(s)`, `chave(s) de acesso`, `Passkey(s)`, `Problem(e)`, `certificado(s)`, "{n} dias" / "{n} days" and the import counts ("1 linha", "1 row", "1 Zeile") and "at most 1 replica" have a real plural and a singular (the ATAK certificate warning takes its "within N days" from `days_within`, so "within 1 day" and "1 certificate expires"); German and pt-PT keep their gender-neutral wording, Chinese has no singular keys (the staff page tests allow that). The controller's start check and its start guards say "1 zone node joined", "box 4", "node 3" instead of "node(s)" (the Python controller and the phone controller alike); the editor's "is running ... with 1 box" and German "Einstellung ... hat den Typ ..." (was "ein(e)"); the app's respawn text ("1 vida"), the registration-import counts and the mode-keys line (now "Chaves de confiança: 2, chaves das caixas: 1", no count agreement to get wrong); the site server's operator commands ("1 session revoked", "1 note", erasure ledger lines) and the simulator's test summary; the pt-PT manuals quote the texts as the app shows them. Tests: staff page singular/plural tests (Python and the real `t()` in node), editor browser test, controller start-check/guard text tests, app strings tests. Not changed on purpose: the site (no count phrases), the console's georeference line (always 3 or more points), the box's serial log lines and the developer tools' messages that list names.
- **Project page: the first real Field Box, a bench build, with a photo and a clip** (decisions FB-1 to FB-8; `site/src/build.html`, `index.html`, new partial `realvideo.html`, `site/style.css`, `site/i18n/*.yaml`, `site/img/field-box-*`, `REUSE.toml`, `site/README.md`): the build guides page's Field Box section opens with "The first build, on the bench": the maintainer's photo of 2026-10-31 (a Heltec V4 board, the 2.8" ILI9341 TFT, the OLED, a 4x3 keypad and team buttons on loose wires, no enclosure) as a responsive `<picture>` (960 and 1600 px, WebP and JPEG), and a 9 s muted loop of a Domination capture changing from red to blue (H.264 and VP9, 720 px, 126 and 165 KB, with a poster); the main page's "What it looks like" gets the same clip as "The real box, on the bench" beside the mode animations. The captions in pt, en, de and zh-tw say it is a bench build and the first board to run a game, not a finished box. The clip has a pause button and does not autoplay under `prefers-reduced-motion`; all files are self-hosted. All metadata is stripped from every image and video, and the audio is removed. The roadmap (phase 1), the build page's status line and the glossary no longer say that no box was built (the Field Box only; the Satellite, I/O pod and target placeholders stay). Added weight: about 1.2 MB in the repository, about 290 KB for a visitor who scrolls to both (WebP 960 and WebM).
- **The Field Box's big screen shows the capture hold bar, how long the zone has been held, and each team's points** (decisions TZ-1 to TZ-5; `firmware/esp32/main/screen_renderer.c`, `screen_renderer.h`, `shell.c`, `modes/domination/assets/screens/captured.yaml`): while a team holds its button on a zone screen the TFT draws a bar with that team's name along the bottom, from the same logic as the OLED's (BN-26); the captured screen counts up `held 1:23` (pt `ocupada há`, de `gehalten seit`, zh-tw `已佔領`) instead of `held since <time>`; under Domination each team's points at this box show in team-coloured chips in the top right. Only the changing widgets are redrawn. The OLED is unchanged. Eight Domination reference images changed (the chips) and four were added; `test_screen_renderer.c` checks the totals.
- **Project page: the main page shows the mode animations, the box's screenshots moved to the modes page** (`site/src/index.html`, `modes.html`, new partials `modegif.html` and `viewer.html` shared by both, `site/style.css` `.gif-row`, `site/i18n/*.yaml` `screens.intro`, `gifs_more`, `box_title`, `box_intro`, `tools/site_browser_test.py`): "What it looks like" opens with the Domination, Bomb and Sabotage GIFs (same markup as the modes page, with the play/pause button and the reduced-motion still) and a link to every mode, then the app's screenshots as before; the eight box stills and their zoom viewer are now "The box's screens, step by step" on the modes page. The browser test checks both pages in four languages at 375, 768 and 1280 px, with and without reduced motion.
- **Project page: the top menu is three dropdowns and GitHub** (decisions NAV-7 to NAV-12; `site/src/partials/header.html`, `site/style.css`, `tools/build_site.py` `NAV_GROUPS`, `tools/site_browser_test.py`): Learn (modes, radio, how it fits, glossary), Build (build guides, try it, roadmap) and Community (community hub, registry), in pt, en, de and zh-tw. The menus are `<details>`, so they open with Enter or Space without any script, `name="nav"` closes the others, a tiny optional script adds Escape and click-outside, the menu that holds the page being read is underlined and its link has `aria-current`. On a phone they stay on one row under the brand. The browser test opens each menu with the keyboard on every page at 375 and 1280 px.
- **Project page: the language switcher is four emoji flags at the top right, and body text uses its section's width** (decisions NAV-1 to NAV-6; `site/src/partials/header.html`, `footer.html`, `site/style.css`, `site/fonts/`, `tools/site_browser_test.py`): the PT/EN/DE/中文 links left the crowded menu for a strip above it, one flag each (🇵🇹 🇬🇧 🇩🇪 🇹🇼), each a link to the same page in that language with `hreflang`, `lang`, `aria-label` and `title` in the language's own name and `aria-current` on the current one. Chrome and Edge on Windows draw a flag emoji as two letters, so the four flags come from a 7.8 KB subset of Twemoji Country Flags (self-hosted, `unicode-range: U+1F1E6-1F1FF` so no other text uses it, first in the flags' font stack; art CC BY 4.0, credited in every page's footer, `LICENSES/CC-BY-4.0.txt`, `REUSE.toml`, `site/fonts/README.md`). The `ch` caps on the hero introduction, the name line, the GIF section's intro, the footer, the build guides' text and the figure captions are gone, so they use the section's width. `tools/site_browser_test.py` (Playwright) opens every page in every language at phone and desktop widths in both colour schemes.
- **A staff page for the hub link code** (decisions HLC-1 to HLC-12; `/staff/account/hub-share`, `server/atalaia_server/web/templates/staff/hubshare.html`, `static/staff/hubshare.js`, texts in `staff_i18n_hub.py` in pt-PT, English, German and Traditional Chinese): in a hosted hub, a signed-in person makes a link code at one field and enters it at another, sees the other fields their account is linked at and whether sharing is on there, gives or withdraws this field's chrono-sharing consent (`PUT`/`DELETE /me/consents/chrono-sharing/{field}`, with the tick and the text that says what crosses), and unlinks. A wrong, expired or used code, the rate limit (10 an hour) and every other refusal are shown in words. It is linked from the account page only when the server is a hub (a single field answers 404 `not_hosted`). API: `GET /me/hub-share` also answers `field`, `consent` and `others`; a third `SECURITY DEFINER` function `public.atalaia_share_peers(uuid)` (made by `hosted migrate`, checked by `hosted check`) lists the other fields a person is linked at. Tests: `tests/test_hub_share_page.py`, new cases in `tests/test_hub_share.py` and `tests/test_tenants.py`, `tests_browser/test_hubshare_browser.py`. User and staff manuals (en, pt), `server/README.md` and `server/docs/DEPLOY.md` updated.
