- **The check-in privacy notice is now version 2, on both controllers, and everyone is asked again** (decisions NV-1 to NV-8; `controller/atalaia_controller/config.py` and `app/lib/controller/controller_checkin.dart` `notice_version`/`noticeVersion` 1 to 2, `controller/templates/checkin-notice-pt.md` and `-en.md`, `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`, `app/lib/checkin/checkin_screen.dart`, `app/lib/player/player_screens.dart`): the notice text that version 2 points at already says the reviewed things (the membership card is checked and the number erased; position sharing is a separate, unticked choice), so the maintainer's bump (LEG-18) only had to move the number. A client that still sends version 1 gets 409 `notice_version_changed`; the app turns that into the new notice with "read and confirm" (in the four languages) instead of a raw error, with the position box unticked. A "yes" to position sharing recorded under version 1 no longer counts: positions are refused (409 `position_sharing_off`) and not shown until the player says yes again under version 2, and the app's position switch names the notice it showed (`PUT /api/v1/checkin/me/position-sharing` takes an optional `notice_version`) and shows the new notice first when it changed. The shared contract cases, the Pi's tests and the phone controller's tests moved to version 2 together. The site server's own notice version (`ATALAIA_PRIVACY_NOTICE_VERSION`) is a different thing and is not touched; the preview server keeps `preview-2026-10-31`.
- **The project page footer says what the privacy template is** (site/i18n, four languages): the template states the legal positions the maintainer's lawyer approved on 30 October 2026, but its wording is still a draft that no lawyer has reviewed as text, and each field checks and adopts it.

- **Docs: site login on iOS and the epoch follow-up brought into the README, the phase 2, 3 and 4 status files, SECURITY.md and the project page** (decisions IOSL-1 to IOSL-9 and EP-1 to EP-12, filed in `docs/review/decisions-to-review.md`): the roadmap and status lines that called site login on iOS "not built" now say it is built in Swift, compiled on CI, not run on an iPhone, with passkeys opt-in at build time; RFC 0001 Amendment 25 (the epoch floor, the former-leader id rule, Wi-Fi props getting CMD peer) is described where the epoch and replay limits are, as host-tested only with the bench steps in `docs/runbooks/epoch-recovery-bench.md` not run on any board. The project page's phase 2 and 3 lists are updated in pt, en, de and zh-tw and the pages rebuilt.

- **Site login on iPhone: the social-login browser and passkeys** (decisions IOSL-1 to IOSL-9; `app/ios/Runner/BrowserPlugin.swift`, `PasskeyPlugin.swift`, `LinkPlugin.swift`, `AppDelegate.swift`, `Runner.entitlements`, `Runner.xcodeproj`, `app/test/platform/ios_channels_test.dart`, `app/docs/ios-on-a-mac.md`): Swift twins of Android's `BrowserPlugin.kt` and `PasskeyPlugin.kt` on the same channels (`org.atalaia.app/browser` `open`; `org.atalaia.app/passkeys` `available`, `create`, `get`), with the same answers and error codes, so nothing in `lib/` changed. The browser is an `ASWebAuthenticationSession` with an https callback on the site's `/app/oidc` (iOS 17.4 or later; earlier iPhones read as "not available"), and its callback URL is handed to Dart through the links channel as Android's App Link is; passkeys use `ASAuthorizationPlatformPublicKeyCredentialProvider` (iOS 16 or later) and answer the WebAuthn JSON the site server verifies. They need the paid Apple program, `webcredentials:<site host>` in the app's associated domains (`Runner.entitlements`, off until `ATALAIA_ENTITLEMENTS` is set so a free account still signs) and `ATALAIA_APP_IOS_APP_IDS` on the site server (which already served `/.well-known/apple-app-site-association`); the steps are in `ios-on-a-mac.md`. Dart tests pin the channel contract; **the Swift was compiled by CI's `ios-build` (on a draft pull request) and has not been run on an iPhone**.

#### Security

- **The controller's access log no longer holds the GM, player or read tokens** (finding LIVE2-1, Medium, of the 2026-10-30 authenticated security test; decisions SR-120 to SR-125; `controller/atalaia_controller/stream_auth.py`, `api.py`, `guard.py`, `controller/console/src/ui.js`, `app/lib/controller/controller_api.dart`, `app/lib/player/field_feed.dart`, `server/preview/demo/field/proxy.py`): the console opened the stream as `wss://.../api/v1/stream?token=<token>`, and uvicorn's access log keeps the query. The token now travels in the `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` header (`atalaia.v1` plus `atalaia.token.<base64url>`; the server answers `atalaia.v1` and never echoes it); the app sends `Authorization: Bearer`. `?token=` still works for one transition (a console or an app installed before this, a scoreboard link opened in an old browser tab), logs a deprecation warning once, and goes two releases after the console and the app both use the header. In every case a logging filter blanks `token=`, `access_token=`, `code=`, `state=`, `key=` and similar query values, `#token=` and `Bearer` values in uvicorn's logs, on the Pi controller and on the site server (the phone controller keeps no access log; the site server's stream and staff pages authenticate by cookie and never took a token in the address). Tokens already in old logs stay there: change the GM and read tokens (`gm_token` and `read_token` in `[api]`, or delete the generated ones so new ones are made) on any field whose logs were shared.
- **`rand()` in the modes can no longer be predicted from what the API shows players** (finding LIVE2-2, Medium, game integrity; decisions SR-126 to SR-129; `core/src/rand.c`, `core/include/atalaia/node.h`, `protocol/vectors/rand.json`, `simulator/atalaia_sim/core.py`, `firmware/esp32/main/box_game.c`, `app/lib/ffi/core_node.dart`): the seed was only the game seed and the node id, and both reach players (`GET /games/{id}`, `/nodes`, the site server's game lists), so Hacking Terminal codes, Target Windows teams and the like could be worked out. The state is now the first 16 bytes of HMAC-SHA256(game key, `atalaia/rand/v1` || seed || node id), the same on the box, the Pi controller and the phone (shared vectors in C, Python and Dart, the HMAC also recomputed from `hashlib`). The game seed is no longer returned to players, the read token or the scoreboard by either controller, nor by the site server to anyone but staff. **Replays:** the same game (same key, seed, node ids and inputs) replays identically, but a game recorded before this release replays to different `rand()` values than it was played with: the old key-less sequence is only used for a stand-alone game (no key) and for the simulator's own mode tests. A game in progress while a box or controller is updated is not affected for its nodes that keep running, but a node that reboots into the new firmware mid-game and a controller that restarts into the new build draw other values from then on: do not update in the middle of a game.

- **The Lua sandbox: five holes from the ASan/UBSan review of 2026-10-30 closed** (findings LIVE2-S1 to LIVE2-S5 and the low notes; decisions SR-130 to SR-139; `core/src/sandbox.c`, `script_api.c`, `node.c`, the vendored `ltablib.c` and `luaconf.h`, `core/third_party/README.md`). S1 (High): `field.nodes` leaked the host's JSON buffer whenever the decoder raised (bad JSON, nesting, the memory cap, the watchdog), because Lua's error skipped the `free()`; it decodes in a protected call, frees, then raises again (an ASan case fails without it). S2: `table.move`, `insert`, `remove`, `concat`, `unpack` and `sort` ran in C with no instruction hook, so a loop of them could hold the game task for seconds; they now charge the budget in proportion to the elements they touch, and `sort`'s pivot no longer comes from `clock()` and `time()` (a replay could sort differently). S3 (High on the ESP32): Lua's 200 nested C levels need 69 to 273 KB of C stack against the game task's 12 KB; the limit is 20 (the built-in modes need up to 14), `LUAL_BUFFERSIZE` is 64 and the pattern depth 32, and the game task's stack is 24 KB (the mode-fetch task's 20 KB); the figures come from `-fstack-usage` on the Xtensa, the bench measurement is still to do. S4: one host entry (an event, an advance of time) could chain callbacks, each inside its own limit, for minutes; the callbacks of one entry share 4 times the limit (the built-in modes' worst entry is under 2,000 instructions; `after(0)` loops of tiny callbacks count 250 each). S5: each coroutine restarted the hook's counter, so a loop of short coroutines ran about 50 times its budget (measured: 9.8 million instructions on a 200,000 limit); making a coroutine costs one hook step, and a trip reaches the main thread. Low notes: node ids over 16 bits no longer wrap in `field.owner/send/common_owner`; a timer past 49.7 days of game time stops instead of wrapping to "now"; `field.send` is limited to 256 a host entry; `emit` refuses `script.*` event types (the runtime's). The sandbox-escape battery grew from 52 to 74 scripts, plus tests for the coroutine metering, the entry cap, parser nesting and the timer wrap.
- **A hand-built invite part longer than a slot no longer overflows the reassembler** (fuzzing finding, defence in depth; decision SR-139; `core/src/field_frame.c` `atalaia_ff_reasm_add`): `chunk_len` is a byte and the slot holds 153, and only the wire decoder capped it. The Python and Dart reassemblers keep their parts in growable buffers and cannot overflow.
- **`set-password`: an operator sets a password on an existing account, and the HTTPS redirect's WebSocket refusal is tested** (the two "Left" items of the 2026-10-30 site-server security review; decision SP-1; `server/atalaia_server/cli.py`, `server/tests/test_roles.py`, `test_tenants.py`, `test_https_redirect.py`): a passwordless staff account with no second factor cannot change its login methods from the site (SR-2) and nothing gave the operator a way to unblock it. `python -m atalaia_server set-password --username NAME --reason TEXT [--yes] [--password-stdin] [--field SLUG]` asks for the password twice without echo (or reads one line of standard input with `--password-stdin`, which needs `--yes`); there is no password option, so it cannot land in the shell history or `ps`. It applies the API's policy (`passwords.policy_problem`, shared with `create-admin`), refuses an unknown or erased account and `ATALAIA_PASSWORD_LOGIN=off`, revokes every session of the account, and writes `password_set` to the audit log (never the password). Documented in `server/README.md` and the preview runbook. A new test runs a `ws` scope through `HttpsRedirectMiddleware` and checks it is closed with 1008 without reaching the application (and that `wss`, no proxy header and loopback pass through); the middleware was right, no code change.

- **A recorded game command played back later no longer pauses or ends a game** (security review finding FLD-1, High; decisions SR-20 to SR-26, RFC 0001 Amendment 23; `core/src/replay.c`, `firmware/esp32/main/radio/radio_protocol.c`, `field_link.c`, `main/modem/modem.c`, `simulator/atalaia_sim/replay.py` and `field_controller.py`, `app/lib/radio/replay.dart` and `app/lib/controller/field_controller.dart`, `protocol/vectors/replay_window.json`): the receivers kept only a 30-second (box, modem) or 60-second (controllers) table of the packets they had seen, and refused a *lower* epoch only, so anyone with an SDR who recorded the controller's CMD PAUSE or END, or a TIME, could play it back after the table forgot it, in the same epoch, and the boxes obeyed with no key. RFC 0001 promised a per-source replay window that did not exist. Now every receiver (the Field Box, the USB modem, the Pi controller and the phone controller) keeps one per source: the highest `epoch << 16 | seq` and a 64-bit bitmap, applied only to a packet that authenticated; a counter already seen or 64 or more below the highest is dropped, however long after. A box also refuses a game-state CMD (start, pause, resume, end) or a TIME older than the last one of its kind it applied, which closes the gap of a recording of a frame it never heard. The per-source table grew from 32 to 64 entries for a field of 30 boxes (the Field Box image leaves 193,497 bytes of internal RAM for the heap instead of 194,049; the old duplicate tables are gone). **No wire change**: boxes and controllers from before interoperate, and a box from before stays exposed until it is updated. Tests: the shared vectors in C, Python and Dart (11 cases each), a host test that replays a CMD after 31 s and is refused, one that replays an END the box never heard live after 64 newer packets, the stale CMD and TIME rules, 40 sources with their own windows, the modem and both controllers refusing a late copy. The window is in RAM, so a box that just rebooted accepts the first packet of each source (RFC 0001 Amendment 23, "Left open").
- **A box whose epoch store was lost no longer reuses its nonces** (security review finding FLD-10; decision SR-27, RFC 0001 Amendment 23 item 5; `firmware/esp32/main/radio/radio_protocol.c` `atalaia_radio_proto_boot_epoch`, `radio_nvs_esp.c`): a box that resumed its stored game but had no epoch record for the game key (the store erased or replaced, the key kept) restarted at epoch 1 under the same key and repeated the nonces of its earlier life, which breaks AES-CCM (two packets under one nonce and key). It now draws an epoch in 4096 to 61439 from the hardware random generator, stores it before its first packet and goes on from there; with no random source it stays silent. A fresh join still starts at 1. The cost was that receivers that knew the box at a low epoch did not follow it until a controller named its epoch or the game was joined again (the GM's remedy for a reflashed box); see the next entry.
- **A box at a random high epoch is found by every receiver and named by the controller; the controller's own epoch cannot repeat after losing its record** (follow-up of the two entries above; decisions SR-110 to SR-119, RFC 0001 Amendment 24; `firmware/esp32/main/radio/radio_protocol.c` `deep_search` and `set_source_epoch`, `field_link.c` CMD `peer`, `simulator/atalaia_sim/peer_epoch.py`, `field_controller.py`, `controller/atalaia_controller/engine.py`, `store.py` `next_tx_epoch`, `app/lib/controller/peer_epoch.dart`, `field_controller.dart`, `controller_radio.dart` `nextTxEpoch`, `protocol/vectors/peer_epoch.json`, `tx_epoch.json`, `cmd.json`): a box that lost its epoch store took an epoch in 4096..61439 (SR-27) and nobody heard it again, because receivers follow a source only 16 epochs above the last they heard (256 for a HELLO or TIME) and nothing named the new epoch. Now (1) every receiver (box, modem, pod) that cannot open a HELLO or TIME tries it against the rest of the range, 2048 epochs per search of the existing budget (a source every 2 s, 20 a minute), so such a box or controller is found within a minute or two with nobody's help; (2) a controller that hears a box more than 16 epochs above the last it knew, or a known box's HELLO at a higher epoch, tells its modem (`SET_PEER_EPOCH`) and floods **CMD opcode 11 `peer`** (`{"src", "epoch"}`, sent again after 4 s and 10 s; the leader sends, a standby tells its modem only) so the boxes follow at once, on both the Pi and the phone controller (`protocol/vectors/peer_epoch.json`: the rule and the repeats, in Python and Dart); (3) a receiver's epoch for a source **only ever goes up**: `SET_PEER_EPOCH` and CMD `peer` ignore a lower or equal epoch, and the controllers never resend a lower one to a reconnected modem. And the controller's own epoch (the other half of FLD-10): the Pi and the phone took epoch 1 for a resumed game with no record of its key (the record lost, or pushed out of the 8 kept), repeating the nonces of its earlier life, and the Pi after a database restored from an older backup went on from the older epoch; now such a game starts at a random epoch in 4096..61439 as a box does (`protocol/vectors/tx_epoch.json`, both controllers), and the Pi keeps the list in a synced file beside the database (`<db>.epochs`) and goes on above the higher of the two. No frame changes; an old box ignores CMD 11 (and does not deep-search). Not built, with the reasons in Amendment 24 item 5: a persisted replay window across a reboot (SR-26). Tests: C deep-search, never-back and CMD `peer` host tests, the modem finding a source at epoch 41000 by HELLOs, the two vector files and the controllers' use of them in Python and Dart, and the Pi's lost, restored and deleted database cases.
- **A second loss of the epoch store cannot draw a lower epoch; a controller that has been on the air keeps its node id; a Wi-Fi prop is told CMD peer** (the three gaps Amendment 24's report left; decisions EP-1 to EP-12, RFC 0001 Amendment 25; `firmware/esp32/main/radio/radio_protocol.c` `floor_read`/`floor_raise`, `radio_nvs_esp.c`, `controller/atalaia_controller/store.py` `next_tx_epoch`, `engine.py` `_controller_ids_on_air`, `prop_link.py`, `simulator/atalaia_sim/field_controller.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_radio.dart` `nextTxEpoch`, `controller_engine.dart`, `controller_props.dart`, `app/lib/radio/pod_radio_link.dart`, `app/lib/prop/prop_session.dart`, `protocol/vectors/tx_epoch.json`): (1) the Pi's prop link dropped CMD opcode 11 on purpose and the phone controller's sent it as an unnamed `cmd_11`; both now send it to every prop as `cmd` `peer` with `{src, epoch}`, the prop gives it to its pod's receiver (SET_PEER_EPOCH, never down, sent again after each configuration of the pod), and a CMD 11 the pod hands its phone on the radio is taken the same way; (2) a lost store's random epoch was drawn in 4096..61439 with no memory, so a second loss could land below the first (receivers never lower a source: deaf for good) or on an epoch the first life used (a repeated nonce): every node that draws now keeps the highest epoch it ever used with any key apart from the per-key record (the box: its own NVS entry `epfloor`, read and written by two new optional NVS ops; the Pi: the setting `tx_epoch_floor` and the epoch file's `floor`; the phone: the same setting), and the draw is in `[max(4096, floor + 1), 61439]`, with no room left meaning no transmission (box) or a refusal (controller); (3) a former leader that lost its database and its epoch file and was joined again as a standby under its old node id would repeat its nonces (epoch 1 under a key it had used) and be deaf to everyone who heard it: a controller's join is now never issued under the id of a controller that has been on the air in the game (the issuer of the join, or any whose TIME or CLAIM was heard, kept per game), not even as "the same device"; it gets a new id. A bench build of the box gets `@epochwipe` (and `@epochwipe all`) to make a lost store on a board, and the box's "epoch store lost" log line no longer says controllers will not hear it. Left, accepted: every record of a node lost together (a box's NVS with its key, a Pi's database and file) draws as before. Tests: C host tests (a second loss above the first in 100 seeds, a floor with no room, an unreadable or unwritable floor, the blob's epochs as the floor), the shared vectors in Python and Dart with floor cases, the Pi's and the phone's second losses, the former-leader id refusals in both engines (and across a restart), the Wi-Fi forward in both controllers, and the prop's pod hearing a controller at epoch 3000 only after the CMD peer (host pod and modem). Bench checks that only boards can do: [docs/runbooks/epoch-recovery-bench.md](docs/runbooks/epoch-recovery-bench.md).

#### Fixed

- **After an MQTT reconnect the broker keeps the newest score and node state, not an older one republished late** (decision MQF-2; `controller/atalaia_controller/mqtt.py` `_publish`, `_on_connect`): on reconnect the bridge copied its last retained payloads under its lock but published them after releasing it, so a score that changed between the copy and the republish went out first and the older republished one overwrote it on the broker (scoreboards subscribing later saw the old score until the next change). Storing and sending a retained payload, and the whole republish, now happen under the lock, in one order. Test: `RepublishOrderTests` in `controller/tests/test_mqtt.py` forces that interleaving with a recorded client (fails before the fix). The flaky `test_retained_topics_republished_after_a_broker_restart` (failed in the merge queue under load, 5 of 30 runs on a loaded machine) was a test race, not this bug: `wait_connected` returns once the republish is queued, and a subscriber faster than the bridge gets the score live, with the retain flag 0. The test now first sees the score with one subscriber, then checks the retain flag with a second; `test_cmd_topics` waits for the bridge's subscription the same way (decision MQF-1). 0 failures in 90 runs of it under CPU load (and 30 runs of the whole file).

- **A controller that lost its epoch record twice or three times can no longer be refused by bad luck, and the draw keeps off the epochs a stale database backup forgot** (decision EPW-1; `controller/atalaia_controller/store.py` `epoch_lost_bounds`, `epoch_file_seen`; `app/lib/controller/controller_radio.dart` `epochLostBounds`; `protocol/vectors/tx_epoch.json` `lost_lo`/`lost_hi`; RFC 0001 Amendment 25 note with the options and numbers): the draw of a lost epoch record was uniform over everything above the floor up to 61439, and each draw becomes the next floor, so the room halved at every loss and a draw near the top left none ("none is left above them" after two or three losses, about 2 runs in 200 of the five-loss store test). With a floor it is now uniform in the lower half of what is left above floor + 1024, so each loss takes at most half and 15 fit in the worst case; epochs used since an old database backup (contiguous above its floor) are skipped up to 1024 of them, 8192 when the Pi's epoch file is missing although it was written before (the database may be a backup; warning logged). With no record at all the draw is still over the whole range. A floor is still written before an epoch is used, and the epoch never goes below or onto one used under an intact floor. Tests: bounds, stale-floor, missing-file, collision-odds and worst-case-run tests with injected randomness on the Pi, the same bounds on the phone, and the shared vectors. The Field Box's draw follows in the next entry.

- **The Field Box draws a lost epoch record's epoch the same way, so repeated NVS losses cannot exhaust its range either** (decisions EPW-2, EPW-3; `firmware/esp32/main/radio/radio_protocol.c` `atalaia_radio_epoch_lost_bounds`, `radio_protocol.h` `ATALAIA_EPOCH_LOST_MARGIN`; `protocol/vectors/tx_epoch.json`; `docs/runbooks/epoch-recovery-bench.md`): the box drew uniformly over everything above its floor entry, so its room halved at every loss as the controllers' did. With a floor it now draws uniformly in the lower half of the room above `max(4096, floor + 1 + 1024)` (the margin gives way near the top; only floor 61439 is refused, the box then does not transmit as before), with no floor over the whole range. The box has no wider margin for a missing file: its floor is its own NVS entry, written before use and never restored from a backup. Tests: the host tests take the shared vectors through a generated header (`tools/protogen.py` writes `firmware/esp32/test/tx_epoch_vectors.h`; each case's range, then the boot against it with 40 random sources) and a run of repeated losses that must fit at least 10 times. The modem and the I/O pod draw no epoch (the controller behind them sets it), so nothing changes there.

- **The pentest stack's field container reports healthy** (decision PT-H; `tools/pentest_stack.py`, `docs/runbooks/pentest-stack.md`): the field image is built on the live demo's, so it inherited that image's HEALTHCHECK on `:8001/demo/health`, which the pentest controller does not serve, and the container was always `unhealthy`. The Compose `field` service now has its own healthcheck on the controller's `GET /healthz` (plain HTTP 8080 inside the container), like the fake provider's.

#### Added

- **A disposable stack for an authorised, authenticated security test** (`tools/pentest_stack.py`, `tools/pentest/` (`fake_idp.py`, `field_runner.py`, `Dockerfile.field`), `docs/runbooks/pentest-stack.md`): `python3 tools/pentest_stack.py up` builds today's code and starts, on 127.0.0.1 only, a hosted hub with two fields (`campo-a.hub.test`, `campo-b.hub.test`; Caddy's own CA, `ATALAIA_OIDC_RELAY=on`), a fake OpenID provider that answers as Google and as Microsoft (the provider host names are network aliases and the server trusts its CA, so the server's code is unchanged; the sign-in subject is chosen with `login_subject=`), throwaway accounts made through the real paths (`create-admin`, registration, TOTP enrolment, role grants: server administrator, admin, GM, marshal and player, plus one social-only account) and a field controller with HTTPS, its field CA, Mosquitto over TLS, a simulated radio with three boxes, a demo game and site sync to campo-a. `status`, `reset-server` (or one field), `reset-controller` and `down --yes` operate it. The secrets and state stay outside the repository (`~/atalaia-pentest`), the credentials file is mode 0600, and no real provider credential is ever configured.
- **The antenna's gain is a build option** (`tools/docker_fw.py build|bench|modem|pod|wokwi --antenna-dbi=N`, 0 to 20 dBi, 2 when left out; Kconfig `ATALAIA_RADIO_ANTENNA_GAIN_DBI`): a board with a higher-gain antenna, such as a 10 dBi whip, is built with that gain and the firmware lowers its conducted power by it, so the radiated power stays within the sub-band's limit. Before, the gain could only be changed in menuconfig. Firmware guide (en, pt), the Field Box build guides and the firmware README.
- **Follow-ups of the 2026-10-30 security and privacy work** (decisions FU30-1 to FU30-19):
  - **`preview.sh up` and `update` install the preview's privacy notice themselves** (`server/preview/preview.sh` `install_notice`, `preview.sh notice`; `docs/runbooks/preview-server.md`; `tools/server_preview_check.py` runs it in a scratch folder): the files of `ATALAIA_PRIVACY_NOTICE_VERSION` from `server/preview/privacy-notice/` go into the folder the server reads, so an update no longer drops the notice, and a version whose Portuguese or English file is missing stops `up` before anything is built.
  - **The live demo's proxy sends `Strict-Transport-Security`** on every answer to a request that came over https per `X-Forwarded-Proto` (`server/preview/demo/field/proxy.py`; test in `tools/docker_server.py preview-demo-unit`). Read-only: nothing is stored.
  - **The phone controller catches up with the Pi's security review** (SR-55 to SR-57): signed-mode revocation fails closed (`app/lib/controller/controller_signed_modes.dart`: `revoked.json` fetched again before every install and upload, a missing, unreadable or malformed list installs nothing, the last good list is kept in the store across restarts, `revocation_max_age_days` for an offline phone, `min_versions` as a floor per mode; both settings are on the *Mode packages* screen in four languages), and the staff ATAK feed can use TLS with a pinned CA (`staff_tls`, `staff_tls_ca`, `staff_tls_cert`, `staff_tls_key` as PEM text, `staff_tls_name`; port 8089; TLS 1.2 or later; `app/lib/controller/controller_atak.dart`, the site screen). The shared contract cases gain two floor cases (`mode_min_versions`); revocation is tested on the phone with the Pi's own cases (`app/test/controller/controller_modes_test.dart`).
  - **Team results without player ids are kept 365 days on the controllers too** (SR-88; store schema 19 `game_results` on the Pi, the same in the phone's check-in document): written when the purge drops a game at 90 days, no player id, no member list, no squad; `GET /api/v1/game-results` (GM or read token, read-only). A shared contract case runs it on both (the Pi's `Controller` now takes a `wall` clock, so a case moves a game's dates too). The privacy notice templates' retention row now says "field controller and site server".

- **The privacy notice templates state the legal positions the maintainer's lawyer approved on 2026-10-30, and there is a DPIA template** (decisions LEG-60 onwards; `docs/templates/privacy-notice-en.md`, `privacy-notice-pt.md`, `dpia-template.md`, `dpia-template-pt.md`, `docs/README.md`): the notice's [LAWYER] marks the review settled are replaced by text, still a template for each field to fill in. Accounts and registration are on contract (Art. 6(1)(b)) with a notice-shown record and a minimum age of 16 by tick box; the APD card is checked at check-in, the number erased once the check-in is confirmed and a verdict snapshot kept; violations and bans rest on legitimate interests, a ban ends after 12 months by default (never after the chronograph retention), "repeat offender" is advisory and plain warnings do not count, contesting goes through the field's contact; the field is the controller, the hub operator its processor, and the fields joint controllers for the sharing step only (the essence of the Art. 26 terms); transfers in a table (EU data centres only, no transfer by the field to sign-in providers, the map services including Overpass, a setting to turn map fetching off). The maintainer decided the rest: no data protection officer (Art. 37(1) reasoning), position sharing by consent as a separate unticked opt-in at check-in that never blocks play, and game history (events, scores, nodes) kept 90 days on controller and server, with team and squad results without player ids kept 365 days. Part D now lists what is settled and what stays open (erasure of signed readings, the bases for NFC tags, game history and clubs). The new DPIA template (English and Portuguese) covers the violations register, cross-field sharing and positions, with the legitimate interest assessment and a risk table.
- **The card check at check-in: the field must compare the physical membership card with what the player typed** (maintainer decision 2026-10-30, consistent with the lawyer-approved position; decisions LEG-1 to LEG-11; `protocol/apd.md` "The card check", `controller/atalaia_controller/checkin.py`, `store.py` (schema 18), `app/lib/controller/controller_checkin.dart`, `app/lib/marshal/chrono_queue.dart`, `controller/console/src/components/checkin-panel.js`, `server/atalaia_server/services/registration.py`): when the field's rule has `checkin`, a self check-in (and a ticket scan) stays `pending` until a marshal or the GM ticks **card seen, matches** (`federation_seen: true`), even with a valid verdict and `confirm = "auto"`. The tick records the staff id and the time, **stores the verdict** ("valid on 2027-01-15"; the verdict used to be never stored) and **erases the membership number** on the controller; the APD, the expiry, the stored verdict and who checked it stay for the normal check-in retention. A marshal override without the card stays possible, is logged with its reason (`no_card`) and never the number. The site server erases its copy of the number when the game's outcome arrives or the game ends. The check-in CSV gains `card_checked_by` and `card_checked_at`. Legal basis in the docs: Art. 6(1)(c) GDPR for fields in Portugal, Art. 6(1)(f) elsewhere. Pi and phone controllers share the contract cases of `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`; the console's Check-in tab, the app's marshal queue and the en/pt manuals follow, and the player-facing texts explaining why the card is checked are in four languages.
- **Position sharing is a separate, unticked choice at check-in** (maintainer decision 2026-10-30, Art. 7(4) GDPR; decisions LEG-12 to LEG-16; `protocol/checkin.md` "Sharing the position", `controller/atalaia_controller/positions.py`, `app/lib/checkin/checkin_screen.dart`, `app/lib/player/player_screens.dart`): the check-in form (app, console hand check-in) asks "Share my position with the game during this game", with who sees it (squad or team as the mode allows, marshals and the GM, the remote GM only when the field turns that on). Declining blocks neither the check-in nor the game; the choice is recorded on the check-in with its time and the notice version; the player can switch it off or on at any time (`PUT /api/v1/checkin/me/position-sharing`), and `POST /api/v1/positions` is refused (409 `position_sharing_off`) without it. The app's position switch is now this same choice, still changeable during the game.

- **The app can make an account, after showing the privacy notice** (decisions LEG-*, S17-16 unblocked; `app/lib/site/site_screens.dart` `_signupStep`, `site_client.dart` `SiteSession.register`, `site_auth.dart`, `app/test/site/signup_test.dart`): the login step has *Create an account* (username, optional email, password; the server's `POST /auth/register`, then the login) and the social buttons make an account on a first login as before. Both need the notice opened first (the box *I have read the privacy notice* stays closed until then), that box ticked and *I am 16 or older* ticked; with no notice published the sign-up is closed. The age refusal, in the four languages, says younger players check in at the field without an account, under the field's rules for parents or guardians.
- **A field accepts the cross-field sharing terms before it shares chrono readings** (decisions LEG-40 to LEG-47, hosted mode and sharing; `server/atalaia_server/sharing_terms.py`, `api/fields.py`, `chrono_registry.py`, `hub_share.py`, migration `0030_sharing_terms`, the staff settings page, `docs/templates/sharing-terms.md` and `-pt.md`): each field is the controller of its players' data, the hub operator its processor, and the fields that share are joint controllers for that step only (Art. 26). Switching sharing on needs the field's administrator to accept the current terms on the settings page (the API: `sharing_terms_accepted`; the version, the date and the account are recorded, and the audit log has the version); without it the switch is refused (`409 sharing_terms_required`). When the terms' version changes (`ATALAIA_SHARING_TERMS_VERSION`) the field is asked again and its sharing is suspended until it accepts. A field that already shared before this release has no acceptance, so its sharing waits for one. In four languages.
- **The DPA template says who is who, and has a pt-PT version** (decisions LEG-40, LEG-44, LEG-46; `docs/templates/dpa-template.md`, new `dpa-template-pt.md`): the field is the controller, the hub operator the processor, joint controllers for the sharing step only; EU/EEA data centres only for the hosting, the backups and every sub-processor, with a Chapter V safeguard recorded in the annex for anything else; the [LAWYER] marks the legal review answered are gone.
- **The hosted go-live checklist** (decision LEG-45; `server/docs/DEPLOY.md` "Hosted mode", `server/README.md`): who is responsible for what, and the six conditions before any field is offered hosted mode (a signed DPA per field, the sharing terms, every sub-processor in the annex and in the EU, a filled-in privacy notice, the operator's own papers if it is a natural person, a proof on a real server).
- **The field can turn the console's map fetching off** (decisions LEG-50 to LEG-53; `controller/atalaia_controller/engine.py`, `api.py`, `controller/console/src/components/map-fetching-settings.js`, `field-map.js`, the app's `controller_engine.dart`, `controller_api.dart`, `console_files.dart`): *Settings*, *Map fetching* (or `PUT /api/v1/settings/map-fetching`) stops *Load the field from a map* from reaching OpenStreetMap, Overpass, Nominatim and the aerial imagery services. Off, both controllers (the Pi and the phone) serve that page with the strict headers, so the browser cannot reach any of them, and the page and the Field tab say so in pt, en, de and zh-tw. On by default.
- **The preview server has a privacy notice of its own** (finding LIVE-2 of the 2026-10-30 security and privacy review; `server/preview/privacy-notice/`, `docs/runbooks/preview-server.md`, `REUSE.toml`): tests.atalaia-project.link let anyone register while `/privacy` answered 404. The notice, in pt-PT and English and in the maintainer's name as controller, says what the preview keeps (feedback and its optional contact, accounts, Google and Microsoft sign-in, sessions, the security log), on what basis and for how long, Cloudflare as processor, the cookies, the rights and the CNPD; it is deployed as version `preview-2026-10-30`.
- **One social login callback for a whole hosted hub** (decisions LG-14 to LG-19, ADR-P4-13 built; `server/atalaia_server/api/oidc_relay.py`, `oidc.py`, `tenancy.py`, `api/login_methods.py`, `Caddyfile.hosted`, `relay-on.caddy`, `relay-off.caddy`, `docker-compose.hosted.yml`; tests `tests/test_oidc_relay.py`). Opt-in with `ATALAIA_OIDC_RELAY=on` (hosted mode only; off by default, so a single-field server such as the preview and a hub that leaves it off keep each field's own callback). The operator registers **one** redirect address per provider, `https://<hub domain>/api/v1/auth/oidc/callback`, instead of one per field and custom host name. The `state` a field sends the provider is signed by the hub (HMAC, a key of its own) and names the field; the hub's callback checks it and its ten-minute expiry, looks the field up in the tenant registry (unknown or suspended: one plain 400 page, nothing forwarded) and answers a `303` to the field's own callback on a host read from the registry, never from the request. The field re-checks the state is its own and for that provider, then finishes the login as before (binding cookie, one-use login row, code exchange, ID token, nonce), so the binding to the browser, single use, the PKCE verifier and the per-field database role are untouched; the app's return to `/app/oidc` works through the relay. `docs/DEPLOY.md` ("Social login") and `server/README.md` tell the operator what to register; the hub's own name needs a DNS record and `relay-on.caddy` (Compose picks it by the same setting).
- **A plan for a hardened release image** (security review finding FLD-2/FLD-3, decision SR-42): `firmware/esp32/docs/RELEASE_HARDENING.md` describes a release sdkconfig with flash encryption, NVS encryption, secure boot v2 and the eFuse anti-rollback floor (FU3-8), what each means for USB re-flashing, the bench and OTA, the order of work, and the questions only the maintainer can answer. Nothing is built or burned; burning eFuses is irreversible.
- Tests for the review's findings: eight sandbox-escape cases for string patterns and `string.rep` (hostile patterns in `find`, `match`, `gsub`, `gmatch`, inside a `pcall` loop, a quadratic plain search, `rep` limits, and that ordinary patterns still work), a depth-limit test for skipped CBOR values (`core/tests/test_msg_codec.c`) and a PIN-gate test for the serial commands (`firmware/esp32/test/test_admin_pin.c`).
- **GDPR review, code part: the audit log's addresses, inactive accounts, copies at other fields, a restore that does not bring back the erased, the chrono repeat notice and ban end, and sign-up needs a published notice** (decisions SR-70 to SR-89; migration `0028_privacy_retention` (chain it after srsrv's); `server/atalaia_server/erasure_ledger.py`, `erasure_replay.py`, `personal_data.py`, `chrono_registry.py`, `web/staff_i18n_chrono.py`, `web/static/staff/chrono.js`, `backup.sh`, `restore.sh`, `docker-compose.yml`, `controller/atalaia_controller/sync_format.py`, `sync_client.py`, `checkin.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_sync*.dart`, `controller_checkin.dart`, `protocol/sync.md`, `protocol/vectors/sync.json`): an audit entry loses its network address after `ATALAIA_AUDIT_IP_DAYS` (180, 90-365) and keeps the row (PRIV-6); an account unused for `ATALAIA_INACTIVE_ACCOUNT_MONTHS` (24) is warned (account page, `GET /me` `inactivity`) and anonymised 30 days later through the erasure path, off with `off` (PRIV-7); a person's erasure or a withdrawn chrono-sharing consent reaches other fields' controllers in the schedule answer (`copies_revoked`, `?revocations=1`), and the Pi and the phone controller drop the copies they pulled, game started or not (PRIV-5, Art. 17(2)); erasures are appended as hashes to a ledger in a Docker volume outside the database, copied beside the backups, merged and applied again by `restore.sh` (`erasure-ledger apply`; hosted per field) (PRIV-4); the "repeat offender" mark is an advisory notice on the new staff page `/staff/fields/<field>/chrono` (pt, en, de, zh-tw), a plain warning never counts (a fixed rule, approved with the lawyer's brief on 2026-10-30), and a "ban" ends (`chrono_ban_days`, 12 months by default, never past the chrono retention) (PRIV-2); new accounts are refused with `privacy_notice_unpublished` while no privacy notice is published (`ATALAIA_ALLOW_SIGNUP_WITHOUT_NOTICE` lifts it for development), with a start-up warning, and a hosted hub on a `template-*` notice version warns at start (LIVE-2). The project page's footer links the notice template in all four languages (PRIV-13) and the notice template has a draft on the host's logs, marked as pending a lawyer (PRIV-11; the minors paragraph is the approved one, 16, LEG-61). Game history (events, scores, nodes, scheduled games) now keeps **90 days by default** with a player id, and the team results without one (`game_results`, `GET .../history/results`) 365 days after the game, on the site server and both controllers, by the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-30 (was 730 and 365; the range stays 30-3653 on controllers and 30-730 on the server; RET-3 unchanged; existing stored settings keep their value). Tests: the server's `test_privacy_review.py`, the Pi's `test_sync_schedule.py`, the phone's `controller_sync_test.dart`, the shared vectors (24 valid, 86 invalid answers).

- **Find boxes' sweeps are capped per rolling hour on channels with little air time** (decision RL-9, approved by the maintainer on 2026-10-30; `simulator/atalaia_sim/lobby.py` `Inviter.sweep_refusal`/`sweep`, `controller/atalaia_controller/reinvite.py`, the phone's `controller_boxes.dart`, the console's Look for boxes result, `docs/review/state-load-results.md` section 6): a sweep says a MOVE twice on each of the last four channels, 185 ms of air a channel on that channel's sub-band, and the once-a-minute limit let a GM pressing the button spend a 1 % sub-band's whole 36 s (123 %) or a 0.1 % band's many times over. Now, on a sub-band under 10 % duty, the sweeps may use 5 % of that sub-band's budget over a rolling hour (1.8 s on 1 %: two sweeps of four channels or nine of one; none on 0.1 %, where one channel alone is 0.185 s of 0.18 s); the 10 % default sub-band keeps only the once-a-minute limit (worst case 12 % of its budget, only between games). 5 % is below what the repeats of a typical hour already cost the controller on 1 % (2.3 s), see the results note. A refused sweep is never silent: `POST /api/v1/boxes/discover` answers `"sweep": []` with `"sweep_refused": {"reason": "cap", "band", "duty_pct", "share_pct", "limit_ms", "used_ms", "cost_ms", "retry_after_s"}` (null when there is no refusal; `retry_after_s` null when it never fits), and the console says it under the button in Portuguese, English, German and Traditional Chinese with the minutes to the next sweep. Same arithmetic in both controllers (integer microseconds), shared case *S7c Find boxes: the hourly cap on a 1 % channel refuses the fifth sweep and says when the next is possible* (the contract runners learned `"times"` on `advance_ms`); `atalaia_sim.state_load.sweep_cap` and its test lock the per-sub-band numbers. Docs: the protocol note, RFC 0001, the controller README, both game master manuals, the field-test and bench runbooks. The record is in memory (a restart forgets it); the modem's duty-cycle guard is unchanged.
- **The relay and air-time model now includes the state changes' TIME repeats, MOVE and the sweep** (decisions RL-1 to RL-8; `simulator/atalaia_sim/relay_study.py` `Engine(state_changes=...)` and `Policy.time_repeat_gaps_ms`, new `simulator/atalaia_sim/state_load.py`, `simulator/tests/test_state_load.py`, `docs/review/state-load-results.md`, `docs/runbooks/radio-field-test.md`): since RB-8 a start, pause or resume is CMD + TIME and four more TIMEs, and each box relays each one; the study never had them. For a 20-box field on the default 10 % sub-band a state change costs the controller 0.47 s of air (0.33 s of it the repeats) and a box 0.04 s; a typical hour (7 changes) adds 2.2 s to the controller (+0.06 point of the hour) and 0.31 s to a box (+0.01 point), nothing refused; every limit and margin the documents state still holds, so the controller is unchanged. On a 1 % sub-band the controller's margin goes from 0.11 to 0.04 point (past the limit with about ten pauses an hour; the modem's guard refuses the extra frame), and a sweep once a minute can spend a small sub-band: both written up with the mitigations considered. The 20-box field test plan measures the repeats, the start latency and a channel change plus sweep.
- **The console says what a change of channel does to the free boxes** (decision RB-13, RFC 0001 Amendment 19; `controller/console/src/components/radio-settings.js`, `box-invites.js`, `app.js`; `atalaia_controller/reinvite.py` and `engine.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_boxes.dart` and `controller_channel.dart`): the radio settings and the `radio` stream message carry `move` (`telling` with the MOVEs said so far of three, then `moved`, null before a change), on both controllers and in a shared contract case (`S7c MOVE progress`). The channel panel shows *Telling the free boxes on the old channel ... said n of 3 times* while the MOVEs repeat (about 5 s) and then *The controller has moved to ...*; *Look for boxes* (a button in the panel between games, and the invite dialog's) shows which earlier channels were swept (*Also looked on the earlier channels: 869.525 MHz*), how many boxes answered since, and a help line: a box still missing needs to be adopted again or be switched on, free and near during the next change. Four languages; the shell's file list is unchanged, so the service worker's cache name stays.
- **Browser tests of the live demo's player view** (decision DEMO-20; `tools/demo_player_browser.py`, `tools/dev/demo-browser/Dockerfile`, `tools/server_preview_demo.py`, `docs/runbooks/preview-server.md`): `python3 tools/docker_server.py preview-demo-unit` now also parses every demo JavaScript file (the duplicate `const` of DEMO-18 would fail here) and drives `/player/` in headless Chromium against stand-in `/player/data.json` shaped as `proxy.py` makes it: the open field, the three-storey building (floor buttons, each floor's plan and beacons), beacon squares, GPS circles and hollow rings, numeric and named team colours, the waiting state and the four languages (same keys in every text table); about five seconds.
- **Indoor beacon positions on iPhone** (decisions IOSB-1 to IOSB-8; `app/ios/Runner/BeaconScanPlugin.swift`, `AppDelegate.swift`, `LocationPlugin.swift`, `app/lib/player/player_screens.dart`): a Swift plugin on the same channels and message shapes as Android's `BeaconScanPlugin.kt` (`org.atalaia.app/beacons` and `/sightings`), over CoreLocation: one `startRangingBeacons(satisfying:)` constraint per registered UUID (a Field Box's per-field UUID and the tags' alike), the one-second readings grouped into the game's beacon window, one average signal strength per beacon, the same -95 dBm floor and strongest-beacon rule in the shared Dart code. It needs only the location *When in use* (no Bluetooth permission, no *Always*; the explain-first dialog and *Continue* / *Not now* are as on Android, with iPhone texts in pt, en, de and zh-Hant, and the location usage text names the beacons) and ranges only while the app is on the screen: a locked phone falls back to GPS and the last floor, which the player screen says. Dart tests with a fake channel cover the iOS path; **the Swift has not been compiled or run** (no Mac here: CI's `ios-build` and the maintainer's iPhone are the first check; checklist in `docs/runbooks/ios-beacons-iphone.md`).

- **A standby controller serves the same box-beacon registry after a failover** (decision BBC-20, RFC 0001 Amendment 21; `simulator/atalaia_sim/join.py`, `app/lib/protocol/join_payload.dart`, `controller/atalaia_controller/box_beacons.py` and `engine.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_box_beacons.dart` and `controller_engine.dart`, `protocol/vectors/join.json` `box_beacons`, `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`): the GM's box-beacon choices (the field switch, the boxes disabled, the rooms given) were settings of the primary only, so after a failover the registry served to phones and the join's key 19 could differ from what the GM had set. The standby's join is what goes from a primary to its standby, so it now carries them: the standby reads key 19, and a new optional join key 21, `box_beacons` (a standby's payload only, at most 200 bytes), holds the disabled nodes and the rooms. A join made again for the game and key a standby already follows is a refresh of those settings and the box language instead of a conflict (so later choices can reach it). Tests: a failover with the choices changed on the primary in both controllers (`controller/tests/test_join.py`, `app/test/controller/controller_box_beacons_test.dart`), the codecs' `box_beacons` vectors (18 refused, 3 round-tripped) and the shared contract case "S7 failover: a standby takes the primary's box-beacon choices". A box's place on the field view was still each controller's own then; the next entry carries it too.

- **A console open on a standby controller moves the boxes' markers live** (decision BBC-30, follow-up to BBC-25..29; `controller/atalaia_controller/box_beacons.py` `apply_places` and `engine.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_box_beacons.dart` `applyBoxPlaces` and `controller_engine.dart`, `protocol/box-beacon.md` section 7, `controller/README.md`): applying the places of a join (key 22) was silent on the stream, so a console already open on the standby's plan kept the old markers until it was reloaded. Now both controllers send the `position` message a GM placing the box sends (`node_id`, `x`, `y`, `level`) for every box whose place or level changed, at the first join (after the `game` message) and at every refresh, and none for a box left where it was. No change to the join, the messages' shape or the boxes. Tests: the contract case "S7 failover: a standby takes the primary's box places from its join" now follows a GM stream through the first join, a move, a level change, the same places again and a join without key 22 (Python and Dart), a unit test of the changed-only rule (`controller/tests/test_join.py`), and a browser test where a console open on a standby's plan sees the marker move with no reload.
- **A standby controller also knows where the boxes are placed** (decision BBC-25, reverses BBC-24; RFC 0001 Amendment 22; `simulator/atalaia_sim/join.py`, `app/lib/protocol/join_payload.dart`, `controller/atalaia_controller/box_beacons.py` and `engine.py`, `app/lib/controller/controller_box_beacons.dart` and `controller_engine.dart`, `protocol/box-beacon.md` section 7, `protocol/join.md`, `protocol/vectors/join.json` `box_places`, `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`, the console's *Join* text in four languages): after a failover the box beacons are how players are placed indoors, and the standby only listed a box once it had heard it and the GM had placed it there too. The channel is a new optional join key, 22 `box_places`, in the standby's join (the one channel a primary has to its standby; a standby need not have a network, so a fetch was out, and key 21's 200 bytes do not hold 20 places as text): one byte string, 7 bytes a box (node id, level, x and y as 16-bit fractions of the plan), up to 28 boxes, 142 bytes for 20. The standby puts each box where the primary has it and lists it at once (no HELLO needed), so its registry is whole when it is promoted and phones pick it up by the usual registry re-read (BLE-20); a box moved on the primary reaches the standby with the join made again (the refresh of BBC-21). More boxes than fit, or a payload over 448 bytes, is refused with the size the places take, never made without them. No box firmware or core codec change, no existing vector changes. Tests: the codecs' `box_places` vectors (4 round-tripped, 12 refused, the rounding), a failover with places moved and added after the standby joined and the 29-box refusal in both controllers (`controller/tests/test_join.py`, `app/test/controller/controller_box_beacons_test.dart`), and the shared contract case.

#### Security

- **A pinned field CA is exactly one certificate** (security review APP-1, decisions SR-30 to SR-32; `app/lib/security/pem.dart`, `app/lib/checkin/field_trust.dart`, `app/lib/controller/controller_sync.dart`): the app hashed only the first certificate of the PEM the controller sent but kept and trusted the whole text, so someone on the field's Wi-Fi at the first fetch could serve `[real CA, attacker CA]`. It now refuses a bundle (or anything but one certificate) and keeps only the hashed certificate re-encoded as PEM. A pin stored with several certificates is narrowed to its first, when that is the pinned one, or forgotten. The site sync's `ca_pem` must be one certificate too. Tests: `test/checkin/checkin_logic_test.dart`, `test/controller/controller_sync_test.dart`.
- **No app-wide cleartext HTTP on Android** (security review APP-3, PD11 as the maintainer changed it, decisions SR-33 to SR-34; `app/android/app/src/main/res/xml/network_security_config.xml`, `AndroidManifest.xml`, `build.gradle.kts`): `usesCleartextTraffic` is gone; the network security config allows plain HTTP only to the loopback, the emulator's host, `192.168.4.1` and `atalaia.local` (the web-view GM console); `-Patalaia.cleartextLan=true` builds with the old, permissive policy for fields whose console has another address. The app's own `dart:io` clients were never under the flag and still pin the field CA.
- **The release APK is never signed with the debug key by accident** (security review APP-2, decisions SR-35 to SR-36; `app/android/app/build.gradle.kts`, `app/README.md` "Signing a release"): the release build type reads a keystore from `ATALAIA_RELEASE_*` environment variables or `atalaia.release.*` Gradle properties and stops with a message when there is none; `-Patalaia.release.debugSigned=true` asks for the debug key on purpose. Debug builds and `verify.sh`'s apk step are unchanged; no keystore is made or kept in the repository, and the key ceremony is written down for the maintainer.
- **The app's social-login ticket is bound to the app instance** (security review APP-4, decisions SR-37 to SR-39; `server/atalaia_server/api/login_methods.py`, `tokens.py`, migration `0026_oidc_app_challenge`, `app/lib/site/site_auth.dart`): PKCE style, the app sends the SHA-256 of a random verifier at `/auth/oidc/{provider}/start` (`app_challenge`) and presents the verifier at `/auth/oidc/complete`, so a ticket lifted from the return link is useless. The staff pages are unchanged (the binding cookie); apps from before keep working unless the operator sets `ATALAIA_OIDC_APP_REQUIRE_CHALLENGE=on` (426 `app_challenge_required`); the new app asks an older server again without the field, together with the `notice_version` retry (APF-10). Tests: `tests.test_login_methods`, `tests.test_migrations`, `test/site/site_auth_test.dart`.

#### Changed

- **Accounts rest on the contract, not on consent; sixteen to make one alone** (decisions LEG-*, the maintainer's lawyer approved on 2026-10-30; migration `0029_account_notice_records`, `server/atalaia_server/personal_data.py`, `models.py` `NoticeRecord`, `api/auth.py`, `api/me.py`, `api/login_methods.py`, the staff login page and its consent panel, the app's notice screens, docs): the `account` consent becomes a **notice record**, which version of the privacy notice was shown and when, with `age_confirmed` on the sign-up row; nothing about it can be withdrawn and erasure stays its own button. **Migration:** every old `account` consent (live or replaced, `before-0003` included) became one `migrated` notice record with the same version and time and no age confirmation, and the `account` rows left `consents`, which now holds only real consents (cross-field chrono sharing; position sharing in a game and "use my Google/Microsoft name" stay acts of the person that need nothing stored). The consent-again flow is now "shown again": `GET /api/v1/me` has `notice` (not `consent`), `POST /api/v1/me/notice` replaces `POST /api/v1/me/consents/account`, the audit action is `notice_shown_again`, the access answer and the export have `notices` (and `notice_shown_at` for `gdpr_consent_at`); the staff pages' panel and the app's banner say "read it and confirm you saw it", not "agree". **Minimum age 16:** `POST /api/v1/auth/register` and a social sign-up's start take `notice_seen` and `age_confirmed` instead of `consent` (400 `notice_required` / `age_confirmation_required`; the callback's `oidc_error` likewise); no date of birth. The staff login page has the two boxes (notice, age) beside the provider buttons.
- **CI and supply-chain hardening from the security review** (decisions SR-60 to SR-69, findings SC-1 to SC-6; `.github/workflows/*.yml`, `.github/dependabot.yml`, `controller/requirements.in` and `.txt`, `tools/release-requirements.in` and `.txt`, `firmware/esp32/docs/RELEASE_SIGNING.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `controller/README.md`): `ci.yml` now has a top-level `permissions: contents: read` (the other workflows already declared theirs; `pages.yml`'s job block gained `contents: read`); the release workflow's signing job runs in the environment `release` and, with the `version` job, fails unless the tag's commit is an ancestor of `origin/main`; every third-party action is pinned by full commit SHA with its version in a comment, the ESP-IDF image by digest (`espressif/idf:v5.3.2@sha256:5b8f58d9...`), checkouts that do not push use `persist-credentials: false`, and the signing job installs `cryptography` from `tools/release-requirements.txt` with `--require-hashes`; `controller/requirements.txt` is now a compiled, hash-pinned lock (uv, Python 3.11+, x86-64 and aarch64 wheels checked) made from the new `requirements.in`, whose cryptography floor rises from 38 to 44.0.1; Dependabot proposes weekly updates for the actions and the pip and pub files. The maintainer must still create the protected `release` environment, move the two signing secrets into it, add a `v*` tag ruleset and review the "create and approve pull requests" setting (steps in RELEASE_SIGNING.md): until the secrets are moved, a repository-level secret still reaches the job. Not run by a GitHub runner here: the workflows were linted, not executed.
- **The rest of the CI pins from the security review** (decisions SR-90 to SR-93, finding SC-3; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `registry/template/.github/workflows/registry.yml`, `tools/ci-sim-requirements.in` and `.txt`, `ci-lint-requirements.in` and `.txt`, `ci-yaml-requirements.in` and `.txt`, `tools/docker_fw.py`, `tools/dev/Dockerfile.esp32`, `.github/dependabot.yml`, `docs/community/registry.md`): every `pip install` in CI now reads a requirements file compiled by uv with PyPI hashes (`--exclude-newer 2026-10-30`) and installs it with `--require-hashes --no-deps --only-binary ":all:"`: the simulator and the game modes (PyYAML, cryptography), the lint tools (check-jsonschema, REUSE) and, for the job in the ESP-IDF container (Python 3.10), PyYAML alone. `reuse` is the exception that publishes a source archive only, so it installs with `--no-binary reuse` (the archive's hash is checked; the build backend is not pinned). The registry template's workflow pins every action by commit SHA with its version, the same as this repository's, and installs its tools from the hashed files of the Atalaia checkout at `ATALAIA_REF` (a ref older than those files cannot run it; `registry/tests/test_registry_template.py` still runs its `check-` steps as written). `tools/docker_fw.py` and `Dockerfile.esp32` use the ESP-IDF image by the digest `ci.yml` and `release.yml` use. Dependabot's `/tools` pip entry already covers the new files. Not run by a GitHub runner here: the installs were run in containers of the runners' Python versions, the workflows were linted, not executed.
- **The CBOR decoders' limits are tested on hostile input** (decision SR-94; `simulator/tests/test_cbor_limits.py`, `app/test/protocol/cbor_test.dart`): the review asked whether the Python and Dart decoders recurse without a bound or trust a count larger than the input. Neither does: both are hand-written (no CBOR library), both refuse containers nested more than 17 deep (the event envelope plus the core's 16 levels of event data) and any length or count larger than the bytes left, and every join payload, field frame, pair frame, check-in ticket, pod and radio frame goes through that one decoder per language. What was missing was a test of it: 200,000 nested openers, counts of 2^64-1 and 2^31, a count one byte too large, text and byte strings longer than the input; the shared vectors are unchanged and still pass. No decoder code changed.
- **`@joinkey` and `@update` on a box's serial console need the admin PIN in a release build** (security review finding FLD-2, decision SR-40): `@joinkey <64 hex> <PIN>` and `@update <version> <release id> <PIN>`, the PIN checked like every admin PIN (counted failures, waits) and refused while none is set. A bench build (`CONFIG_ATALAIA_SERIAL_KEYS`: `sdkconfig.bench`, the Wokwi build) takes them as before, without a PIN, so the bench scripts and BRING_UP.md work unchanged. `@adopt` was already PIN-gated for a box adopted to another field. `admin_pin_gate_serial()` in `admin_pin.c`.
- **Lua string patterns and `string.rep` are metered in the sandbox** (security review finding FLD-8, decision SR-41): a community mode could hang a box until the hardware watchdog fired with a hostile pattern (`('a'):rep(3000):find('.-.-.-.-.-b')`) or `(''):rep(1e18)`. The vendored `lstrlib.c` now calls the instruction hook by hand every 1000 matching steps (one local, marked patch; `core/third_party/README.md`), so patterns spend the node's instruction budget and end in "instruction limit exceeded"; `string.rep` refuses a result over 32 KB. Built-in modes and scenarios are unaffected (every test and the scenario vectors pass). `core/README.md` no longer lists this as a known limit.
- **Skipping an unknown CBOR value is depth-limited** (finding FLD-8): `mc_skip_value` in `core/src/cbor_int.h` recursed once per nested array or map with no limit, so a packet of `0x81` bytes could exhaust a box's stack. It now stops at 16 levels (the codecs read two or three) and refuses a count larger than the bytes left.
- **Field controller security review (FLD-4, FLD-5, FLD-7, FLD-9, SC-7; decisions SR-50 to SR-59)**:
  - **HSTS on answers served over TLS** (`guard.py` `Hsts`; LIVE-1): never on the plain-HTTP listener. `[api] trusted_proxy = true` (for the demo image behind a TLS-ending tunnel) reads `X-Forwarded-Proto`: proxied `http` gets the token refusal and a 308 to https for pages.
  - **No request body is read past a limit** (`controller/atalaia_controller/guard.py`, the phone controller's `_bodyLimit` in `app/lib/controller/controller_field_api.dart`): 64 KiB for JSON, larger only on the upload routes (a plan 4 MiB, the field from a map 6 MiB, a logo or wallpaper 4 MiB, a crest 512 KiB, a signed package 1 MB, a firmware image 4 MiB, a mode, scenario or registration list 2 MiB), judged from `Content-Length` and again while a chunked body streams, before parsing, the token and the rate limit; a WebSocket message is cut at 256 KiB. The phone judged only while streaming and allowed 1 MiB everywhere.
  - **The GM token and players' tokens are refused over plain HTTP** when HTTPS is on (`403` with `https_port`; the read token still reads on HTTP, for the scoreboard; no-token requests, the poster's flow and `GET /api/v1/controller/ca` are untouched; the controller's own loopback is trusted; `[api] http_tokens = true` turns it off). The console's login on a plain HTTP page does not send the code and shows the `https://…:8443/console/` address (new `GET /api/v1/controller/https`; en, pt, de, zh-tw). Refines PE3. The phone controller already served HTTP on loopback only.
  - **MQTT: plain 1883 on the Pi is loopback only, the field gets TLS on 8883** with the field CA (`controller/deploy/mosquitto.conf`, new `mosquitto-tls.conf`, `[mqtt] tls_export_dir`, `tls.export_for_broker`); the phone controller's broker the same (`mqtt_broker.dart` `tls`, 8883, plain on loopback; the controller screen's text in four languages); the example scoreboard takes `--cafile`.
  - **The staff ATAK feed with `players = true` can use TLS** (`[atak] staff_tls`, `staff_tls_ca`, `staff_tls_cert`, `staff_tls_key`; port 8089 by default; only the configured CA is trusted); without it a warning says the positions cross the network readable. `players = false` stays the default.
  - **Signed mode revocation fails closed** (`signed_modes.py`): with an index, every install and upload fetches `revoked.json` again; a missing, unreadable or malformed list installs nothing; a broken index no longer forgets keys seen revoked (the list is kept in the store); an offline controller goes on with the last list for `[modes] revocation_max_age_days` (30), then installs nothing signed. New `[modes] min_versions` floor per mode.
  - **The systemd unit is hardened** (`controller/deploy/atalaia-controller.service`): `NoNewPrivileges`, `ProtectSystem=strict` with `/var/lib/atalaia` writable, `ProtectHome`, `PrivateTmp`, `RestrictAddressFamilies`, `DevicePolicy=closed` with the two serial device classes, no capabilities and more; tested for its directives in `controller/tests/test_deploy.py`.
  - Docs: the controller README, `SECURITY.md`, `protocol/mode-packages.md`, the console README and the GM, quick-start and controller-setup manuals (en and pt) now open the console at `https://<controller>:8443/console/`.
- **Bomb / Rush: an explosion ends the game, the arming team winning** (decision GE-10; `modes/bomb/node.lua`, `mode.yaml` 0.3.0, `modes/bomb/tests`, `modes/README.md`, the GM and player manuals en+pt, the site's modes page): right after `bomb.exploded` and the exploded screen the script calls `game.win` for the team that armed the bomb, so the box shows EXPLODED and the controller ends the game (GE-1 to GE-6). A defuse does not end the game (Bomb's rule is unchanged); Bomb has no time limit; after a power loss in a controller-led game the box starts afresh, so no explosion; Sabotage is unchanged. Bomb with Codes loads at 54.1 KB of the Reduced box's 56 KiB test limit. New test `reboot_armed`.
- **A phone that is already sharing picks up registry changes during the game** (decision BLE-20, amending BLE-5; `controller/atalaia_controller/api.py` `BEACONS_CHANGED` and `make_route`, the app's `controller_stream.dart` and `controller_field_beacons.dart`, `app/lib/player/field_feed.dart`, `player_screens.dart`, `position/beacon_scanner.dart`, `position_reporter.dart`; vectors `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json`): a Field Box placed, moved to another level or disabled, or a tag added or deleted after a player began to share was never used by phones already in the game (BBC-8). The stream message `beacons`, which only staff got, now goes to every connection with no data; a phone that is sharing reads `GET /api/v1/beacons` again two seconds after the last one (several changes, one read; also after a reconnect's hello) and restarts its scan through the same start call only when the set of UUIDs changed, so a change of room name alone costs no radio work. A field whose first beacon appears during the game asks the player once, as at the start (*Continue* / *Not now*). No polling and no new permission; the controller (Python and Dart) and the app change, the Android and iOS plugins do not.
- **The Field Box's internal RAM budget: Wi-Fi at a join and the iBeacon fit together, never at the same time** (decisions BBC-13 to BBC-18; `firmware/esp32/sdkconfig.defaults`, `main/psram_attr.h`, `lvgl_conf/lv_conf.h`, `main/box_beacon_esp.c`, `tools/check_fw_ram.py`): found on the bench on 2026-10-29, when the box's internal RAM was gone at a game's join (Wi-Fi `ESP_ERR_NO_MEM`, then `BLE_INIT: Malloc failed` and an interrupt watchdog reset). Static internal RAM left for the heap went from 45,704 to 194,049 bytes: LVGL's 48 KB widget heap and the game's big static buffers (the preset, the box, the runner, the built-in presets' scratch, the stored-preset lists, the screen template: about 62 KB) live in PSRAM now (`ATALAIA_PSRAM_BSS`, `CONFIG_SPIRAM_ALLOW_BSS_SEG_EXTERNAL_MEMORY`; off in the Wokwi, modem and pod images), and the Bluetooth controller is sized for one advertiser. The beacon starts only with 40 KB of internal RAM free and a 12 KB block, cleans up a controller that failed half-way, retries three times and then stays off for that game, never a panic; the Wi-Fi start waits for the controller to be down; the display falls back to one draw buffer or fewer lines. A log line with the internal and DMA figures at boot, at the Wi-Fi start and around the beacon's start (`mem_monitor_log`), and CI's `esp32-build` fails when the image leaves under 120 KiB of internal RAM (`tools/check_fw_ram.py`). The beacon stays on in the Field Box image. **Not yet run on the board**: `docs/runbooks/box-beacon-bench.md` has the checklist and the figures to expect.
- **The boot splash is a build asset** (decision LOGO-6; `firmware/esp32/assets/splash.pgm`, `main/splash_logo.cmake`, `CONFIG_ATALAIA_SPLASH_LOGO`): the Atalaia tower as a plain-text 30 x 60 PGM that the build embeds, so a builder replaces one file (or points the Kconfig option at theirs) to build a box with another splash; the project's release images always carry the tower. Steps in the firmware guide (section 4, en and pt) and `firmware/esp32/README.md`.

- **A release build refuses a splash that is not the Atalaia tower** (decision LOGO-7; `tools/release_package.py`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `controller/tests/test_release_package.py`): `package` fails when `assets/splash.pgm` differs from the pinned SHA-256, the build's `sdkconfig` has another `CONFIG_ATALAIA_SPLASH_LOGO`, or the generated `splash_logo.c` is not that file's pixels; documented in CONTRIBUTING.md (Cutting a release) and `firmware/esp32/README.md`.

- **The phase state files no longer point at work that is already done** (documentation only, decision SD; `docs/phase-2-field-network/{STATE.json,STATUS.md}`, `docs/phase-3-android-players/STATE.json`, `docs/phase-4-site-server/STATUS.md`, `docs/phase-5-community/{STATE.json,STATUS.md}`): the phase 2 mode pack is built and plan phase F is next but hardware-blocked, the I/O pod relays field frames (PE24-100 to PE24-104, PE24-130), the remote ATAK relay (FU5), positions in the remote view, the remote GM page, the privacy-notice screens and the phase 3 Android controller shell are built, and phase 5's plan phase 13 (13a and 13b) and its contract cases for the mode-package endpoints exist.

- **The roadmap and the documentation brought up to date after 28 to 30 October** (documentation only; `site/i18n/{pt,en,de,zh-tw}.yaml` roadmap phases 1 to 4 and the generated pages, `README.md`, the four `docs/phase-*/STATUS.md`, `docs/architecture.md` and `.pt.md`, the game master manuals en and pt): one bullet per item for the Field Box as an iBeacon and its memory budget, the missing field logo and the replaceable splash, the status screens that go away, the channel MOVE, the repeated TIME, the console's move and sweep display and the hourly sweep cap, the projected score that follows a capture, a box's game end and a Bomb explosion ending the game, the Field Boxes in the beacon registry, the iPhone beacon plugin (compiled on CI, not tried on an iPhone), the standby's box-beacon choices and places, the app's privacy notice and consent, the social account's username, renaming and merging, the notice version on a social sign-up, the purge of anonymised accounts and the demo's three-storey building. Each line says what was tried on a real board or real provider and what was not: Google and Microsoft login against the real providers on the test server, Apple not; the radio fixes and the beacon on a phone still need their board checks. Stale lines fixed ("iOS stays on GPS", "indoor beacons on iOS need a Swift plugin", the box's field logo, `STATUS.md` of phase 3 saying iOS was not built).
- **The roadmap and the documentation brought up to date with the security review, the privacy work and the lawyer's positions** (documentation only; `site/i18n/{pt,en,de,zh-tw}.yaml` roadmap phases 1 to 5 and the generated pages, `README.md`, the five `docs/phase-*/STATUS.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `docs/architecture.md` and `.pt.md`, the game master and player manuals en and pt): one roadmap bullet per item for the radio replay window, the controller hardening (HTTPS-only tokens, MQTT TLS, fail-closed revocation), the PIN-gated serial commands and the release-hardening plan (a plan only), the card check and the position choice at check-in, the app's security review, the step-up for login changes, the site server's review, accounts on the contract with a minimum age of 16, the retention and erasure work (game history 90 days, team results 365), the sharing terms, the notice, DPA and DPIA templates, the hub's one social-login callback, the go-live checklist, the preview's notice (`preview-2026-10-31`), the map-fetching switch, a standby console's live markers and the CI pins, each said to be tested in software only, with the preview server (tests.atalaia-project.link) running it since 30 October and nothing tried on a real board or phone. SECURITY.md is reordered (scope first) and gains what has been tested where, the replay window, the site server and accounts, what the app trusts, personal data and the build. The architecture page (en, pt) describes the replay window, the card check, the position choice and the account rules, and the pt page no longer mentions the box's logo. The retention defaults in the GM manuals (en, pt) now say 90 days for game data, the sign-up sentence in the player manuals no longer says an account cannot be made in the app, and phase 4's STATUS names the right migration number (`0029_account_notice_records`).
- **The documentation, the project page and the decision rows brought up to date with 30 October's last merges** (documentation only; `README.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `docs/phase-2-field-network/STATUS.md`, `docs/phase-3-android-players/STATUS.md`, `docs/phase-4-site-server/STATUS.md`, the game master and player manuals en and pt, `site/i18n/{pt,en,de,zh-tw}.yaml` roadmap phases 2 to 4 and the generated pages, `docs/review/decisions-to-review.md`): the stream token out of the logs and `rand()` seeded from the game key (LIVE2-*), the Lua sandbox, the random-epoch search, the antenna option, the phone controller's revocation and ATAK TLS, team results kept on the controllers, the player's event list, `set-password`, the preview's notice and demo HSTS and the pentest stack are in the status files, `SECURITY.md` (its sections on the controller's network surface, recorded packets and the build, and the line on what has been tested where) and the roadmap in four languages, with what was and was not tried on hardware stated as before. Decision rows SR-110 to SR-139, FU30-1 to FU30-19, DOCS-5 to DOCS-9, SD-1 to SD-3, PF-1 and SP-1 added to the decisions file. A duplicate PF-1 entry below was dropped.

#### Removed

- **The Field Box no longer fetches or shows the field's logo** (decision LOGO-5, superseding PKG-1 and LOGO-1 to LOGO-4; maintainer's call, 2026-10-29): a join starts no Wi-Fi for a logo (the bench's failing fetch), the box keeps no `logo.bin`, and draws only the Atalaia tower. Gone: the firmware's `box_logo` and its tests and four screen goldens, the controllers' `GET /api/v1/field/box-logo` and `/box-logo/release` and `PUT /api/v1/field/branding/logo/box` (Python and Dart), the console's JPEG and WebP conversion for boxes (`box-logo.js`, its texts, its browser tests), `tools/box_logo_vectors.py` and `protocol/vectors/box_logo.json`. The field's logo stays in the app, the console, the scoreboard, the poster and the site server's sync. The join and the radio protocol never carried a logo key, so nothing on the wire is retired; `protocol/mode-packages.md` section 8 is kept as a retirement note.
- **A box that kept the old field logo removes it at boot** (decision LOGO-7; `firmware/esp32/main/legacy_files.c`): a box that fetched a logo before LOGO-5 still holds `logo.bin` (and maybe a half-written `logo.new`) in its LittleFS storage, which nothing reads now. The first boot of this firmware does one `stat` per name and an `unlink` for a file that exists, logging `removed N old field-logo file(s) from storage` once; a clean box pays nothing. Those two names were the only files the logo feature wrote. Host test `test_legacy_files.c`.

- **Site server: the daily retention purge deletes anonymised accounts nothing refers to any more** (`personal_data.py`, `purge`, new count `anonymised_users`): a `deleted-...` row (what erasure leaves; also what an account merge will leave) was kept forever. It now goes once no audit entry and no row of any table with a foreign key to `users.id` refers to it (the check is built from the models' foreign keys, so a later table is covered); the audit entries' own 36 months free it in the same pass. Hosted mode: per field, under the field's role. The privacy notice drafts (pt, en), README and DEPLOY say how long the remains of an erased account are kept.
- **A box's `game.end` now ends the game on the controller when the mode has no `field.lua`** (decision GE-1; `controller/atalaia_controller/engine.py`, the app's `controller_engine.dart`, the simulator's `sim.py`): in Sabotage (the time limit) and any mode whose `node.lua` calls `game.win` / `game.finish`, the box emitted `game.end` and the controller only logged it, so the game stayed running (found on the bench). The **first** `game.end` from a box assigned in the live game, while it runs and this controller leads it, ends the game with the event's winner (one of the game's teams) or as a draw, at the next tick; later ones are only logged. In a mode with a `field.lua` nothing changes: the script decides. The controller's own `game.end` carries who ended it (`by_node`, `by_name`; new event data fields 40 and 41) and the console's log says *ended by box ...* in four languages. A `game.end` that arrives while the game is paused does not end it. Shared contract cases in `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json` (three) run on both controllers.

#### Fixed

- **The conducted transmit power is rounded down, also below 0 dBm** (`firmware/esp32/main/radio/radio_power.h`, `simulator/atalaia_sim/regions.py`): the power is the sub-band's limit less the FEM and antenna gains, in hundredths of a dB, and C's division (and Python's `int()`) rounded a negative result toward zero, that is up: with a 10 dBi antenna the 25 mW sub-bands came out at -7 dBm instead of -8, 0.85 dB over the limit.
