- **Marshals only from the GM's list; the app asks the controller before it acts as one** (the maintainer's request of 2026-10-26, found testing the iPhone: "anybody can fill a value there and act as a marshal"; decisions MR-1 to MR-14, open; MR-4, MR-8 and MR-10 for the maintainer; the offline rule is MR-4: a phone out of reach of the controller may go on as a marshal for two hours after the controller's last yes (the maintainer's choice), never without one, and a marshal cannot chronograph at all at a field with no controller to confirm it). A marshal is someone on the list the GM keeps (the console's *Check-in* tab, *Marshals*, in pt-PT, en, de and zh-TW: add a player who has checked in, the controller gives the id `M-01`, `M-02`... and the role; rename, rebind, remove; whether their app has registered its key; store schema 12), on the Pi's and the phone's field controllers alike (`POST|PATCH|DELETE /api/v1/marshals`; `GET /api/v1/checkin/me` tells a marshal's phone its id, name and key, or `null`; `PUT .../me/marshal-key` takes a key for that id only). The app has no marshal id or name to type any more: it shows "You are marshal M-03", records nothing without the controller's yes (`MarshalRecords.record()` refuses too), and a marshal the GM removes loses the tools at once on the next ask, the readings not yet sent staying on the phone (held, not given up) until the GM adds them back. Contract cases on both controllers, `controller/tests/test_marshal_list.py`, `app/test/marshal/marshal_access_test.dart`, a console browser test; docs: `protocol/checkin.md`, `controller/README.md`, `app/README.md`.
- **The app's iOS project** (the maintainer's MacBook and iPhone for first tests, 2026-10-26; decision PD10 amended, decisions iOS-1 to iOS-12, open; iOS-2, iOS-4, iOS-7 and iOS-10 for the maintainer; first built on the maintainer's Mac with Xcode 27.1 on 2026-10-26, not yet run on an iPhone): `app/ios/`, generated with Flutter 3.44.0 in the app's Docker image, bundle id `org.atalaia.app`, name Atalaia, minimum iOS 15.0 (Xcode 27.1's floor: 13.0 was refused on the first Mac build), Swift Package Manager (no Podfile); `Info.plist` with the camera, NFC reader, location-when-in-use and local-network texts (English, and Portuguese, German and Traditional Chinese in `InfoPlist.strings`) and `NSAllowsLocalNetworking` for the GM console's web view; a Swift `device` channel (private folder out of backups, haptics, screen on) and every other channel left to answer "not available"; the icon and launch image drawn from the Android icon (`app/tool/gen_ios_icons.py`); no entitlements, so a free Apple ID can sign it; CI's `ios-build` job (macOS runner, `flutter build ios --release --no-codesign`, on pushes to `main`); and [`app/docs/ios-on-a-mac.md`](app/docs/ios-on-a-mac.md), the step-by-step install on an iPhone with a checklist. Player, marshal and GM roles only; QR, NFC, position sharing and `atalaia://` links have no iOS side yet, and the prop and the phone as field controller stay Android only.
- **Per-squad objective chains and who of a squad is told them** (the maintainer's request of 2026-10-26; decisions SQ-1 to SQ-10, open; SQ-2, SQ-6 and SQ-8 for the maintainer; in software, on the Pi's and the phone's field controllers): a scenario's manifest takes `reveal: active` (an objective that depends on others is known to its squad only when it opens, so a squad is sent its active objective and the ones it has done, never a later one: not in the game, the log or the stream) and `disclosure: leader` (only the squad's leader gets the objective; the other members get `leader_only` news and the app says *your squad leader has new orders*, in Portuguese, English, German and Traditional Chinese, with a short vibration and no sound). The GM chooses per game, when creating it and while it runs (`objective_disclosure` in `POST /api/v1/games`, `PUT /api/v1/games/{id}/objective-disclosure`, the console's new-game form and game panel); marshals see everything, spectators nothing secret; ATAK shows no scenario objectives. The leader-absent case is the GM's switch for now (SQ-6). New shared case "SQ per-squad objective chains" in `controller_api.json`, golden compiler vectors for the two keys, the example `controller/tests/scenarios/squad_chains.yaml`.
- **A preview deployment of the site server** for the maintainer's first round of outside feedback, at `tests.atalaia-project.link` through a Cloudflare Tunnel (the maintainer's request of 2026-10-26; decisions PV-1 to PV-12, open; PV-2, PV-5 and PV-6 for the maintainer; in software, tested on PostgreSQL and in Chromium, running on the maintainer's machine, not yet seen by a visitor): `ATALAIA_PREVIEW=on` adds a "Preview, not for real games" banner on every page, `noindex`, `robots.txt`, a landing page and a **feedback form** (pt-PT, English, German, Traditional Chinese; no account, cookie or address kept; signed timed token, same-origin check, honeypot, size and rate limits; read by the server administrators at `/staff/feedback`, in the API (`/api/v1/admin/feedback`) and with `python -m atalaia_server feedback list`; migration `0013_preview_feedback`; contacts erased after 90 days, rows after 12 months); **demo data** (`python -m atalaia_server demo load|reset|reviewer`: an invented field, two clubs, scheduled games, finished games with scores, events and boxes, kept fresh hourly) and generated logins for the administrator and for invited reviewers (`create-admin --generate`, `demo reviewer`; files of mode 0600, nothing printed); `ATALAIA_LANG_FROM_BROWSER=off` and `ATALAIA_DEFAULT_LANG` (the preview opens in Portuguese whatever the browser says; a choice with `?lang=` or the switcher is remembered in a `lang` cookie); `server/docker-compose.preview.yml` and `server/preview/preview.sh` (no Caddy, published on `127.0.0.1:8000` only, restart unless stopped, forwarded headers trusted from one Docker subnet) with the runbook `docs/runbooks/preview-server.md`; `python3 tools/docker_server.py preview-check`. The GM console, simulator and editor are not part of it (PV-2).
- Progressive Domination (*Domínio Progressivo*, `modes/progressive_domination/`), the maintainer's request of 2026-10-26 (decisions PD-MODE-1 to PD-MODE-21, open; PD-MODE-5, 9, 14, 16, 18 and 19 for the maintainer): several boxes, one capturable at a time and announced to both teams; the holder scores `points_per_min` for each full minute, a box completed after `complete_min` minutes held is locked and the next is announced; order by the GM's list, box ids or random; ends with the last box or a target score. Events `progressive.activated` (54) and `progressive.completed` (55) with the data key `step` (39); four YAML screens in four languages; the fifteenth Field Box preset (+15,536 bytes, 9 % of the OTA slot free; fonts regenerated for the new Chinese letters); console and app event lines in four languages, the active box ringed on the console's plan and shown in the player app; project page in four languages; 8 mode tests plus simulator, controller, firmware and Dart tests. In software only, not yet on radio hardware.
- **The project page's modes have animated pictures of the Field Box's screens** (the maintainer's request of 2026-10-26; decisions GIF-1 to GIF-12, open; GIF-2 to GIF-5 for the maintainer; in software, looked at frame by frame and on the page at phone and desktop width in headless Chromium): thirteen modes (Progressive Domination among them), each a picture of a game going on as the box shows it, the TFT above and the OLED below, in Portuguese, English, German and Traditional Chinese (52 animations of 13 to 71 KB and 52 stills for `prefers-reduced-motion`, 2.3 MB together, lazy-loaded, with a pause / play button, alt texts and captions). Drawn by the firmware's own code, not mocked up: `firmware/esp32/test/mode_gif_frames.c` (a host program built with the other host tests) renders scripted screen updates (`tools/mode_gifs.yaml`, one entry per mode) with the same LVGL, screen renderer, mode templates and OLED view as the device, and `tools/gen_mode_gifs.py` frames and encodes them with a plain-Python GIF writer, so `--check` (CI's firmware-test job) compares the files byte for byte. Domination's picture is in the README too. Adding a mode is one entry in the YAML, one `<figure>` and its texts (`site/README.md`).
- **Radio regions: Taiwan's band (AS923-TW) alongside EU868** (the maintainer's RF-AS923-1, 2026-10-23; RFC 0001 Amendment 12, proposed; decisions RF-AS923-2 to RF-AS923-14, open; RF-AS923-3, RF-AS923-4, RF-AS923-8 and RF-AS923-13 for the maintainer; in software and host tests, **not yet on air, not certified**). One region table per language (`core/src/duty_cycle.c`, `simulator/atalaia_sim/regions.py`, `app/lib/protocol/radio_region.dart`) holds each region's LoRa settings, sub-bands, power limit and its kind (ERP, EIRP, at the antenna port), airtime budget, default channel and candidates; nothing checks a frequency outside it. **AS923-TW**: 920-925 MHz under NCC LP0002 5.8.1, LoRa **SF9 at 500 kHz** (a single channel that does not hop gets full power only at 500 kHz or more; SF9 keeps SF7/125 kHz's symbol time and nearly its sensitivity), at most **0.5 W at the antenna port** (13 dBm from the SX1262 with the default gains), this project's own 10% airtime, default **921.5 MHz** clear of LoRaWAN's 923.2/923.4 MHz and Meshtastic's 923.875 MHz, nine 500 kHz candidates. **Firmware**: Kconfig `ATALAIA_RADIO_REGION` (EU868 default; `tools/docker_fw.py build|bench|modem|pod --region=as923-tw`): a Field Box, USB modem or I/O pod uses only its build's region, its SF and bandwidth (with the SX1262's 500 kHz TX fix and CAD threshold per SF), and refuses another region's join (new `BOX_JOIN_REGION`), CONFIGURE, SET_CHANNEL and MEASURE; the modem's IDENT names its `region`; a channel whose limit the SX1262 cannot keep even at -9 dBm is now refused instead of clamped (RF-AS923-9); the Wi-Fi setup page offers only the build's region (it listed US915 and others the radio never used). **Join key 18 `region`** (absent for EU868, so EU868 joins are unchanged; old readers refuse a Taiwanese join by its channel), in the C, Python and Dart codecs with a `region` section in `protocol/vectors/join.json`. **Controllers** (Pi and phone): `[radio] region` and `PUT /api/v1/radio/region` (no live game, no scan; back to the region's default channel, unconfirmed); `GET /api/v1/radio` names the region, the regions, the modem's and a mismatch; channels, scans, random picks, pairing and joins follow the field's region; a standby refuses another region's game; a shared contract case. **GM console**: the region in the radio settings (pt, en, de, zh-tw), changed after a confirmation, with a warning when the modem is built for another region, limits shown with their reference. **Simulator**: the radio model and the free boxes take a region. The project page's radio section explains the regions in all four languages.
- *Load the field from a map*: **more open orthophotos** (decisions MF-73 to MF-80, open; MF-74 to MF-77 for the maintainer; in software, tested in Chromium with every service mocked, the services themselves checked by hand with CORS requests on 2026-10-23): **Belgium** by region (Brussels from the NGI-IGN's mosaic, Flanders from Digitaal Vlaanderen, Wallonia from the SPW), **Luxembourg** (geoportail.lu, CC0), **Switzerland and Liechtenstein** (swisstopo's SWISSIMAGE), **eleven German Länder** (Bayern, Berlin, Brandenburg, Bremen, Hessen, Mecklenburg-Vorpommern, Nordrhein-Westfalen, Rheinland-Pfalz, Saarland, Sachsen, Thüringen; one source per Land, each with its own licence's credit), **Poland** (GUGiK), **Czechia** (ČÚZK) and IGN France's orthophotos over the **French overseas** departments and collectivities. `tools/ortho_coverage.py` also reads Natural Earth's admin-1 states and provinces, for the regions; `ortho-coverage.js` grows to 98 KB (33 KB gzipped). Only `field-map.html`'s policy gains the new origins: each WMS in `img-src` and `connect-src`, the three WMTS on hosts of their own in `img-src` only. Left out, with the reason (MF-80): Austria (WMTS only), Hamburg (a closed licence mixed in), Baden-Württemberg (its WMS broken today), Niedersachsen (in maintenance), Sachsen-Anhalt and Schleswig-Holstein (CORS only for https pages), Italy, Denmark (token), Andorra, Gibraltar, the Azores and Madeira.
- **The phone as the field controller does everything a Pi's does**: the rest of slice S7 of PE24, pulled forward with the maintainer's approval of 2026-10-23 (decisions PE24-118 to PE24-129, proposed; PE24-118, PE24-125, PE24-126 and PE24-127 for the maintainer; in software, not yet on a phone or the bench). **Failover**: the phone runs the Pi's CLAIM election (`controller_failover.dart`, the simulator's `election.py` in Dart; CLAIM checked against `claim.json`), so a phone and a Pi can each be the other's standby: a join payload of kind `controller` from the leading controller (`POST /api/v1/controller/join`), the leader's TIME followed, *Promote* (`POST /api/v1/controller/promote`) or automatic failover after three missed beacons (`failover_mode`, in the app's *Definições*), the winner's TIME with the new term and its `field.lua` taken over. **The site sync** (`controller_sync.dart`, `sync_client.py` in Dart; every body checked with `controller_sync_format.dart`, the Dart port of `sync_format.py`, against all of `protocol/vectors/sync.json`): games, logs and scores to the site server when the internet is there (offline first), the chrono registry, game day with the schedule (`GET /api/v1/schedule`, a game made from a scheduled one), rosters until the start and outcomes after the end, claims answered, the field's branding and saved teams and the scenario library from the site. **The remote GM** (`controller_remote_gm.dart`): the stream the phone opens to the site server, the live view, the field's GMs' commands checked, carried out and logged (`GET /api/v1/remote-gm`). **ATAK** (`controller_atak.dart`): CoT by multicast on the field's Wi-Fi, staff devices over TCP, the remote relay through the site server's stream, byte for byte the Pi's (`protocol/vectors/atak_xml.json`, written from `atak.py` by `tools/atak_xml_vectors.py`, checked in `check.sh` and CI). The settings (failover, the site server with the field's API key, shown afterwards only by its fingerprint, ATAK) are a new screen of the app's *Definições*. `whoami` now names every area the Pi does (`failover`, `schedule`, `atak` added). The shared contract cases gain a block `s7` (another controller's TIME and CLAIM, `join_encode`, `failover_mode`): 4 new cases, 63 in all, passing on both controllers; end-to-end tests run the phone against a site server, a WebSocket and UDP and TCP listeners in the tests, and two phone controllers fail over on a shared air.
- **A grid over the field's map**, as the maintainer asked (2026-10-23; decisions MF-81 to MF-93, open; MF-84, MF-85, MF-88 and MF-92 for the maintainer): the GM turns on *Quadrícula no mapa / Grid on the map* under the plan, or over the preview on *Load the field from a map*, with 1-52 lettered rows (A ... Z, AA ... AZ) and 1-52 numbered columns, for calling positions on the radio ("C4"). One grid per level, kept by the controller (`GET|PUT /api/v1/field/grid` and `/levels/{n}/grid`, a `grid` in each level and in `POST .../from-map`, the stream's `field_grid`; no store migration) on the Pi and the phone controller alike (a shared contract case); drawn over the image, not into it (white lines and labels on a dark halo, labels thinned out on small cells), in the console's field view (and so in the app, which shows the console) and the field-map preview; any viewer may hide it in their own browser; the boxes' labels and the map features' list say their cell. In pt-PT, English, German and Traditional Chinese (German and Chinese AI drafts).
- **The built-in modes and the site server's pages in German and Traditional Chinese (Taiwan)**, the two parts German and Chinese had left out (the maintainer's approval of 2026-10-23 for P5H-7 and P5H-8; decisions P5H-12 to P5H-18 and P5K-13 to P5K-15, open; P5H-12, P5H-15, P5H-17 and P5K-13 for the maintainer; **drafted by an AI model, not yet reviewed by a native-speaking player**, `docs/translations/status.yaml`): every built-in mode's `mode.yaml` has `i18n.de` and `i18n.zh-tw` (name, description, every parameter and command), shown by the GM console and the app in the GM's language, and the registry's sample `org.example.hill` too; every mode's screen template has German and Chinese wherever it had Portuguese (a chain per text, English last), so a German or Chinese box, and now an Android prop with the join's language, shows the modes' own screens in its language; the Medic Kit, Hacking Terminal and VIP take `de` and `zh-tw` in their `lang` parameter (Portuguese stays the default; the Medic Kit's refusals in four languages); the Hacking Terminal shows a Chinese guess as Taiwan's 1A2B game. **A template may hold 3072 bytes of text (was 1536; the file stays at most 4096 bytes)**: a box before this change refuses a fetched template over 1536. The Field Box's fonts gain the Chinese letters of the modes' screens and scripts, each at the size its label draws it (`tools/gen_box_fonts.py` reads the templates and writes `box_fonts_cjk.h`; its `--check` fails on a template letter the fonts lack), and a template keeps a Chinese letter its label's font has (others are `?`): Field Box image 0x1c3fc0 → 0x1cd860 (+38 KB, 10 % of its slot free), bench 0x1be0a0 → 0x1c7930, modem 0x7f640 → 0x83dd0; 33 new reference images (29 TFT, 4 OLED), the existing ones identical but the two that pinned English on German and Chinese boxes. The site server's field, club, scoreboard and staff pages in German and Chinese: `?lang=de|zh-tw` or `Accept-Language` (Traditional Chinese only: `zh-Hant`, or `zh-TW`, `zh-HK`, `zh-MO`; Simplified falls to the next choice), `lang="de"` / `"zh-Hant-TW"`, dates written out in each language, the four languages in every switcher. `tools/i18n_check.py` checks the manifests' every text (it read names only) and lists `modes` and `server` for both languages. Kill Confirmed, Respawn and Player Check-in stay English on the box (they have no Portuguese either), as do the box's preset names (P5H-18). The mode editor carries German and Chinese like Portuguese (name, description, parameter and command labels; its three examples have them), so a mode made in it writes `i18n.de` and `i18n.zh-tw`.

### Changed
- **The Medic Kit, the Hacking Terminal and VIP / Payload follow the game's language** (found while writing the user manuals; decision P5H-23): their screens (and the Medic Kit's refusals, which its script writes) took their language from a mode parameter `lang` that defaulted to Portuguese whatever the game, the console or the box said. The parameter now defaults to empty and the host fills an empty one with the game's language (English when the game has none): the field controller when it makes the game (so the boxes' joins carry it) and the box when it starts one with none (`box_text_fill_lang`; the built-in presets hold `"lang":""`). A language the GM sets stays. No script API change (a script still cannot read the box's language, P5H-20; it reads a parameter the host filled). The phone's own controller and an Android prop fill it the same way (`fillLangParam` in `app/lib/controller/controller_modes.dart`; a prop takes its join's language).
- **The Field Box's built-in mode screens no longer show English on a Portuguese, German or Chinese box** (found 2026-10-26: Bomb's small OLED screen said "DEFUSED" in the Chinese version; decisions P5H-19 to P5H-22, open; P5H-21 and P5H-22 for the maintainer): the OLED's middle line (the template's name: DEFUSED, EXPLODED, and the Medic Kit's, Hacking Terminal's, Money Factory's and the other built-in templates' names) and its header (the mode's name) are in the box's language (`firmware/esp32/main/box_text.c`, `mode_names` and `screen_names`; a test fails for a built-in mode or template without a name), the keypad's headers ARM, DEFUSE and CODE too, and the TFT screens of Kill Confirmed, Respawn and Player Check-in have Portuguese, German and Chinese texts. The box's fonts carry the new Chinese letters (+18 KB; the Field Box's image has 8 % of its slot free).
- **One Chinese word for the Codes modifier: 密碼** (a code players type or guess on a keypad), **代碼** only for codes that name something (pairing, join, ticket, access); the box's keypad screens said 代碼, the app, console and project page 密碼 (decisions P5K-16 and P5K-17, open, for the maintainer; `docs/translations/glossary.md`).
- **The C core builds as a shared library on a Mac, and the app's Dart tests find it**: `app/test/support/core_libs.dart` looks for `.dylib` on macOS, `.so` on Linux and `.dll` on Windows; on macOS only the core's CMake also builds the stub and leaves its unit tests, mbedtls and `-Werror` off (new option `ATALAIA_WERROR`; Linux is as before), so `cmake -S core -B build -DATALAIA_SHARED=ON` is enough; the steps are in `app/README.md` ("Tests on a Mac") and `app/docs/ios-on-a-mac.md`. Written without a Mac, to be tried on one.
- The app's address fields (the GM console's, the player's controller and the site's) are easier to edit, found on the first iPhone install (2026-10-26): a clear button (an x, labelled in Portuguese, English, German and Traditional Chinese) empties the field and keeps the focus while there is text, and on iOS the first focus of a prefilled address selects all of it, so typing replaces it (a tap in a URL there put the cursor at its start, a URL being one word); later taps place the cursor as usual, and Android keeps its own tap behaviour. One shared `AddressField` (`app/lib/ui/widgets.dart`), tests in `app/test/ui/address_field_test.dart`, goldens `player_start_*` and `gm_address_*`.
- **One scenario compiler, in the core**, for the Pi's and the phone's field controllers (the maintainer's approval of 2026-10-23 for PE24-84; decisions PE24-109 to PE24-117, open; PE24-109, PE24-110 and PE24-113 for the maintainer): the validator and the compiler of custom scenarios, which were written twice (Python on the Pi, about 1,330 lines and the runtime; a Dart port on the phone, about 3,800 lines) and kept alike only by the shared vectors, are now written once, in Lua, and run by the shared C core (`core/scenario/`, `atalaia/scenario.h`: `atalaia_scenario_check`, `atalaia_scenario_compile`; each call in a Lua state of its own with only the safe libraries, a 64 MB and a 200-million-instruction limit, a manifest past them being a problem, `too_complex`). Each controller only reads the manifest's text (the Pi with PyYAML, the phone with its PyYAML-compatible reader) and hands the core what it read as the manifest's data tree, typed as `scenario_compile.json`'s `loads` with YAML aliases kept (`core/scenario/README.md`). The rules, `field.lua`'s writer, `mode.yaml`'s content and a PyYAML-exact emitter for it, the Python semantics of the problems' paths and messages, the radio size of the scenario events (from the core's own protocol tables) and the runtime (moved to `core/scenario/runtime.lua`) are one Lua implementation of about 4,100 lines (with the YAML emitter and the Python semantics that PyYAML and Python had given the Pi for free), plus about 470 lines of C; gone are the Python validator, Lua writer and package logic and the Dart validator, Lua writer, emitter, Python semantics and runtime copy (the scenario code on the Pi goes from 1,417 to 532 lines of Python, on the phone from 4,018 to 1,104 lines of Dart, the reader that stays). Every case of `protocol/vectors/scenario.json` and `scenario_compile.json` gives the same problems and the same packages byte for byte on both controllers, and so did 130,000 generated manifests compared with the Python compiler, messages included. The Unicode classes are one table now (Python 3.12's 15.0.0, `tools/scenario_unicode.py`); three inputs that made the Pi's check fail with a 500 (a language JSON cannot write, a dependency cycle through about 1,000 objectives or more, an integer too large for a float in a destination) are problems now; a manifest nested more than 100 levels deep is `bad_manifest`. The core builds it with `ATALAIA_SCENARIO` (on by default; its Lua sources embedded by CMake alone); the Field Box's firmware never builds it, and its images keep their size. Without the core library, scenarios are read but not checked (`checked_in_core: false`) or installed (503), on both controllers; the command line's `validate` needs the core too. New: `core/tests/test_scenario.c` (the event sizes against the core's codec, the limits, malformed and 3,000 mutated data trees; clean under ASan and UBSan), data tree tests on both controllers.
- The OLED start screens (the Field Box's and the USB radio modem's) show the firmware's version in a short form that fits (decision FV-8, open; FV-6's follow-up): the version on one line and its commit on the next (`v0.4.0-dev.12` and `abc1234`, `*` after the commit for a build with uncommitted changes), a release alone (`v0.3.0`), where they showed `v` and the first 20 characters, clipped. The tower and the text moved a few pixels left for room; the modem's version line is Montserrat 14 like the box's. Host tests and six OLED reference images (`firmware/esp32/test/screens/oled/splash*`, `modem_splash*`).
- CI's firmware images (`esp32-build`, `esp32-modem-build`, `esp32-pod-build`) carry their commit, `0.4.0-dev.N+g<hash>`, as local builds do (decision FV-9, open; FV-3's follow-up): the jobs check out the whole history and pass `tools/docker_fw.py version`'s answer to the build, and check the image has it. `docker_fw.py` on a shallow clone, which cannot count the commits, gives the version with the commit and no count (`0.4.0-dev+g<hash>`) instead of a wrong count.

### Fixed
- **The Pi's controller fails a firmware update the radio refused, as the phone does** (the Pi follow-up of decision PE24-133, now built): `engine._on_tx_ack`'s `tx.failed` reaches `firmware_updates.py`, and an update still `sent` whose packet seq the refused TX_ACK names, within 30 s of the offer, becomes `failed` at once with `radio_duty_cycle`, `radio_queue_full` or `radio_failed`, instead of staying `sent` for the 600 s timeout and holding the game's start; nothing is resent. Python tests and a new shared contract case with a refusing radio (both controllers pass it).
- **`GET /api/v1/games/{id}` could answer 409** (found by the console's browser tests: the Check-in tab's `chrono-list` request failed when the game had ended between the check that it was live and its start check): the game is then just not live (`controller/atalaia_controller/engine.py` `public_game`).
- **The Portuguese box text *Tente daqui a %lu min* is *Tenta daqui a %lu min*** (the project's pt-PT addresses the player as "tu"; found while reviewing every Portuguese box text for formal forms and gendered words: it was the only one). The *PIN locked* screen's reference image changed with it.
- **`BRING_UP.md`'s Bomb sequence was missing a `#`** (game 1, `#` for the timer, `#` to confirm, `#` to start: three, not two), in step 4 and in the bench paragraph; step 11 says what the firmware does (the way back into setup, the captive portal and its limits).
- **The site's register page no longer tells players to "make an account on this site"** (found while writing the user manuals; decision S17-16's wording): nothing on the site or in the app creates a player account yet (sign-up waits for a privacy notice and the legal review), so the page says an account is needed, that the app and the page cannot make one yet, to ask the field's organisers or the site's administrator, and to log in with one already held; in Portuguese, English, German and Chinese.
- **An open console no longer misses a game made or started elsewhere** (found on the bench, 2026-10-26: the console showed no game while game 358, made and started from another session 15 minutes before, was running, and a phone checked in to it; decision CL-2, open): the controller already broadcasts a `game` message on every create, start, pause, resume and end, to the GM's and the read token alike, and the console follows it (a browser test now makes, starts, pauses, resumes and ends games through the API with a GM page and a read-token page open and no reload), so the cause was a stream that stayed open but silent (a half-open WebSocket after a sleep or a Wi-Fi drop: the browser is never told, and the 10 s refresh only looked at a game the page already knew). The console's stream now pings every 8 s (the controllers already answer `ping` with `pong`, on the Pi and the phone) and replaces a connection silent for 20 s, and a tab brought back to the front or a network that returns checks at once; the 10 s refresh asks `GET /api/v1/controller` for the live game and fetches one the page does not know. No change to the stream's messages or to the phone controller; the scoreboard shares the stream client. Browser tests for both failures (a stream that stops delivering, a stream that stops answering).
- **The check-in form no longer draws a hint over the next field's label** (found on the maintainer's iPhone, 2026-10-26): the name hint was cut to one line and overlapped the "Equipa preferida" label below it, because the fields had no space between them and the hints could take one line only. The check-in form and the site's registration form now leave 16 dp between fields, show their hints in up to three lines (`helperMaxLines`), and the same hint limit is set on the other forms' hinted fields (game setup, chrono, replica registry, tag writer); the replica dialog's fields are spaced too. `checkin_form_layout_test.dart` checks at 360 dp, text scale 1.0 and 1.3, in all four languages, that nothing overflows, no hint is cut and no hint touches the next label; the site registration goldens are redrawn.
- **The player's start screen no longer calls a check-in poster a bad link** (found on the maintainer's iPhone, 2026-10-26: *Participante*'s "Ler o código QR do marcador" read the poster's `atalaia:checkin:…` and said "Falta o endereço ou o código de acesso."): a poster scanned there, or typed in the address or code field, opens the check-in with it, as *Ler o cartaz* does; a join card, a site link or (on the check-in, join, site and replica screens) any other code of Atalaia's own now says what it is and where to use it (pt-PT, en, de, zh-TW; `app/lib/ui/code_kind.dart`), the marshal's replica scans no longer take a poster or a link for a replica's label, and anything else is still `player.bad_link`. Tests in `app/test/player/wrong_code_test.dart` and `app/test/marshal/chrono_queue_test.dart`.
- **A box's ACKs no longer stop for good after a lost packet** (decision SL-8, built; found by the relay study, branch `worktree-agent-a5a8a8ab00155782c`): the controller's `AckTracker` (the Pi's, in `simulator/atalaia_sim/field_controller.py`, and the phone's port in `app/lib/controller/field_controller.dart`) kept its base below the first seq of a box that never arrived (a STATE lost on air is never sent again, an event's resend takes a new seq), and 256 packets later the box's newest seq was outside the 256-bit bitmap and no ACK could name it until the box rebooted: about an hour into a game, about 13 minutes for a HELLO retried every 3 s. Both trackers now count every seq more than 128 below the highest received as received. Controller only, no wire change, no box change. New shared vectors `protocol/vectors/ack_tracker.json` (the Pi and the phone give the same base and bitmap), a test on each controller that failed before.
- **The field's channel is no longer left stale when a scan ends as the GM picks one** (decision RF-7, built): the retune after a scan read the old channel outside the controller's lock and could be sent after the GM's new one; `RadioModem.request_status()` answers a STATUS with the modem's own reply, which a ping's reply no longer overwrites (the cause of a flaky controller test under load).
- The GM console's marshal list: a name being typed in a row was put back to the old one whenever the panel was drawn again (the live stream, the list's 5 s refresh), so a slow GM's edit could be lost; the typed name now stays until the field is left and saved, and the refresh waits while a row is being edited (found by the browser test under load, 2026-10-26).
- The on-device check-in test failed on an iPhone (iOS 27.2, 2026-10-26): its fixed server certificate was valid 3650 days, and Apple's SecTrust (iOS and macOS) refuses any TLS server certificate valid for more than 825 days, private CAs included. `integration_test/check_in_test.dart` now makes its CA and 365-day server certificate at run time with the app's own `lib/controller/field_tls.dart` (the fixed PEMs in `test_certs.dart` are gone, so they cannot expire either); guard tests in `controller/tests/test_tls.py`, `server/tests/test_atak.py` and `app/test/controller/field_tls_test.dart` keep every server certificate within 825 days. Production was not affected (365 days).
- The site server's field pages: a missing page (404) no longer carries a `Last-Modified` (it was the current time, so a HEAD and a GET a second apart differed and `test_head_answers_like_get` failed once for `/f/nope/`); the feeds' 404 never had one.
- The app's Settings > About showed 0.2.0 on a 0.3.0 install, and a phone controller's `/healthz` and `/whoami` gave 0.2.0 where a Pi gives 0.3.0 (found on the first iPhone install, 2026-10-26): the version is now in one place, `app/lib/version.dart`, also used for a prop's `app-<version>`, and `app/test/version_test.dart` keeps it equal to `pubspec.yaml`'s and the Pi controller's `__version__`.
- Two Pi controller bugs found by the phone's port, fixed in both controllers: a standby restarted after a takeover claimed a term no higher than the boxes knew, so they would have ignored its TIME (PE24-119); and the site sync and the remote GM's live view left out every box that had said HELLO, whose integer capability ids the sync's format refused as texts (PE24-121).
- The phone controller's field script did not know the box names the GM gives in the Join tab (`field.nodes()` returned each box's own name, as the Pi did before M6-7), so a scenario could not find its boxes by name on a phone controller; now both give the Join tab's names (decision SQ-9, open).
- The mode editor: choosing two examples quickly could load the first one chosen, when its file came last; now only the one chosen last loads, and *New* or *Open* drops an example still on its way (decision FL-3, open). Its browser tests wait for the example to be loaded (`body[data-example]`) instead of for text the example before it already showed, which made the line-map test fail once under load.

## [0.3.0] - 2026-10-23

The first tagged release: everything built since the project started (phases 0 to 5 in software, the bench tests on real Heltec V4.3 boards listed below). Firmware images for Field Boxes are signed separately with the project's offline release key (firmware/esp32/docs/RELEASE_SIGNING.md); this tag is the source release.

### Added
- *Load the field from a map*: **open orthophotos beyond Portugal**, after the maintainer's answer to MF-57 ("open source would be nice"; decisions MF-65 to MF-72, open; MF-68 to MF-70 for the maintainer; in software, tested in Chromium with every service mocked, the services themselves checked by hand on 2026-10-23): **Spain** (IGN España's PNOA, "PNOA CC-BY 4.0 scne.es"), **France** (IGN France's orthophotos, Géoplateforme, "Orthophotos © IGN (Licence Ouverte 2.0)") and the **Netherlands** (PDOK's 25 cm aerial photographs, "Luchtfoto © Beeldmateriaal.nl / PDOK (CC BY 4.0)"), beside the DGT's in mainland Portugal. A small table of sources (`components/ortho-sources.js`) replaces the single DGT source; each country's coverage is its own land (Natural Earth, public domain, simplified to about 1 km, `tools/ortho_coverage.py`). The map's *Ortofoto* layer follows the map's centre and says whose it is (*Ortofoto (IGN España)*), with its tiles from each service's WMTS (the DGT's still per-tile WMS) and its credit in the corner; the drawn image uses the orthophoto of the country containing the outline's centroid (at a border, the neighbour's once if the first is white), within each WMS's size limit (PDOK's 2500 pixels), with the same source's credit burned in and shown under the preview. Outside every coverage the switch stays hidden, as before. Only `field-map.html`'s policy gains `https://www.ign.es`, `https://data.geopf.fr` and `https://service.pdok.nl` (images and connections).
- The phone as the field controller: boxes over the radio (slice S7c of PE24, pulled forward with the maintainer's approval of 2026-10-23; decisions PE24-90 to PE24-99; in software, tested with the Pi's USB radio modem and Field Boxes' own code on a PC, not yet on a phone). The phone lists free boxes and invites them into the next game with sealed radio invites; it tells a game's boxes it is over (GAME_OVER, also after a restart without the key); the start waits for missing roles unless forced, and for firmware updates. New boxes are adopted by pairing code over LoRa or with the field card, which the phone writes by NFC; there is no USB adoption on a phone. Firmware updates between games go over the phone's HTTPS with its field CA, with the release keys on the controller's screen. `whoami` adds boxes, adoption and firmware; 10 new shared contract cases pass on both controllers. A USB radio modem plugged into the phone (probed against an I/O pod) also answers MEASURE, so the channel scan works with it. The radio for invites, GAME_OVER and pairing is the Pi's USB radio modem on the phone for now: the I/O pod does not relay field or pair frames yet (PE24-91, a pod firmware follow-up).
- *Load the field from a map*, after the maintainer's first real use (2026-10-23; decisions MF-50 to MF-64, open; MF-51 and MF-57 for the maintainer; in software, tested in Chromium with every outside service mocked, not yet against the live services after the change): a busy Overpass API (429, 502, 503, 504, a busy remark, or no readable answer, e.g. an error without CORS) is **retried with exponential backoff and jitter** (2 s doubling to 16 s, at least 30 s after a 429; at most 6 tries in about 60 s), asking meanwhile **one other public instance** (`overpass.private.coffee`, whose data may be a few months old, as the page says; added to that page's `connect-src` only; VK's instance in Russia left out pending the maintainer); each request ends after **35 s**; the status line and a banner at the top say which try, where, and the seconds to the next, and **Cancel** stops it. A **Mapa / Ortofoto** switch on the map shows the **DGT orthophoto** as the map's tiles in mainland Portugal (one WMS GetMap per 256-pixel tile in EPSG:3857, only the tiles in view, kept while panning; "Ortofotomapas © DGT (CC BY 4.0)" shown; the choice remembered in the browser; hidden elsewhere, with why: no open high-resolution imagery found for other countries, proposals recorded). On a wide window the map takes the width with the outline beside it (centred, up to 100rem; checked at 320 to 1850 px). The drawn image **scrolls into view with the focus on its heading** and *Use as the field* right under it; leaving with an image not used **asks first** (the back link, and `beforeunload` for the tab); the outline's corners **survive a reload** (this tab only); after saving the page **goes back to the console's Field tab** on that level, which says the field was loaded.
- The I/O pod relays field and pair frames, so a phone with only a pod for a radio can send sealed invitations and re-invites, end a game with GAME_OVER and adopt a box by its code (follow-up PE24-91 of the Android controller's slice S7c; decisions PE24-100 to PE24-104, open; in software and host tests, not yet on a pod with a phone): the capability proxy gains RADIO_FIELD_TX, RADIO_FIELD_RX and RADIO_FIELD_ACK (`0x0C`-`0x0E`, the modem's FIELD_TX, FIELD_RX and FIELD_ACK plus 3, payloads unchanged; pair frames ride them as on the modem) and three optional IDENT bools, keys 6 `field_frames`, 7 `pair_frames` (the numbers the phone's side of slice S7c reads) and 8 `measure` (within version 1; an older pod leaves them out). The pod runs the modem's own code for them (the shape check, `hop_limit` 0, busy during a MEASURE, the duty cycle) and never opens a frame; its IDENT still fits in 256 bytes (237 with every part, the flags and the longest version; four button colours are now left out a little sooner). The host pod relays them too, for the app's end-to-end tests. Core and Python codecs, shared vectors (a new `pod_relay` section of `protocol/vectors/capability_proxy.yaml`), host tests, `protocol/capability-proxy.md`, `usb-modem.md`, the firmware and core READMEs and RFC 0000 updated; the phone's side is S7c's.
- Every firmware build carries its commit in its version (the maintainer's request of 2026-10-23, decisions FV-1 to FV-7, open): the next version lives in `core/include/atalaia/version.h`, now **0.3.0 with the pre-release `dev`** (`atalaia_version()` and HELLO's `core_version` say `0.3.0-dev`; a release empties `ATALAIA_VERSION_PRERELEASE` and is tagged, nothing is tagged yet), and `tools/docker_fw.py` gives every image (Field Box, bench, Wokwi, modem, pod) `0.3.0-dev.<commits since the last release tag, or since the first commit>+g<the commit's first 7 hex digits>`, with `.dirty` when tracked files have uncommitted changes (`0.3.0-dev.164+g8560d54`), worked out on the host and printed before and after the build; `docker_fw.py version` prints it alone. A copy with no commit history (the verify script's fresh `git init`, a tarball) or a plain `idf.py build` gets `0.3.0-dev`; `--fw-version=` still sets an exact version. Firmware versions now take Semantic Versioning build metadata (`+...`, ignored in every comparison) on the box (`fw_update.c`), the controller, the site server's catalogue, `tools/firmware_release.py` and the console's update offer: every dev build of 0.3.0 is newer than 0.2.0 and older than 0.3.0, `dev.N` compares as a number, and two builds with the same count are equal, so a box still refuses an update that is not newer. Mode package versions still refuse `+`. The pod shortens the build metadata to fit IDENT's 23 bytes (`pod_fw_version`); the modem's and the pod's boot logs show their image's version. Shared vectors in `protocol/vectors/firmware.json` (versions with build metadata, a signed dev-build release), host tests for the box's checks and the pod, Python tests for the version a build carries (with a real repository where git is installed), and the console's ordering checked in the browser against the vectors. Firmware README, `BRING_UP.md` (step 10c's bench update now goes from a dev build to `0.3.1-bench.1`), `RELEASE_SIGNING.md`, `protocol/firmware-update.md` and `protocol/capability-proxy.md` updated.
- The Android controller role, slice S7a: the field plan and the radio channel on a phone controller (pulled forward from S7 by the maintainer on 2026-10-23; decisions PE24-74 to PE24-79, open; in software, checked against the Pi controller by shared contract cases and with the I/O pod firmware's own code on one machine, not on a phone or a pod): the phone now serves the Pi's field plan (a plan per level, PNG, JPEG or WebP up to 4 MB, kept as files in the controller's folder; multi-story levels; each plan's map position from two corners or 3-8 points, with the Pi's arithmetic and refusals; map features with the field boundary and their visibility checked for every token on REST and in the stream's `map_features`; the field loaded from a map, all or nothing; the boxes' places on the plan) and the field's radio channel (`GET /api/v1/radio`, the channel choice, a random suggestion and *find a quiet channel*, a noise scan through the I/O pod, which measures with the USB modem's own code; its IDENT has no `measure` flag, PE24-77, for the pod firmware). `GET /api/v1/whoami` now names `radio` and `field` on a phone, so the GM console shows the *Campo / Field* tab and the radio panel there, and players' out-of-bounds warning works on a phone-controlled field (MF-40's follow-up). The shared cases gain a `field_and_radio` block (raw image bodies, binary answers, the scan's steps): 6 new cases, 35 in all, passing on both controllers.
- Money Factory (*Fábrica de Dinheiro*), the phase 3 mode decision A1 deferred, built after the maintainer's go-ahead of 2026-10-23 (decisions MF-A1 to MF-A12, open; MF-A2, MF-A3, MF-A5 and MF-A6 for the maintainer; in software, not yet on real boxes, NFC tags or radios, Wokwi not run): `modes/money_factory/`. Teams capture **factory** boxes as in Domination; a factory makes `production` money every `tick_s` up to `capacity` while held, and a player of its team scans their tag to take what it holds, up to `carry_max`. At a **bank** box a player banks all they carry: as many points for their team. A1's blocker, state shared between boxes, lives in the controller's `field.lua`: each player's wallet, told to the banks (and factories) with the mode command `wallet` (with a counter, so an older word is ignored) and emptied when the player is healed at a medic kit (`drop_on_heal`); `field.lua` also tells the bank boxes what they are (`bank`: a script does not know its role, M5-5) and ends the game at an optional `target`. Events `money.collected` (33) and `money.deposited` (34) as specified since plan phase A. Seven YAML screens in Portuguese and English (`neutral`, `captured` and `final_score` reused for the box's OLED). The fourteenth Field Box built-in preset (the image grows by 18,400 bytes, 12 % of the OTA slot free); the GM console's and the app's event lines in four languages; the project page's modes page and roadmap in four languages; the glossary. 10 mode tests (simulator and Dart), 7 simulator, 2 controller, 1 browser, Field Box host tests with 8 reference images, Dart catalogue, event text and 4 golden tests.
- Finding the field on *Load the field from a map* by the GM's location or a place's name (the maintainer's request of 2026-10-23, decisions MF-41 to MF-49, open; in software, tested in Chromium with the position and Nominatim mocked, not yet against the live Nominatim nor on a phone at a field): *Use my location* asks the browser for one fresh fix (only in a secure context: `127.0.0.1`/`localhost` or the field's HTTPS address; otherwise the page says why) and moves the map there at zoom 17; with no map position on the level and the location already allowed, the page opens there by itself (a level's map position still wins, and nothing is asked on open). The search box takes a place's name, sent to OpenStreetMap's Nominatim only when the GM presses *Search* (no search as you type), at most once a second, the same question answered from the page's memory, with the page's origin as the Referer and the page's language; up to 5 results as a keyboard-accessible list, the map fitted to the chosen place's box, with the attribution (© OpenStreetMap contributors, ODbL); a typed `latitude, longitude` still goes straight there with no request. The GM's position never leaves the browser. The page's CSP gains `connect-src https://nominatim.openstreetmap.org` (that page only, MF-45), it alone gets `Permissions-Policy: geolocation=(self)`, and every other console page (also the phone controller's copy) `geolocation=()` (MF-43). Strings in pt-PT, English, German and Traditional Chinese (the last two AI drafts). The maintainer decided on the public Nominatim knowingly, after its usage policy and its rule for AI-written code (MF-44); the requests also give the project's contact address (`email=`), Nominatim's own way to name an application a browser cannot name by its User-Agent.
- The German and Traditional Chinese project pages say they were translated by an AI model and not yet reviewed, and ask players who speak them to help (the maintainer's answers to P5H-11 and P5K-12, 2026-10-23: publish now). The maintainer accepted every other open decision as recommended, to review later; Taiwan's LoRa band (AS923) is planned (RF-AS923-1).
- Traditional Chinese as written in Taiwan (`zh-tw`), the fourth language (phase 5 plan phase 11, after P5R-10; decisions P5K-1 to P5K-12; drafted by an AI model, **not yet reviewed by a native-speaking player**, `docs/translations/status.yaml`): the project page at `/zh-tw/` (system CJK fonts, lines broken between Han letters, the app's Chinese screenshots), the GM console (also the scoreboard, poster and field map; a decimal point), the mode editor (with Blockly 13.3.0's own `msg/zh-hant.js`, vendored byte for byte with its checksum), the app (Android's own CJK font in Taiwan's forms from the `zh-Hant-TW` locale, no font in the APK; Noto Sans TC subsets under `app/test/fonts/` for the golden images only) and the Field Box's own screens (join key 15 `zh-tw` or `zh-hant`, no protocol change, older firmware shows English; the controller's box-language setting offers it). The box's fonts gain exactly the 153 letters of its Chinese texts from Noto Sans TC 2.004 Medium (OFL-1.1), 17 of them at 48 px, taken from `box_text.c` by `tools/gen_box_fonts.py`, whose new `--check` (in `tools/check.sh`) fails when a text uses a letter the fonts lack; the host screen tests now check every letter of every label against its own font, so a missing letter fails instead of drawing an empty box. The four box fonts are now compressed (lossless RLE, `LV_USE_FONT_COMPRESSED`; every reference image identical): the Field Box image is 0x1bf220, 1 KB more than before, 13 % of its slot free (uncompressed, the Chinese letters cost 117 KB and left 7 %); the modem +30 KB. 43 new `_zh-tw` reference images (24 TFT, 19 OLED); the OLED's 14 px lines are dense in Chinese, for the reviewer to judge on a panel (P5K-4). Chinese names a GM types (teams, presets, objectives) are not in the box's fonts (P5K-5). Also: the console's scenario blocks showed Blockly's Portuguese menus in German (now English for every language but Portuguese); the app's language picker is a row of chips (four languages broke the segmented button at 360 px); `tools/i18n_check.py` reads `strings_zh_tw.dart` and `BOX_LANG_ZH_TW`.
- Build guides for the Satellite, the I/O pod and the target addon (phase 5 plan phase 12, P5R-14, decisions P5J-1 to P5J-14; text only: no box has been built from them yet, photos come with HW2): `hardware/satellite/BUILD.md` (the Field Box's board and firmware with two or three team buttons, the LED ring through the 74AHCT125 and the board's OLED, one protected 18650; it plays the games a field controller invites it into, adopted by cable or pairing code; the specification's piezo buzzer is not supported by the firmware, so sound is the optional amplifier), `hardware/io-pod/BUILD.md` (the Field Box's parts without the colour screen, plus the enclosure switch on GPIO 47; the pod firmware built with `python tools/docker_fw.py pod` and flashed from `build-pod`, its log on GPIO 43), and `hardware/target-addon/BUILD.md` (what is decided and what is not, with no wiring: **not buildable yet**; among the open questions, the port's bus voltage and that a Field Box has no free GPIO for the addon's interrupt line), each with safety notes (cells, enclosures, airsoft hits) and `<!-- PHOTO: ... -->` placeholders. A new project page renders them shortened in Portuguese, English and German (`/montagem/`, `/en/build-guides/`, `/de/bauen/`, in the header), with the safety rules every build shares. `tools/check_wiring.py` now checks the guides' and the page's pin tables against the Kconfig defaults. Also fixed: the German main page scrolled sideways on phones (its hero title).
- German, the third language (phase 5 plan phase 10, the maintainer's P5R-10; decisions P5H-1 to P5H-11; drafted by an AI model, **not yet reviewed by a native-speaking player**, `docs/translations/status.yaml`): the project page at `/de/`, the GM console, the mode editor (with Blockly's German), the app, and the Field Box's own screens (join key 15 `de`; the fonts gain Ä Ö Ü ä ö ü ß, +9.2 KB; older firmware shows English). The controller's box-language setting offers German. A German text the tables lack shows in English. `tools/i18n_check.py` (in `tools/check.sh`) checks every translated part against English; missing German keys are warnings, while the project page must stay complete in every language. `docs/translations/` (process, glossary, status) for community translators. Mode names and screens, and the site server's pages, are not in German yet. The texts added since the draft (the console's scenario blocks and hidden-areas notice, the app's phone controller screen and out-of-bounds warning) were filled in the same way on 2026-10-22.
- The visual mode editor makes the mode's package itself (the leftover of phase 5 plan phases 3-6, decisions P5I-1 to P5I-6, open; in software, tested in Chromium): *Make the package* downloads the canonical, unsigned archive of the mode being edited (`<id>-<version>.tar`: `mode.yaml`, `node.lua`, `field.lua` when there is one and the Mode tab's screens), made in the page by `simulator/atalaia_sim/editor/package.js` and byte for byte what `tools/mode_package.py pack` makes of the same files (proved against the Python packer for the shared vectors of `protocol/vectors/mode_package.json`, every built-in mode, the editor's three examples and 18 edge cases refused or taken alike). Its panel shows the manifest a mode key will sign (id, version, kind, tier, size, SHA-256) and the exact `tools/mode_package.py sign` and `verify` commands, and `keygen` for a first key; signing stays offline on the command line, and no key reaches the page, the browser's storage or the simulator. Refused, with the reason, while the editor lists problems, or for a version, licence, id or size a package cannot carry; the panel goes when the files change. In English, Portuguese and German (draft); the editor's Content-Security-Policy is unchanged; 5 new browser tests (`python3 tools/docker_editor.py`).
- Scenario blocks in the GM console (phase 5 plan phase 7, ADR-P5-1 and P5-2 as amended, decisions P5R-2 and P5F-1 to P5F-12; in software, tested against the real controller in Chromium, not yet on a real phone at a field): the *Cenários / Scenarios* tab's editor has a *Blocos / Blocks* view beside its YAML, the same manifest as Blockly blocks: the scenario (id, version, name, the boxes' screen language, squads), its texts, teams, parameters, boxes and players, and objectives with who and when (team and squad, who sees it, after another is completed, opening and closing minutes), how (holds and fuses in seconds, a code, tags, a destination circle or area, lives) and what they give (points, a parameter's points, the win, reveals). Mouse, touch or keyboard alone (Blockly 13's keyboard navigation and screen-reader labels, a help line), European Portuguese and English, a phone's width. The YAML stays the editor's text: each change to the blocks writes it again and the controller checks, compiles and installs it as before; its problems are outlined on their blocks, with *Show*; the blocks refuse a manifest they cannot hold as it is instead of rewriting it, and a text's comments stay until a block changes. The controller's `POST /api/v1/scenarios/check` also answers `manifest`, the manifest as it read it, so the browser never parses YAML. Blockly 13.3.0 is vendored in the console (the mode editor's bytes, checksummed, Apache-2.0), loaded only when the view opens and precached for offline use; the console's Content-Security-Policy still has no `'unsafe-inline'`: it allows only the SHA-256 of the two stylesheets Blockly writes. Every scenario example (the two hand-written manifests, the console's example, the five valid vectors) made into blocks and written back compiles to the hand-written package byte for byte (`controller/console/tests/test_scenario_blocks.py`, 7 browser tests)
- The community mode registry's template and processes (phase 5 plan phases 8 and 9, ADR-P5-3, P5-4 and P5-8 as amended, decisions P5G-1 to P5G-14, open; in software: the `atalaia-modes` repository itself waits for the maintainer, P5R-9): `registry/template/` holds the registry repository's files (an empty `index.json`, `keys/` with `revoked.json`, `modes/` for each mode's source and tests, `REUSE.toml`, CODEOWNERS, pull request and issue templates) and its CI workflow, which runs this repository's tools at a pinned ref: `registry-lint` (the JSON Schemas, then `tools/registry_check.py lint`: keys and owners, the author's signature on every version and the project's on verified ones, every package check, the source packing to exactly the newest signed archive, SPDX headers naming the package's licence, no names the sandbox removes in the Lua, assets at most 512 KiB, the same `i18n:` keys in every language, reserved `org.atalaia.` ids, one owner per id, and on a pull request that no published version changed; then REUSE), `registry-test` (each mode's tests in the simulator), `sandbox-escape` (the core's battery), then publishing `index.json`, `revoked.json`, `keys.json`, the packages and a badge per mode to GitHub Pages. The **verified badge** is a second signature, by the project's registry key (the one Field Boxes have built in), over the same archive the author signed (`tools/registry_check.py badge`); authors list their modes with `registry_check.py key` and `add`. `registry/tests/` runs every check step of that workflow as written on a sample submission (`registry/examples/org.example.hill/`) signed with throwaway keys made at run time, and checks the refusals (18 tests; `tools/check.sh` and CI's new `registry-template` job). `CONTRIBUTING.md` gains "Submitting a community mode"; `docs/community/` explains the registry, the review checklist and the badge's criteria, mode keys (keeping, rotating, revoking) and moderation (reports, takedowns, ids and owners, appeals); a draft RFC records version 1 of the package format, the manifest and the index (`docs/rfcs/0000-mode-packages-and-registry.md`). AC-6 met in this repository's template, AC-11 met.
- Missing modes sent to boxes from the GM console, the controller, console and protocol side (phase 5 plan phase 13a, the maintainer's P5R-8, decisions P5B-8 to P5B-17 and P5D-1 to P5D-12; in software, tested against the real controller with a model of the box and in Chromium; the box's side is plan phase 13b): in the invite dialog, under a box that answered *no mode*, the parts of the game it would run with what the controller can send (packages signed with a key built into the boxes' firmware, `[modes] box_keys`) or what to do, and *Send missing modes*, which invites it again with join key 16 (`fetch_modes`, with keys 13 and 14). The box then fetches each part it lacks over the field Wi-Fi, HTTPS pinned to the field CA, from `GET /api/v1/mode-packages/{id}/release` and `.../package` (no token, only a part of the live game's mode, only while it is ready), checks it with its own keys and sandbox, reports (`POST /api/v1/mode-packages/report`, an HMAC with the game key, or the event `node.modes_failed` over LoRa) and only then says HELLO; its progress shows in the dialog, and the game waits for it (`[modes] fetch_timeout_s`, 300 s; *Stop waiting*). The Boxes tab lists each box's fetched modes and can make a box forget them at its next invite (join key 17). The wire contract for the box (the check order, every refusal code, the report, the event, storage limits) is [protocol/mode-packages.md](protocol/mode-packages.md), "Fetching missing modes"; shared vectors `box_checks` and `reports` in `protocol/vectors/mode_package.json` and `mode_fetch` in `protocol/vectors/join.json`. `GET|DELETE /api/v1/mode-fetch`, `PUT /api/v1/mode-fetch/forget/{device}`; 13 controller tests, 2 package-vector tests, 3 join-codec tests, 2 simulator lobby tests, 1 browser test.
- The field loaded from a map, and the field boundary (the maintainer's idea MF-18, built as decisions MF-19 to MF-30; in software, tested against the real controller and in Chromium with the map services mocked, not yet at a real field nor against the live services in a browser): a new map feature type, the **field boundary** (`boundary`, *Limite do campo*: an area of 3-12 corners, at most one per level, of no team, for everyone by default), the plan dimmed outside it in the console and a closed shape without a fill on ATAK; and *Carregar o campo de um mapa / Load the field from a map* in the Field tab, a page of its own where the GM, with internet while setting the game up, finds the field on an OpenStreetMap map (a small hand-written viewer, no library), marks its outline's corners (pressing the map, the arrow keys and a cross, or typed `latitude, longitude`), and the GM's browser draws the field image once: OpenStreetMap's data for the area from the Overpass API over, in mainland Portugal, the DGT's OrtoSat 2023 orthophoto (its open WMS), with "Ortofotomapas © DGT (CC BY 4.0)" and "© OpenStreetMap contributors" burned into a corner. One request, `POST /api/v1/field/levels/{n}/from-map`, then sets the level's plan, its map position (exact, from the image's edges) and its boundary in one transaction. The controller never goes online; only that page may reach the three services (its own Content-Security-Policy); Google Maps is not used. An out-of-bounds warning on phones is recorded for later.
- Field Boxes get the signed mode packages a game needs and they lack from their field controller, between games (phase 5 plan phase 13b, the box's side of the maintainer's P5R-8; decisions P5E-1 to P5E-14; in software and host tests, on real boxes in HW3): a join with key 16 (`fetch_modes`), keys 13 and 14 and a mode key built into the firmware (`CONFIG_ATALAIA_MODE_KEYS`, new) makes the box fetch each missing part over the field Wi-Fi and HTTPS pinned to the field CA (the firmware update's), check it in the contract's order of `protocol/mode-packages.md` (the release text, Ed25519 with its mode keys then its release keys, kind, tier, never a built-in id, at most 256 KiB, room, the archive hashed into a file before any of it is read, its canonical ustar form, the script loaded in a fresh sandbox with the box's heap, its screens parsed, the composed game within 16 KB), keep it on LittleFS (`/littlefs/modes/<id>/`, at most 8 packages and 512 KiB, the least recently used dropped, commits by renames finished or forgotten after a power loss, every kept release re-verified at boot), report each part (`POST /api/v1/mode-packages/report`, HMAC with the game key) and only then say HELLO; a failure the controller could not hear over HTTPS goes over LoRa as the new standard event `node.modes_failed` (id 53, data `mode`, key 38), and the box is free again without counting the game as ended. Key 17 (`forget_modes`) forgets every fetched package; `@modes` and `@forgetmodes` on the serial console. New: `firmware/esp32/main/mode_package.c`, `mode_fetch.c`, `mode_store.c`, `mode_fetch_esp.c`; the core's join codec reads and writes keys 16 and 17 (true only); `box_host --mode-keys --mode-source` for end-to-end tests; the firmware host tests also run under AddressSanitizer and UBSan in CI's `sanitizers` job. The Field Box image grows by 16 KB (0.9 %) and its static RAM by 1 KB; the fetch's buffers live on the heap only while it runs.
- The out-of-bounds warning on players' phones (decisions MF-31 to MF-40, following MF-30; in software, not yet on a real phone at a real field): while a player shares their position in a running game, the app tests the phone's own fixes against the field boundary (the `boundary` map feature the player's token may already read, `GET /api/v1/field/features`, placed on the ground by its level's map position, `GET /api/v1/field/levels/{n}/georef`; fetched again when the stream's `map_features` changes it). A fix is judged only when its accuracy is 50 m or better, counts as outside only beyond its own accuracy and 10 m, and the warning comes when the fixes have stayed outside for 5 s; one fix inside ends it. The player sees a banner (*Fora do campo* / *Out of bounds*, announced by screen readers), feels three long pulses (again every minute while out, one short pulse on coming back) and the sharing notification says it too; no sound. Players, squad leaders and VIPs only (not marshals, the GM's phone or spectators); with the level not known on a multi-level field, out means outside every level's boundary. Nothing about it leaves the phone (no event, no report field, no request), no extra GPS fix is taken, and the controller is unchanged; a boundary for *only the organisers* warns nobody. `app/lib/position/field_bounds.dart`; `app/test/position/field_bounds_test.dart` (11 tests, with geo.json's polygon cases).
- Map features on the field's plan (the maintainer's request of 2026-10-22, decisions MF-0 to MF-16; in software, tested against the real controller and in Chromium): besides the boxes, the GM marks safe zones, a team's respawn or HQ, the car park, first aid stations, objectives and anything else with a name, each as a pin, a circle (a pin and a radius in metres, dragged to scale when the plan has its map position) or an area traced corner by corner, on any level of a multi-story field, from the console's *Campo / Field* tab (*Marcações no mapa*), by mouse, touch or keyboard, in Portuguese and English, with a legend and type and team colours. Each feature says who may see it, everyone, one team or only the organisers (a respawn or HQ is its team's by default), and the controller enforces it on every path: `GET|POST /api/v1/field/features`, `PUT|DELETE /api/v1/field/features/{id}` (writes GM only), the stream's new `map_features` message filtered per connection, and the ATAK feed, where pins are spot markers, circles ATAK's drawn circles (`u-d-c-c`) and areas closed drawn shapes (`u-d-f`), only the features for everyone go on the field's Wi-Fi, the staff's devices get them all, and the remote relay gets pins and circles for everyone (`protocol/vectors/cot.json` cites the TAK examples and ATAK's source for both shapes). Features belong to the field (store schema 11) and go with a level that is removed. Not on the Android controller yet, nor synced to a site server.
- Signed mode packages on the field controller (phase 5 plan phase 6, decisions P5C-17 to P5C-21; in software, tested against the real controller and in Chromium): the GM uploads a package signed with one of the controller's mode keys (`[modes] keys`), or installs one from the field's registry index (`[modes] index`: `https://`, or an `index.json` on disk for a field without internet), in a new *Signed mode packages* panel of the console's *Presets* tab (English and Portuguese). Each is verified (signature, revocation by `[modes] revoked` and the index's `revoked.json`, size, archive, licence) and fails closed before the usual upload checks; the store (schema 10) keeps its release and archive as signed; the modes list shows who signed each package, *not reviewed by the project* for uploads and unverified index entries, and when its key is revoked or no longer trusted. `GET|POST /api/v1/mode-packages`, `POST /api/v1/mode-packages/install`; 9 controller tests, 1 browser test.
- The sandbox-escape battery (phase 5 plan phase 5, ADR-P5-8, decisions P5R-13, P5C-14 to P5C-16; AC-12): `core/tests/test_sandbox_escape.c`, 43 escape attempts run as box scripts (64 KB and 256 KB) and as field controller scripts, under ASan and UBSan in CI's new `sandbox-escape` job, and `simulator/tests/test_sandbox_escape.py` through the ctypes bindings and the simulator. It found two ways for a script to run with the instruction watchdog off, now closed in the core (so on every box, controller and phone): `__gc` finalizers (`setmetatable` now refuses a metatable with `__gc`) and `xpcall`'s message handler after the watchdog's own error (the handler now runs after unwinding, with the watchdog on); and the simulator's field ops no longer let a host exception reach the core as an undefined answer.
- Signed mode packages (phase 5 plan phase 4, ADR-P5-3 and P5-4 as amended, decisions P5R-7, P5B-10, P5C-8 to P5C-13; in software): [protocol/mode-packages.md](protocol/mode-packages.md). A package is one canonical ustar archive of a mode folder's `mode.yaml`, scripts and `assets/` under its id (sorted, fixed times and owners, so its SHA-256 is stable; readers refuse every other form, so no path traversal, links or hidden data); a mode key signs, with Ed25519 as firmware releases, the manifest `atalaia-mode-package-v1` (id, version, kind, minimum tier, size, SHA-256); checks in a fixed order that fail closed (size, hash, a trusted key, not revoked, the archive's form, its own `mode.yaml` agreeing, an approved licence). `tools/mode_package.py` (`pack`, `keygen`, `pubkey`, `sign`, `verify`, `index`), the shared `controller/atalaia_controller/mode_package.py`, the registry index's and revocation list's JSON Schemas (`registry/schema/`, checked in CI with examples), shared vectors `protocol/vectors/mode_package.json` with RFC 8032's keys and 28 refused archives (`tools/mode_package_vectors.py --check`); 17 tests. AC-4 and AC-5 met in software.
- The visual mode editor's remaining blocks (phase 5 plan phase 3, decisions P5C-1 to P5C-7; in software): *Places* (`geo.distance`, `geo.in_zone` with a circle or an area of 3-32 corners, whether an event has a position, and the position's latitude, longitude, accuracy, player and role), *GM commands* (`commands:` in `mode.yaml` with their roles and English and Portuguese labels, and "when the mode command ... arrives", a `cmd.mode` handler for one name), the controller's "send the mode command ... to box ...", a list's item; the *Mode* tab's kind (main mode, modifier, role package), several roles each with its inputs (those the box script reacts to, or its own) and *needed to start*, and the mode's screen files offered in the toolbox; problems for a role without its inputs, an unknown role, screen or command, an area with too few corners. A third example, Master and Satellites (two roles, its screens, the controller's status command), passes all 8 of the hand-written mode's tests; *Test in the simulator* starts a box for each role. 24 browser tests (`python3 tools/docker_editor.py`).
- A controller that restarted without the game's key can still end it on its boxes (the maintainer's decision OT-7, built as OT-13 to OT-16; RFC 0001 Amendment 7's addendum of 2026-10-22, proposed; in software, tested with the C host box through the C host modem and in Chromium, not yet on the bench boards): a sealed GAME_OVER field frame (kind 4: the game id and its key's tag, which the controller keeps beside the game) sent three times on the game's channel after every game a controller ends, the only way for a game whose key was lost; a box in that game ends it as on CMD end and announces itself free; another game's, or its id under another key (an old one played back), changes nothing; no timeout ever ends a game on a box. The console's game card says when the key was lost, its End asks to tell the boxes over the field radio, and says whether it did (`game_over_frame`). Shared vectors for C and Python.
- The admin PIN's guesses are slowed for good, and a Field Box has no default PIN (the maintainer's decision AD-28, built as AD-29 to AD-32; in software and host reference images, not yet on the bench boards): wrong PINs are counted in NVS across restarts; after five in a row each try waits 1, 2, 4 ... 60 minutes ("PIN locked · try again in N min", in the box's language), a restart starting the whole wait again; one check for the game picker and adoption by card or cable; the setup portal refuses 1234, 0000, sequences and repeated pairs; a box with none set refuses the picker and re-adoption (`no_pin`) until `@admin-pin <PIN>` on its USB console; bench and Wokwi builds keep 1234 and log it.

- Multi-story fields in the GM console's *Campo / Field* tab (the maintainer's request of 2026-10-22; decisions ML-0 to ML-12; in the controller and the console, tested against the real controller and in Chromium, not yet at a real field): a *Multi-story building* checkbox, the number of levels (2-10) and their names (filled in as "R/C", "1.º andar", ... or "Ground floor", "Floor 1", ...; a basement is a level named so), a floor plan per level (the same types and 4 MB limit as the field image) on a tab per level with the boxes on that level, and *Each box's level* to move a box to another level. Turning levels off asks first and keeps everything; fewer levels delete the plans that go, after asking, and move their boxes to level 0. API: `GET|PUT /api/v1/field/levels`, `GET|PUT|DELETE /api/v1/field/levels/{n}/image` and `/georef`; the old single-image endpoints are level 0; node positions carry `level` (PUT, GET and the stream's `position`); a player's position report may carry `level` (kept until the phone changes it). Store schema 9 moves the one field image to level 0 and gives every older position level 0. A level shares level 0's georeference unless it has its own points; the ATAK feed places each box by its level's and names the level in its remarks (the relayed CoT form is unchanged). A scenario's box may say its `level`. Field images and levels stay on the controller (not synced)
- The Android controller role, first slice (decisions PE24-1 to PE24-9, [docs/phase-3-android-players/PLAN.md](docs/phase-3-android-players/PLAN.md) "PE24 slices"; in software, no screen yet, not on a phone): the app's game engine (`app/lib/controller/`: the radio side of a game ported from the simulator's FieldController, games created, started, paused, resumed and ended, the mode's `field.lua` on the shared core with the field operations, merged scores, the event log, node commands) and its REST API with the Pi controller's paths, tokens and status codes, on the loopback address only; the core's EVENT decoder bound for Dart. Shared contract cases in `protocol/vectors/controller_api.json` (8 cases: tokens, a Domination game from creation to its end with a box's packets, Hold-All's and Last Stand's `field.lua`, the time limit, gm_only data, refused requests) run against both controllers (`controller/tests/test_api_contract.py`, `app/test/controller/api_contract_test.dart`). The stream, keeping games across a restart, the radio through a pod, Wi-Fi props, check-in and the controller's screens are the next slices.
- Testing a mode from the visual editor in the simulator (phase 5 plan phase 2, decisions P5B-1 to P5B-7; in software): the editor's *Test in the simulator* (with a number of boxes) sends the generated `node.lua`, `field.lua` and `mode.yaml` to the simulator's new `POST /api/reload`, which loads them in the core first (on an error the game that was running goes on), then starts the game; *Open the game* shows it in the simulator's page, which now has a *Field controller* panel for the mode's `field.lua` (already run, not shown before). Errors the scripts hit, on loading or while the game runs, come back to the editor's problems list and onto the blocks they come from. The same server rules (local host names, JSON only, CSP `'self'`), 1 MB at most; 7 new simulator tests and 3 new browser tests (`python3 tools/docker_editor.py`).
- Planned: boxes getting the modes a game needs from the GM console (the maintainer's decision on P5R-8; phase 5 plan phase 13, decisions P5B-8 to P5B-17, open; not built): *Send missing modes* in the invite dialog, signed packages fetched over the field Wi-Fi and checked with keys built into the box's firmware and the sandbox, only between games; decision K10 amended for that path (`docs/phase-5-community/ADR.md`, "Note 2026-10-22: P5R-8").
- The Android controller role, second slice (decisions PE24-10 to PE24-15; in software, no screen yet, not on a phone): the live stream `WS /api/v1/stream` on the app's controller with the Pi controller's hello snapshot, messages in the same order, gm_only data for the GM token only, ping, 4401 for an unknown token and 4408 for a client that falls behind (it reconnects for a new hello; the Pi has no `since` replay either, PE24-11), and the Pi bridge's MQTT topics and payloads on the app's embedded broker (events without gm_only data, the score and the nodes retained, commands from `cmd/`), which now takes logins (`gm` and `reader`, the GM and read tokens as passwords, PE24-12) with the Pi's ACL (`controller/deploy/atalaia.acl`) and listens on the loopback address by default (PE24-13). The shared contract cases gained stream and MQTT steps and an ACL table (5 new cases, 13 in all, and 17 ACL checks), run against both controllers.
- The Android controller role, third slice (decisions PE24-40 to PE24-44; in software, no screen yet, not on a phone): the app's controller keeps its games in files (`FileControllerStore`: games, the event log, scores, nodes and settings in JSON files in one directory, written through, whole files replaced by rename, the log appended, a last line cut short by a crash dropped) and takes the live game up again after a restart as the Pi controller does: its clock (saved every 5 s and at start, pause and end), each box's counters, the GM's adjustments, the nodes with their link and battery state, and `field.lua` run again with its earlier points; the game key is kept only with `persist_game_key` (on by default, decision OT-7), else the game comes back `key_lost` and can only be ended. The shared contract cases gained a `restart` step and a `persist_game_key` case setting (4 new cases, 17 in all), run against both controllers.
- The Android controller role, fourth slice (decisions PE24-45 to PE24-53; in software, tested with the I/O pod's and the Field Box's own C code on one machine, not on a real pod, box or phone): an I/O pod is the phone controller's radio (`app/lib/controller/pod_modem.dart`: the pod's RADIO_* frames as the Pi's USB modem, the game's CONFIGURE again on every new link, pings) and the controller's packets carry a nonce epoch per game key, stored before use, as on the Pi; the phone issues join payloads (`POST /api/v1/join`, `/nodes/{id}/join`, the boxes' language) sealed with the field join key, never twice for one node id; Android props link to it over Wi-Fi (`WS /api/v1/prop`, `controller_props.dart`, events checked against the schema with the core's own tables), and without a radio its commands still reach them. The shared contract cases gained radio, join and prop steps (4 new cases, 21 in all) and known answers for the prop link's proof (`protocol/vectors/prop_link.json`), run against both controllers; new end-to-end tests run the phone's engine with the host pod as its radio and host boxes on a UDP air (a game joined, played and ended, a restart mid-game, Hold-All on two boxes), for which `tools/docker_app.py` and CI's `flutter-test` job now build the host box too. The field's channel choice moves to slice S7 with the channel scan (PE24-52).
