- The Android controller role, fifth slice (decisions PE24-54 to PE24-60; in software, no screen yet, not on a phone): field check-in on the app's controller as on the Pi (`app/lib/controller/controller_checkin.dart`): the poster's settings (no text until the phone serves HTTPS), open games, field identities, player tokens signed with a key the phone makes per game and checked against the check-in on every request, declarations, the staff's list, manual check-ins and changes (a new team, squad or role closes the player's stream with 4409 for a new token), Ed25519-signed chrono series from marshals' registered keys marking replicas passed or refused, the replicas-to-chrono list, the CSV, erasure and the retention purge; player positions (`controller_positions.dart`) with the Pi's visibility rules, the level kept from a player's last report, a team's temporary view, a VIP's GPS to `field.lua`, gone when the game ends; player tokens on the REST API and the stream (the player's hello with its own check-in and the positions it may see, names to staff only); all of it kept across a restart. The shared contract cases gained a fixed wall clock, player tokens in steps and a closed-stream step (7 new cases, 28 in all), run against both controllers; they found the Pi controller's poster settings unchecked with HTTPS off (below).
- The Android controller role, sixth slice: the phone as the field controller (decisions PE24-61 to PE24-69; in software, tested on one machine with the real core, sockets and TLS and the I/O pod's own code, the screen with the phone's plugins replaced by fakes; an emulator test written for CI; not yet on a phone): *Controlador de campo* / *Field controller* on the app's home screen (Android only) starts the controller on its own isolate (the store in the app's private storage, the game engine on the core, check-in, positions, the API, the MQTT broker), under the foreground service with its wake and Wi-Fi locks, whose notification follows the live game; while it runs the app's Flutter engine outlives its activity, so the back button or a swipe away no longer stops a controller or a prop's game. The GM console is the Pi's own `controller/console/src`, copied into the APK at build time (`app/tool/sync_console.dart`, checked byte for byte) and opened in the app's web view on the phone's loopback address, logged in with the GM token. Opened to the field's Wi-Fi, the phone serves HTTPS on port 8443 with a field CA it makes itself as the Pi does (ECDSA P-256, a new server certificate when its address changes), whose fingerprint goes on the check-in poster and into join payloads for Android props, and MQTT on port 1883; closed, nothing listens on the network. The screen shows the console's addresses, the CA's fingerprint, the GM and read codes (hidden until asked), links an I/O pod as the radio, and helps with the field's Wi-Fi: the network and the hotspot's state, Android's settings, or a network the app makes for the game with a QR code players scan to join it; in European Portuguese and English, laid out for 320-pixel-wide screens, checked against Android's accessibility guidelines. New dependency: pointycastle 4.0.0.
- The GM console on a phone controller, slice S6's follow-ups (decisions PE24-70 to PE24-73; in software, tested in Chromium against the real controller and in the app's Dart tests, not yet on a phone): the console takes its token from the address's fragment (`/console/#token=...`, optionally `&lang=en`), as the scoreboard does, stores it as a typed token once the controller accepts it and removes it from the address at once, so it does not stay in the address bar or the history; the app opens its own console that way instead of writing the console's storage. `GET /api/v1/whoami` now lists the console's areas the controller serves (`features`; an API addition on both controllers, with a shared contract case): the Pi lists them all, the phone check-in, joins, the boxes' language and players' positions, and the console hides the tabs, panels and requests of the rest (the field map, adoption, presets and mode packages, scenarios, the radio channel, branding and team crests, inviting boxes, firmware, ATAK, the schedule, standby controllers) instead of showing their errors, with a note in the *Controlador / Controller* tab naming what this controller does not serve yet. In the app's web view, a short note says that printing the check-in poster and saving the CSV need a computer's browser.
- The Android controller role, slice S7b: presets, mode packages, scenarios and team branding on the phone (pulled forward from S7 by the maintainer on 2026-10-23; decisions PE24-80 to PE24-89; in software, tested against the real core and the Pi controller's own data, not yet on a phone): presets (`GET|POST /api/v1/presets`, a game made from one), mode uploads checked in the core (`GET|POST /api/v1/modes`) and games of a main mode with modifiers and role packages, or led by a scenario, each box's join naming only what it runs; signed mode packages uploaded or installed from the registry index with its revocations (`app/lib/controller/mode_package.dart`, the Dart port of `mode_package.py`, checked against `protocol/vectors/mode_package.json`), with the `[modes]` settings (trusted keys, the boxes' keys, revoked ids, the index) in the app's *Definições*; the modes sent to boxes (release and package without a token between games, reports with the game key's HMAC, a HELLO ending a fetch only with reports for every part, join keys 16 and 17 in the app's join codec); scenarios checked, compiled and installed on the phone (the validator and compiler ported to Dart: the same problems for `protocol/vectors/scenario.json` and the same `field.lua` and `mode.yaml`, byte for byte, for the new `protocol/vectors/scenario_compile.json`, made by `tools/scenario_compile_vectors.py` from the Python compiler); team branding (the field's name, accent, logo and wallpaper, crests, saved teams, a game's team names and crests, `branding` in the stream's hello; images checked and stripped as the Pi's `images.py`, byte for byte). The phone's `whoami` lists `presets`, `branding`, `scenarios` and `mode_packages`, so the GM console shows their tabs and panels. Boxes invited over the radio (slice S7c) can be sent the modes they lack. 11 new shared contract cases (56 in all) pass on both controllers.
- The visual mode editor (phase 5 plan phase 1, ADR-P5-1 and P5-2 as amended 2026-10-22, decisions P5R-1 to P5R-14 and P5A-1 to P5A-7; in software): a page of the simulator at `/editor/` (`python -m atalaia_sim serve ...`, then `http://localhost:8000/editor/`) where a mode is built from Blockly blocks, one workspace for the boxes' `node.lua` and one for the field controller's `field.lua`, plus a *Mode* tab; 37 blocks for the script API (memory with `state.node{}` / `state.field{}`, events and their values, `emit` with a priority, timers kept to stop them, game, score, the box's screen, sound and team display, settings, `rand`, `log`, and `field.nodes`, `owner`, `common_owner`, `send`, `score`) with Blockly's logic, numbers, text, lists, variables and functions. The generator (Blockly's `LuaGenerator` class) writes readable Lua in the modes' style (licence header, the memory first, `local function`s before their callers, 2-space indents, double quotes, `on("game.start", show)`) and a `mode.yaml` whose role needs come from the blocks used and whose settings come from "setting ... is a ..." blocks with English and Portuguese labels; problems point at their block. Blocks save as `atalaia-mode-editor` JSON files, with a draft kept in the browser. English and European Portuguese (Blockly's own `msg/pt.js` for its blocks). Blockly 13.3.0 vendored unmodified with checksums (Apache-2.0), no network needed; `/editor/` has its own Content-Security-Policy (inline styles for Blockly, scripts from the simulator only). Examples: Domination and Hold-All Domination as blocks, whose generated files pass every test of the hand-written modes and give their manifests. Tests: 15 in headless Chromium (`simulator/editor_tests/`, `python tools/docker_editor.py`, new CI job `editor`) and 6 in `tools/check.sh` (`simulator/tests/test_editor_files.py`)
- Phase 5 design refresh (2026-10-22): every ADR of `docs/phase-5-community/` re-checked against phases 2-4 as built, with dated amendments (the editor in the simulator for modes and a block view of the console's scenario builder later; registry packages as controller packages since boxes run built-in scripts only; mode packages signed like firmware releases; each language domain in its built format; German, then Traditional Chinese); the plan re-cut into 12 software plan phases and 3 hardware ones
- The GM console's *Boxes for this game* dialog says when the game is ready and can start it (the maintainer's request on the bench; decisions GM-0 to GM-4; in the console, tested in Chromium against the real controller): *Ready to start: every role has a box* with the joined count per role, live as boxes join, or the roles it still waits for, and the mode's unmet minimums as warnings, all from the engine's start check the controller already pushes (no new endpoint); a *Start game* button (*Start anyway* while a role has no box) asks *Review the game's other settings first?*: *Review settings* closes the dialog and opens a new read-only *Game settings* section on the game card (the parameters the game was created with), *Start now* starts it through the existing start and its force path (logged). pt-PT and English, keyboard and screen-reader friendly
- Box screens in the field's language, English or European Portuguese (decisions BN-14 to BN-17, the maintainer's request; RFC 0001 Amendment 10 proposed; in software and in host reference images, not yet on the bench boards): the join payload's optional key 15 `lang`, the controller's *Box language* setting (by default the language of the console that creates the game; `GET|PUT /api/v1/settings/box-language`), carried by every join of the game; on the box a table of every text it draws itself (`main/box_text.c`), the modes' templates choosing their words by `lang` (Domination, Master and Satellites, Bomb, Target Windows), fonts with the Portuguese letters (`main/fonts/`, `tools/gen_box_fonts.py`), the language kept between games and after a reboot
- Field Box screens that follow the game (decisions BN-10 to BN-13, found on the bench): "Ready / next game" from boot on a free adopted box, "Waiting: the game hasn't started" from the join to the start, "Game on" at the start, a neutral zone's own screen ("Neutral: hold to capture") instead of the Bomb's standby, and Hold-All and Last Stand showing who holds the box
- The USB radio modem's own OLED shows its status (decisions MU-1 to MU-6; in software and in host reference images, not yet on the bench board): a 2 s splash with the tower, "ATALAIA", "MODEM MODE" and the version, then under a "MODEM MODE" header the link to the controller (`OK` while frames arrive within 12 s, `DOWN` after, `waiting` before the first), the game id or `IDLE`, the channel, TX and RX counts with TX failures and MIC failures (the controller's `tx`, `tx_fail`, `rx`, `rx_auth_fail`), the duty-cycle budget left, and `RADIO FAULT` with the reason when the SX1262 did not come up. A display task at priority 1 redraws twice a second when a line changes; the USB link and the radio never wait for it, and a board without an OLED logs once and runs as before. Kconfig `ATALAIA_MODEM_LINK_TIMEOUT_S` (12); the modem image grows from ~295 KB to ~483 KB (LVGL and its fonts)
- Adopting a box by its code over the radio, the third adoption method (decisions AD-15 to AD-27, RFC 0001 Amendment 8 proposed, [protocol/adoption.md](protocol/adoption.md) section 7; in software, tested with the C host box through the C host modem and in Chromium, not yet on the boards): an unadopted Field Box with a radio shows a 6-digit code at start (`PAIR THIS BOX`, on the TFT and the on-board OLED; `@pair` for a new one) and offers itself in **pair frames**, message type 10 (game id 0, never relayed, in the clear) on 869.525 MHz; in the console's *Adopt* tab, *Pair by code (radio)*, the GM finds the box and types its code, and the controller and the box run **CPace** (CPACE-X25519-SHA512, draft-irtf-cfrg-cpace-14) with it: the field join key is sent, sealed with AES-128-CCM under a key from the run's ISK (a nonce from no RNG: a sender id drawn per run, boot 1, count 0), only after the box's confirmation proves the same code, and the box adopts the field through the same rules as a card or the cable, then starts its field frames. A wrong code ends the run and burns the box's code; three failed runs make it wait a minute, ten stop pairing until a restart; a sid runs once; at most two answers per run; offers only while the code shows, every 15 s or more. The USB modem carries type 10 like field frames (IDENT `"pair": true`); with the field's channel elsewhere and no game live the controller listens on 869.525 MHz for five minutes. Box: `firmware/esp32/main/box_pair.c`, `cpace.c` (Monocypher's X25519, Elligator 2 and SHA-512); codec `core/src/pair_frame.c` and `simulator/atalaia_sim/pair_frame.py`, `cpace.py`; controller `pairing.py`, `GET`/`POST /api/v1/adopt/pair`, `POST /api/v1/adopt/pair/listen`, every run recorded with the other adoptions. Shared vectors `protocol/vectors/pairing.json` (`tools/pairing_vectors.py`): the CPace draft's Appendix B.1 and B.1.10 copied from the draft, a whole run whose REPLY and DONE the box reproduces byte for byte, 19 bodies and 6 frames to refuse
- Adopting boxes (decisions AD-1 to AD-14, [protocol/adoption.md](protocol/adoption.md); in software, tested with the C host box and in Chromium, not yet with a real board on the controller's USB or a PN532 reading a card): a new Field Box gets the field join key, now the field's master secret, once. **The NFC field card**: an NTAG215/216 with one MIME record `application/vnd.atalaia.field` (version, the field's name, the join key, an 8-byte HMAC check), made in the console's new *Adotar / Adopt* tab (`POST /api/v1/adopt/card`, GM token, never cached, with the warning that the card is the field's master secret) and tapped once on each new box. **The USB cable**: the controller finds the Field Boxes plugged into it (Espressif USB-Serial-JTAG ports but the modem's, and `[adopt] usb_ports`, e.g. a COM-to-TCP bridge's `socket://`), asks each `@adopt-info` (one `ATALAIA-ADOPT {json}` line: name, MAC, state), and after the GM confirms a box (checked again by its MAC) sends `@adopt <key> <PIN|-> <name>` (`POST /api/v1/adopt/usb/scan`, `POST /api/v1/adopt/usb`). The box's rules (`firmware/esp32/main/adoption.c`, Kconfig `ATALAIA_SERIAL_ADOPT`): an unadopted box takes the first field; an adopted box takes another field's key only with its admin PIN (on the keypad after the card, or in `@adopt`; five wrong ones lock it until a restart); nothing while a game is joined or played; a field card during a game is ignored, never a player tag; the screen shows ADOPTED with the field's name and key fingerprint (`XXXX-XXXX`), or the PIN screen. `@joinkey` and the setup portal's field follow the same rules. Every card and adoption is logged and recorded (last 100, `GET /api/v1/adopt`, never the key or a PIN). `python3 -m atalaia_controller join-key --fingerprint` and `--rotate` (a new key after a card is lost, the old fingerprint kept so the console tells boxes on the old key apart). `tools/serial_bridge.py` serves a COM port on TCP for a controller in Docker. Shared vectors `protocol/vectors/field_card.json` (4 valid cards, 18 invalid), checked by C (`test/test_adoption.c`) and Python (`simulator/atalaia_sim/field_card.py`); the host box takes `@adopt-info`, `@adopt`, `card <hex>`, `--admin-pin`, `--mac`. The third way, a pairing code over LoRa (CPace on X25519 with SHA-512; known answers from draft-irtf-cfrg-cpace-14), is designed in the protocol document and an RFC 0001 amendment proposal, to be built on the radio invites
- Boxes invited into the next game over the radio, and the start guard (found on the bench with real boards; decisions RJ-1 and RJ-2 by the maintainer, RJ-3 to RJ-15; RFC 0001 Amendment 7 proposed, `protocol/field-frames.md`; in software, in the firmware's host tests, with the C host box and host modem and in Chromium, not yet on the bench boards: BRING_UP step 10b2): **field frames** (message type 9, game id 0, never relayed) sealed with AES-128-CCM under HKDF-SHA256 of the field join key, with nonces that never depend on a random number generator (the sender's pseudonym, a box's boot count stored in NVS before each run or a controller's sender id fresh at every start, a frame count; decision RJ-15, the box's tokens and device id from entropy collected at boot with `bootloader_random_enable()`): a free box's ANNOUNCE (an 8-byte random device id kept in NVS, a token that changes every 10 minutes and on every accepted invite, its name and last role), the controller's DISCOVER, and an INVITE carrying a box's join payload in 1-3 parts of 153 bytes for its device id and token. A Field Box with no game or whose game is over announces itself on its last channel 2-20 s after the end and then within 2 % of its sub-band's duty cycle (about every 72 s on 869.525 MHz), answers a DISCOVER within 3 s, takes an invite through the card's checks (refusing it while in a game, with an old token, or for a game it played to the end; its token goes with every answer, join and game end, so a recorded invite never works later), answers, and shows "Invited"; 30 s after a game its final screen gives way to "Ready / next game" (new TFT and OLED references); `@lobby` on the console. HELLO carries the device id (key 10, optional; C, Python and Dart codecs, a new vector). The USB modem carries field frames as they are (FIELD_TX, FIELD_RX, FIELD_ACK, status `busy`, IDENT `"field": true`), with or without a game, and never holds the key. The controller lists the free boxes (`GET /api/v1/boxes`, `POST /api/v1/boxes/discover`) with the previous game's marked and invites them (`GET/POST /api/v1/games/{id}/invites`, retries, progress on the stream); the GM console's *Boxes for this game* dialog opens when a game is created, the previous game's boxes ticked, a role per box. A game starts only once each role it needs has a joined box or linked prop (`start_check` on the game, `GET /api/v1/games/{id}/start-check`; 409 otherwise); the console's *Start anyway*, after a confirmation, sends `{"force": true}`, logged and told to every console; a mode's minimums only warn. Shared vectors `protocol/vectors/field_frame.json` (RFC 5869's HKDF cases, 7 valid frames, 40 refusals, invite parts; `tools/field_frame_vectors.py`), checked by C (`core/tests/test_field_frame.c`) and Python
- Kconfig `ATALAIA_RADIO_LOG_PACKETS` (off by default, on in the bench variant): logs every packet sent (length, budget left) and received (length, RSSI), for BRING_UP.md's radio checks (decision RF-3)
- The remote ATAK relay through the site server (Phase 4 follow-up, decisions FU5-1 to FU5-20; ADR-P4-7's amendment, which M9-10 and S19-10 had deferred; in software, tested in Docker with TLS clients made from the data packages, the real controller through the real site server, and the single-field and hosted deployments through their published port, not yet with a real ATAK device, HW3, or on a real server). Off by default on both sides. The field controller's `[atak] remote = true` pushes its emitter's CoT events on the sync's WebSocket it opens itself (`cot` messages; player positions only with `remote_players = true`); the site server's `ATALAIA_ATAK_RELAY=on` relays them from a TLS listener in its process on port 8089 (`server/atalaia_server/atak_relay.py`), published straight from the server's container: TLS 1.2+ only, a client certificate required, signed by the field's CA and found in its list (not revoked, not expired, its holder still with a role on the field); the certificate's field picks the field and, in hosted mode, the tenant; props and zones to every enrolled device of the field, player positions only to a gm's or a marshal's certificate issued with that option (P4R18). Enrolment (`/api/v1/fields/{id}/atak`, the staff page `/staff/fields/{id}/atak`, `python -m atalaia_server atak issue|list|revoke`): the field's admin or gm in a TOTP session issues a certificate for a named device from a per-field CA (RSA 3072, its key sealed under a key derived from `ATALAIA_SECRET_KEY`, never exported), 7 days by default and 30 at most, handed out once as a TAK data package (connection, the relay's CA, the device's PKCS#12) with its password shown once and not stored; revocation closes the device's connection at once; every issue, revocation and connection is in the audit log. Migration `0012_atak` (`atak_cas`, `atak_certs`, personal data: access, export, erasure, purge). Shared vectors: 2 valid and 19 invalid stream messages (`hello` with `atak`, `cot`), each event checked on both sides by `sync_format.cot_event` (only the emitter's elements, with only its attributes in its forms). An independent review's fixes (FU5-13 to FU5-20): positions need an issuer with gm or marshal as well as such a holder; the issuer is in every connection's audit row (FU5-14, accepted by the maintainer); the relay's in-memory pictures are bounded (1,024 uids a field, 2 hours at most) and a field's controller sends at most 120 `cot` messages a minute; connections in their TLS handshake have their own caps (64, 4 per address, an IPv6 address by its /64) so they never crowd out enrolled devices; erasure scrubs the ATAK audit details; a CA's rotation and an erasure close connections at once; the 30 s check runs whatever loading the CAs does, one tenant's failure not stopping the others; two first certificates of a field at once share one CA.
- Hosted mode's deployment on the site server (Phase 4 follow-up, decisions FU4-1 to FU4-10; in software, tested in Docker on one computer with Caddy's own certificate authority, not yet on a real hosted server with a real domain, and hosted mode is not live before the legal review, P4-6): `server/docker-compose.hosted.yml` and `Caddyfile.hosted` on top of the deployment (every field at `https://<slug>.DOMAIN`, Caddy's on-demand certificates only for an active field's name, asked of `atalaia_server/tls_ask.py`, rate limited; the server in hosted mode as a role that owns nothing; `hub`, the operator's commands as the owner role, migrating every field before each start), `setup.sh DOMAIN --hosted` (two more passwords, the roles through `hosted-roles.sh`, the registry), `backup.sh` and `restore.sh` for a hub (owners and grants kept, a backup of the other mode refused, the isolation checked after the restore), `python -m atalaia_server hosted check`, and `python3 tools/docker_server.py deploy-hosted` (CI's `server-deploy-hosted`, with ShellCheck): two fields at their own names over HTTPS, an unknown name refused a certificate, the isolation as the server's role, a backup restored into a fresh hub with every table, owner, grant and policy compared
- Staff web pages on the site server (Phase 4 follow-up, decisions FU2-1 to FU2-12; in software, tested in headless Chromium against the real API and PostgreSQL in Docker, not yet on a real deployment): `/staff/` with login (password, then TOTP) and, per field, the game history and its settings, a game's score timeline and event log, the scenario library (check, save as revisions, archive) and presets, device health and the remote GM (live view, commands, log); pt-PT and English, phone-sized; same-origin scripts only (CSP unchanged), data only from the API with the bearer token; `python3 tools/docker_server.py browser` (`tools/dev/server-browser/Dockerfile`), CI job `server-browser`
- Firmware updates for the Field Box over the field Wi-Fi (phase 4 follow-up, decisions FU3-1 to FU3-14, RFC 0001 Amendment 6 proposed, `protocol/firmware-update.md`; in software and in host tests, not yet run on a real board: BRING_UP step 10c is the bench procedure): the GM uploads a signed release (the image and its `.release.json`) in the console's *Boxes* tab and updates one box or all while the live game has not started; the controller keeps it only if a `[firmware] release_keys` key signed it and sends each eligible box CMD update (opcode 9); the box joins the field Wi-Fi (setup portal or `@wifi`), fetches the release over HTTPS pinned to the field CA of its join (keys 13 and 14, which the C core now reads), verifies the Ed25519 signature with release keys built into its firmware (`CONFIG_ATALAIA_OTA_RELEASE_KEYS`, empty by default: every update refused), refuses a lower or equal version, another board or variant, streams the image into the inactive OTA slot checking its size and SHA-256, and only then sets the boot partition; an interrupted update leaves the running image untouched, and a new image that fails its self-test or crashes before it is rolled back by the bootloader (`CONFIG_BOOTLOADER_APP_ROLLBACK_ENABLE`). The box reports with an HMAC of the game key and its next HELLO shows the version; the game cannot start while an update is on its way. Ed25519 on the box is Monocypher 4.0.2 (vendored, CC0-1.0 or BSD-2-Clause); `tools/firmware_release.py` makes release keys and signs releases (`firmware/esp32/docs/RELEASE_SIGNING.md`); `docker_fw.py` takes `--fw-version=` and `--release-keys=`; the firmware's version is now `PROJECT_VER` (HELLO's `fw_version`). The manifest code is shared by the site server's catalogue, the controller and the tool (`controller/atalaia_controller/firmware_release.py`), with shared vectors in C and Python (`protocol/vectors/firmware.json`, RFC 8032's Ed25519 vectors among them) and a CMD vector for opcode 9
- Node health events (phase 4 follow-up, decisions FU1-1 to FU1-9; in software, tested with the C host box, the real controller and the real site server on PostgreSQL in Docker, not yet on real boards: the bench board has no battery gauge): the field controller in charge logs `node.online` when it first hears a box and `node.offline` when a box it heard is silent for `[health] node_offline_s` (120 s; at most one link event per box a minute, so a flapping link reports where it ended up), and `battery.low` from the level a box's STATE or HELLO reports below `[health] battery_low_pct` (20 %) when the box's own does not come within 30 s, once per crossing with 5 points of hysteresis; their node is the box's id, their seq the controller's own, kept across a restart; the sync takes them to the site server's device health (`server/tests/test_device_health_e2e.py`). The Field Box firmware reads its MAX17048 gauge every 10 s in a joined game and sends one `battery.low` (telemetry, one packet) below `CONFIG_ATALAIA_BATTERY_LOW_PCT` (20), again only 5 points back above it, nothing without a gauge; its HELLO now carries the level. `GET /api/v1/nodes`, the stream and MQTT's `health` topic carry `link` and `battery_low`; the console's *Boxes* list marks a box offline and its battery low. Shared vectors: 3 more valid events (`battery.low`, `node.offline`, `node.online`)
- A Field Box warns in its log when the mode it loads uses 85% or more of its script memory (decision M4-8: on a Heltec V3, the Hacking Terminal with Codes comes close to the 64 KB); the runner's `memory_tight` flag, tested on the host
- Hosted multi-tenant mode on the site server (phase-4-site-server plan phase 21, ADR-P4-4 as built, decisions S21-1 to S21-12; in software, tested against PostgreSQL 16 in Docker, not yet on a real hosted deployment, and not live before the legal review P4-6): `ATALAIA_HOSTED=on` with `ATALAIA_HOSTED_DOMAIN` (off by default: a club's own server works exactly as before). Each field is a PostgreSQL schema `field_<slug>` with every table at the same revision, reached at `https://<slug>.<domain>`; any other host name gets `404 unknown_field`, an API key is checked inside the field its host chose. Every transaction sets `search_path` and `atalaia.tenant` transaction-locally, connections start with no schema on their path, and under that every table has `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and a policy on the schema's own name; the server's role owns nothing, has no `BYPASSRLS` and no `TRUNCATE`, and the server refuses to start otherwise (`atalaia_server/tenancy.py`). Access tokens, lockouts and the remote GM's links are per field; one field per tenant. The operator's commands (`atalaia_server/hosted.py`, owner role): `python -m atalaia_server hosted init|create|list|migrate|suspend|resume|export|delete`, each in the hub's audit log; a field's export is a ZIP of CSV tables with a manifest (mode 0600) that `python -m atalaia_server import-field` loads into a single-field server; deletion drops the field's schema in one transaction, audited with row counts only; `create-admin --field` and the purge per field; `server/docs/DEPLOY.md` "Hosted mode". Tests: `server/tests/test_tenants.py`, adversarial across every surface (host routing, accounts, tokens, lockouts, ids, lists, the sync over HTTP and the WebSocket, the remote GM, history, pages, feeds, scoreboard, clubs, personal-data exports, metrics) and the row-level security as the server's own role
- Game history and the scenario library on the site server (phase-4-site-server plan phase 20, decisions S20-1 to S20-8; in software, tested against the real controller, the real site server and PostgreSQL in Docker, not yet on a real deployment): the field's synced games with filters (`GET /api/v1/fields/{id}/history`), each game's score timeline derived from the per-node counters as they arrived (`/api/v1/games/{id}/timeline`) and its event log by type, team and game time with counts per type (`/log`); staff see everything, other roles a live game `spectator_delay_s` behind (60 s by default) and without secrets, telemetry or player ids; a public scoreboard per field, off by default (`/f/<slug>/scoreboard` and `.json`, no JavaScript); the field's scenario library (`/api/v1/fields/{id}/scenarios`: checked with the controller's own compiler and validator and the core's load check, P4R9; every save a revision; archive) and game presets; migration `0011_history`; the person's game history in access and export (`game_history.json`); ADR-P4-8's per-field history retention (`retention_days`, 30-730)
- Controller `[sync] library = true` (the default): the sync client pulls the field's scenario library and presets (`GET /api/v1/sync/presets`), fetches each manifest that changed (`GET /api/v1/sync/scenarios/{id}`) and installs it as the console does, checking it again in the core; every controller of the field does it, so a standby has the scenario a join names (decision M8-3)
- Shared vectors: 3 valid and 9 invalid answers of `GET /api/v1/sync/presets` and `GET /api/v1/sync/scenarios/{id}` (`answers` in `protocol/vectors/sync.json`, checked by `sync_format.check_presets` and `check_scenario`)
- The keypad's expander is found at any PCF8574 (0x20-0x27) or PCF8574A (0x38-0x3F) address, as listings sell both under one name; the configured address is tried first, and the bus's other parts (NFC 0x24, battery gauge 0x36, OLED 0x3C) are never probed (decision HB-11)
- Scenarios in the controller, console and app (phase-4-site-server plan phase 8, ADR-P4-6 as amended, decisions P4R8, P4R24, M8-1 to M8-9; in software, tested with C host boxes and in Chromium, not yet on radio hardware or a real phone in the field): the controller's `POST /api/v1/scenarios/check` (every problem of a manifest with its place, then the compiled `field.lua`'s load in the core), `POST /api/v1/scenarios` (GM: compile and install, the manifest kept next to the package), `GET /api/v1/scenarios` and `GET /api/v1/scenarios/{id}` (GM: the manifest and the generated files); the GM console's *Cenários* / *Scenarios* tab, a YAML editor checked as it is typed with an example, the boxes to name and the installed scenarios; in the *Join* tab the GM gives each box one of the scenario's names, which the scenario's `field.nodes()` returns (`controller/atalaia_controller/scenario_service.py`)
- Scenario objectives reach the players they are for (P4R24): staff see every objective event; players see an objective by its visibility (`public`, the assignee team's or squad's, a hidden one only once revealed) on the stream, the log and the game, which carries the scenario's names and the objectives each viewer may know; MQTT and other teams learn only that a team completed, failed or was shown an objective
- The app's player view shows the scenario's objectives with their names in the app's language, where each stands (waiting, in progress, completed by a team, failed and why), its team and squad and its points, and names them in the list of what happened
- A game whose id does not fit a join (63 bytes) is refused when it is created; a standby controller that lacks a game's scenario says which part to install (decision M6-1)
- A game picked on the Field Box survives a power loss with its mode's state (decision HB-7): the box saves the script's `state.node` (as JSON, up to 1 KB), the scores and the game's seed with its time every second, and gives them back on resume without replaying the time between, so nothing is counted twice; an armed Bomb comes back armed with the fuse it had left (a new `node.rebooted` handler). Core: `atalaia_node_state_save`, `atalaia_node_state_restore`, `atalaia_node_resume_at`, `atalaia_node_score_restore`. The state file (`/state/current.bin`, version 2) is written to a temporary file and renamed, so a power loss mid-write keeps the last good state. A field game resumes as before (its controller sets the mode up again)
- ATAK / Cursor on Target feed on the field controller (phase-4-site-server plan phase 9, ADR-P4-7 as amended, decision P4R18; in software, tested with loopback receivers, not yet with a real ATAK device: HW3): `controller/atalaia_controller/atak.py` sends every box placed on the field image as a coloured spot marker (`b-m-p-s-m`: name, owner, the owner's colour as `<color>` and, for a team named after an ATAK team colour, `<__group>`, battery) and a VIP / Payload extraction zone, every `interval_s` (30 s) and at once on a capture, neutralisation, move or game change, with `stale` at twice the interval and every marker dropped when the game ends; over UDP to `[atak] multicast` (`239.2.3.1:6969`, TTL 1), one event per datagram (TAK Protocol version 0). **Player positions are never multicast**: only with `players = true`, and only to the staff ATAK devices of `staff_endpoints` (TCP, 4242 by default), by pseudonymous player id, never by name. XML built with ElementTree (escaped, characters XML forbids dropped, texts capped). Off by default (`[atak] enabled = false`, `ATALAIA_ATAK_*`), only on the controller in charge; `GET /api/v1/atak` for its counters. The field image's georeference (`controller/atalaia_controller/georef.py`, `GET`/`PUT`/`DELETE /api/v1/field/georef`): two opposite corners of a north-up image, or 3-8 control points for a rotated one (least-squares affine fit in a local plane), with degenerate, mirrored and inconsistent points refused; kept in the store's settings, cleared by a new image. The GM console's *Campo / Field* tab gets *Posição no mapa (ATAK)*: the two corners pasted as `latitude, longitude`, the four corners shown back and a pin per point on the image (`console/src/components/georef-panel.js`). `[atak]` in `controller.example.toml`. Tests: `controller/tests/test_atak.py` (35), a browser test; decisions M9-1 to M9-10
- Shared vectors `protocol/vectors/cot.json`: the CoT rules the emitter must follow, each copied from its cited source (the CoT base-event schema, MITRE's CoT types, the TAK detail schemas and examples, the TAK protocol description), and the values of two TAK example events (`Marker - Spot.cot`, `Marker - 2525.cot`) that the emitter rebuilds as known answers
- The Heltec V4's own OLED shows the game (`firmware/esp32/main/oled_view.c`, SSD1306 driver `main/hal/esp/hal_esp_oled.c`; checked on a V4.3 board): every screen the TFT gets is mirrored as a header (mode or picker step), one big value (PIN dots, the chosen game, the timer, the countdown, the phase) and a footer (team, hint or message), with a progress bar while a team holds its button; a bare board without the TFT can be set up and played on it. A second LVGL display packed to one bit per pixel, only changed pages sent over the shared I2C bus. A 2 s boot splash with the tower from the site's favicon (`tools/oled_logo.py` writes `main/oled_logo.c`) and the version. Kconfig `ATALAIA_OLED_STATUS` (on), `ATALAIA_OLED_ADDR`, `ATALAIA_OLED_RST_PIN`, `ATALAIA_OLED_FLIP`, `ATALAIA_OLED_SPLASH_MS`. Tests: `test/test_oled_view.c` and 17 reference images in `test/screens/oled/`
- Bomb shows how far a hold is: while the attackers hold, the standby screen gets `progress` (0-1) and an "Arming... keep holding" bar; while the defenders hold, the countdown gets `defuse` and a defuse bar; letting go early empties it (`modes/bomb/tests/hold_progress.yaml`, new TFT references and app goldens)
- Bench variant of the Field Box firmware, `python tools/docker_fw.py bench` (`sdkconfig.bench`): keys from the USB serial console and no setup portal, for a bare board on the desk
- Button bring-up logging: each button's state at rest at boot (a warning when it reads pressed: NC tab or short) and every debounced press and release (`hal_btn`)
- Mode tests: a key given as `null` in `expect_screen`'s `data` must be absent (Lua leaves out `nil` values), in the simulator and the app's Dart runner
- The site server's deployment (phase-4-site-server plan phase 22, ADR-P4-10 as amended; in software, tested in Docker on one computer with Caddy's own certificate authority, not yet on a real server with a real domain and certificate): `server/docker-compose.yml` puts Caddy (`caddy:2.11.7-alpine`, pinned by digest; `server/Caddyfile`) in front, with an automatic certificate, HTTP redirected to HTTPS, the controllers' WebSocket proxied, `Permissions-Policy`, `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy` and `X-Permitted-Cross-Domain-Policies` added and `Server` and `Via` removed; only Caddy publishes ports, the server and PostgreSQL are on internal networks (no outside access, no way out). The image runs one worker (`--workers 1`) with `--proxy-headers`, so rate limits and the audit log see the client's address. `server/setup.sh DOMAIN` writes `server/.env` (mode 0600) with four new 32-byte secrets, never printed, keeps existing values when run again, and starts everything; `server/backup.sh` pipes `pg_dump -Fc` through `python -m atalaia_server.backup_crypt seal` (AES-256-GCM, STREAM construction, `ATALAIA_BACKUP_KEY`) so the dump is never on disk in clear, checks the new backup, keeps 30 days; `server/restore.sh FILE --yes` checks the whole backup first, then restores it into a freshly created database and starts the server; `server/docs/DEPLOY.md` for operators. `python3 tools/docker_server.py up` now runs behind Caddy on `https://localhost:8443`; `python3 tools/docker_server.py deploy` (`tools/server_deploy.py`, new CI job `server-deploy`) tests setup, HTTPS, the WebSocket through Caddy, the backup and its restore into a fresh database with every table compared; decisions S22-1 to S22-10
- Device health, a firmware catalogue and the remote GM (phase-4-site-server plan phase 19, ADR-P4-3's amendment and P4R12; in software, tested against the real controller, the real site server and PostgreSQL in Docker, not yet on a real deployment or over 4G): the site server shows each device's battery, link, firmware and health flags from the synced nodes and `node.online`, `node.offline`, `battery.low` and `node.tamper` events (`GET /api/v1/fields/{field}/devices`, `/api/v1/games/{game}/devices`); a firmware catalogue (`/api/v1/firmware`, migration `0010_firmware`) that lists releases only, stores no image and takes only entries signed with Ed25519 by a key in `ATALAIA_FIRMWARE_KEYS` (none: no entries), checking them again at every listing; and the remote GM: a user with the `gm` role on the field, in a TOTP session, sees the live game of a controller with `[sync] remote_gm = true` and sends it `start`, `pause`, `resume`, `end`, a GM message or a declared mode command (`/api/v1/fields/{field}/remote-gm`, `.../commands`, `.../log`), over the WebSocket the controller opened; the controller checks each command again, refuses all while the setting is off, and both sides log every one (decisions S19-1 to S19-11)
- Controller `[sync] remote_gm` (default `false`): `remote_gm.py` keeps the stream to the site server open with a small RFC 6455 client on the standard library (`ws_client.py`), sends its live view (no positions, tokens or check-ins), carries out the remote GM's commands through the engine and logs them in `remote_commands` (store schema 8), shown at `GET /api/v1/remote-gm`
- Shared vectors: 13 valid and 24 invalid messages of the remote GM on the stream (`stream` in `protocol/vectors/sync.json`, checked by `sync_format.STREAM` on both sides)
- Club profiles and branding sync (phase-4-site-server plan phase 18, P4R19; in software, tested against the real controller, the real site server and PostgreSQL in Docker, not yet on a real deployment): clubs on the site server (`/api/v1/clubs`: name, short name, slug, description, colour, logo, crest; members invited by the club's admins, who accept; the last admin kept) with a public page off by default (`/c/<slug>/`, pt-PT and English, no JavaScript), a report button (a plain form, no address kept, 5 an hour per address) and a server administrator who sees the reports and can hide a page; a field's branding (name, accent, logo, wallpaper) and saved teams (a club's team carries the club's short name and crest) edited by its staff; every image checked by the controller's own `images.py` with the same limits, metadata stripped, stored once by SHA-256, each upload with the rights reminder (`rights_confirmed=true`); migration `0009_clubs`; club membership in access, export (`clubs.json`, `club_reports.json`), erasure and the purge (invitations after 30 days, decided reports after 12 months, unused images after a day); the privacy notice templates list it
- Controller `[sync] branding = "site"` (default `"local"`): the sync client pulls the field's branding and saved teams (`GET /api/v1/sync/field`), fetches the images it lacks by SHA-256 (`GET /api/v1/sync/images/{sha256}`), checks each again with `images.py` and applies them to the field's branding and to team presets of their own; the console then gets 409 for those, its own presets stay the GM's (`protocol/sync.md`, decisions S18-1 to S18-12)
- Shared vectors: 2 valid and 9 invalid answers of `GET /api/v1/sync/field` (`answers` in `protocol/vectors/sync.json`, checked by `sync_format.check_field`)
- Registration import and platform adapters on the site server (phase-4-site-server plan phase 17, ADR-P4-12; in software, tested against PostgreSQL in Docker, no real platform connected): a field's `admin` or `gm` imports a registration list exported from elsewhere into a scheduled game (`POST /api/v1/fields/{field}/scheduled-games/{id}/registrations/import?source=&dry_run=&basis_confirmed=`), CSV per RFC 4180 or JSON, at most 1 MiB and 5,000 rows, reading only `display_name`, `external_ref`, `team_preference`, `federation_number` and `replicas` (any other column is named as not imported and never stored); a dry run previews every row (created, updated, unchanged, skipped, refused with a code) and writes nothing; rows upsert by `(source, external_ref)`, so re-importing the same export changes nothing; imported registrations have no account and no ticket, count toward the places, go in the controller's roster and are deleted 30 days after their game (guests). `GET .../registrations.csv` exports a game's list with every cell safe from spreadsheet formulas, and imports back unchanged. Platform adapters (`atalaia_server/integrations/`): the `PlatformAdapter` interface, the rule that an adapter uses only a platform's documented API or a written agreement (checked when it is registered), per-field links off by default (`/api/v1/fields/{field}/integrations`), configuration encrypted with Fernet and never shown again, publish, update, cancel and fetch registrations only when the staff ask, 20 seconds per call, every call in the audit log; only an in-memory test adapter ships (`ATALAIA_TEST_ADAPTER=on`), no adapter for any named platform (decisions S17-1 to S17-9, S17-17); migration `0008_integrations`; `server/docs/integrations.md`
- The app's online registration (decision S16-13; tested against a stand-in site server, not yet a real deployment or a phone): *Participante › Inscrições online* logs in to a field's site server (password, then TOTP; the access token in memory, the rotating refresh token in the app's private files), lists the field's games (new `GET /api/v1/fields-by-slug/{slug}`), registers with pre-declared replicas, keeps the check-in ticket on the phone (QR code and text, shown at the field) and can send it with the app's check-in, changes and cancels, and claims the phone's player id at the field; the public register page opens it with an `atalaia://register?site=&field=&game=` link or the page's address pasted into the app (decisions S17-10 to S17-16)
- Registration and game-day sync (phase-4-site-server plan phase 16, ADR-P4-11 as amended; in software, tested against the real controller, the real site server and PostgreSQL in Docker, not on a real game day; the app's screens come later): accounts register for a published game on the site server (`/api/v1/fields/{field}/scheduled-games/{id}/registrations`, `/api/v1/me/registrations`) with a display name, a team preference, the federation number when asked and pre-declared replicas (classes of the field's `limit_template`, at most its `max_replicas`), within the window and the places (no waiting list); each registration gets a 32-byte check-in ticket shown once, as the app's secret and as a printable `atalaia:ticket:` code (`checkin_ticket.py`, `app/lib/protocol/checkin_ticket.dart`, 30 shared vectors built by hand from RFC 8949), and only its SHA-256 is kept. An account adopts the player id its phone already has at a field by a claim the field's controller confirms (`/api/v1/me/player-ids`, `GET`/`POST /api/v1/sync/claims`; the controller's hashes never leave it). The controller pulls the schedule (`GET /api/v1/sync/schedule`), makes a game from a scheduled game (`POST /api/v1/schedule/{id}/game`, the console's *From the schedule*), pulls its roster until the game starts (`GET /api/v1/sync/roster`) as **expected** check-ins with their replicas pending in the chrono list, checks them in by the app's ticket (`POST /api/v1/checkin` gains `registration_id` and `ticket`), a marshal's scan (`POST /api/v1/games/{id}/checkins/ticket`) or by name, and after the game pushes each registration's outcome and its declarations' states, never a name (`POST /api/v1/sync/checkins`); the scheduled game follows its game to in progress and completed. Store schema 7; migration `0007_registration`; registrations and claims in access, export, erasure and the purge (the federation number 30 days after the game). The public game page shows *Register* only while registration is open and places are left, and the JSON feed's `registration.open` follows
- Shared vectors: 4 valid and 15 invalid sync bodies (`checkins`, `claims`, a game made from a scheduled game) and 4 valid and 14 invalid answers of the endpoints the controller pulls (`answers` in `protocol/vectors/sync.json`)
- Scheduling, the public field page and open feeds on the site server (phase-4-site-server plan phase 15, ADR-P4-11 and ADR-P4-12; in software, tested against PostgreSQL in Docker): the field's admin and GMs schedule games (`/api/v1/fields/{field}/scheduled-games`: drafts, edits, publication and cancellation with a reason by the admin; times kept in UTC and shown in the field's time zone; migration `0006_scheduling`); a field that turns on its page (`public_page`, with a description, an address and its federation label) gets `/f/<slug>/` and a page per game, server-rendered in Portuguese and English with no JavaScript and nothing loaded from other sites, with schema.org `Event` data; an iCalendar feed per field and per game (`games.ics`, RFC 5545: UTC times, stable UIDs, `SEQUENCE` raised when the time, place or status changes, lines folded at 75 octets, checked against the RFC's own examples) and a JSON feed (`games.json`, `atalaia.games` version 1, with a JSON Schema in `server/schemas/` and `server/docs/feeds.md`), with ETags and open cross-site reads. No personal data on the pages or feeds; drafts never appear. Online registration comes with plan phase 16, so the feed's `registration.open` is false until then. New setting `ATALAIA_PUBLIC_URL` for the links and UIDs
- The controller sync (phase-4-site-server plan phase 13, ADR-P4-3 as amended; in software, tested against the real site server and PostgreSQL in Docker, not yet over a real 4G link): field controllers push their games to a site server, opening every connection themselves (decision P4R12). Site server: `POST /api/v1/sync/games`, `events`, `scores` and `nodes` and `WS /api/v1/sync/stream`, with a field API key (32 random bytes kept only as SHA-256, at most two live during a rotation, managed by the field's admin at `/api/v1/fields/{field}/sync-keys`, P4R13); a game is `(field, game_id, game_seed)` (P4R11), so a standby controller's pushes land in the same game and a reused 16-bit id is a new one; events unique on the controller's own key, so replays, duplicates and out-of-order batches change nothing; score counters per node (and per run of a controller's field script) merged by `GREATEST`, totals derived with every `score.delta`, so a GM's negative adjustment lowers them (P4R10); per-game roles (`user_game_roles`, `/api/v1/games/{game}/roles`) and a small games API (a field's games, a game with its totals, counters and nodes, its events); per-game roles in access, export and erasure, and games purged 24 months after they ended; migration `0004_sync`; sync counters in the metrics. Controller: `sync_client.py` (a thread that only reads the store, so a game never waits for the network; every 30 s in a game and 5 min between, doubling on failure up to 5 min, honouring `Retry-After`, woken by a game's start, pause and end; HTTPS against the system's CAs or `[sync] ca_file`, no redirects followed; its per-game cursor in `sync_state`, store schema 5), `[sync]` settings (off by default), `python3 -m atalaia_controller sync-key` (store a key, `--status`, `--clear`, `--resend`) and `sync` in `GET /api/v1/controller`; never sends positions, check-in names, tokens or keys. The wire format is `protocol/sync.md`, checked on both sides by `controller/atalaia_controller/sync_format.py` against the shared vectors `protocol/vectors/sync.json`. No new dependency on either side. Tests: `server/tests/test_sync.py`, `test_sync_stream.py`, `test_sync_e2e.py` (the real controller engine and client against the site server under uvicorn on the test PostgreSQL), `controller/tests/test_sync_client.py` (against a small site server in the test process, privacy checked field by field) and `test_sync_format.py`; decisions S13-1 to S13-12
- The chrono registry (phase-4-site-server plan phase 14, ADR-P4-5 as amended; in software, tested against the controller's client, the real site server and PostgreSQL in Docker, not yet with a real chronograph): the controller now also pushes the field's marshal public keys with the period each was the marshal's key, its replica registry and every signed chrono reading with its series and outcome (`POST /api/v1/sync/marshals`, `replicas`, `chrono`, also on the stream; field-level bodies with 3 valid and 20 invalid new shared vectors in `protocol/vectors/sync.json`). The site server verifies each reading with the controller's own `chrono.py` against the keys of its marshal valid when the controller received it (`server/atalaia_server/chrono_verify.py`, its tests with the shared chrono vectors and tampered records in `tools/check.sh`), stores it exactly as sent (`chrono_readings.record_json`), keeps the ones that do not verify apart with their reason (`chrono_rejects`, named in the answer, rechecked when the marshal's key arrives; never silently dropped), counts a reading sent twice as a duplicate and another record under a stored id as a conflict, and turns failed or warned spot checks into violations that staff resolve. Public keys only, never a private key (P4R14): the controller now keeps a marshal's earlier keys (`marshal_key_history`) and what it sent of the registry (`sync_items`) in store schema 6; a marshal can register their phone's key from their account, and a field's admin can revoke one. Staff views for the field's admin, gm and marshal (readings, violations, replicas, keys, a player's history), the person's own history (`GET /api/v1/me/chrono`), sharing between fields with both fields switched on and the person's consent for each (`PATCH /api/v1/fields/{field}`, `PUT`/`DELETE /api/v1/me/consents/chrono-sharing/{field}`), a field's erasure of a player's chrono data; the registry in access, export (`player_ids.json`, `chrono_records.json`, `marshal_keys.json`), erasure (the signed records stay, the link to the person goes) and the purge (the field's `default_retention_days`, 365); migration `0005_chrono` (with `field_player_ids` and per-field consents); chrono counters in the metrics. No new dependency. Tests: `server/tests/test_chrono.py`, `test_chrono_verify.py`, the chrono part of `test_sync_e2e.py`, `controller/tests/test_sync_client.py` and `test_sync_format.py`; decisions S14-1 to S14-12
- Site server personal data rights (`server/`, phase-4-site-server plan phase 12, ADR-P4-8; in software): `GET /api/v1/me/data` (everything held about the account: profile without credentials, roles per field, sessions with addresses and user agents, the person's audit entries and the ones others made about the account without who made them, consent records) and `GET /api/v1/me/export` (the same as a ZIP of JSON files with a README in English and Portuguese); rectification with `PATCH /api/v1/me` (username, email) and `POST /api/v1/me/password` (ends every other session), both behind the current password; erasure with `DELETE /api/v1/me` (the password, and a TOTP code for an account with TOTP) that anonymises the account as a `deleted-...` tombstone, deletes its roles, sessions and consents, keeps its audit entries without their addresses and logs the erasure with no account and no address, refusing the last server administrator; consent records with the privacy notice's version (`consents`, `ATALAIA_PRIVACY_NOTICE_VERSION`); the daily retention purge inside the server (`ATALAIA_PURGE_HOUR`, 03:00 UTC by default: sessions 30 days after they end, the audit log after 36 months) and `python -m atalaia_server purge [--dry-run]`; migration `0003_personal_data`; tests in `test_personal_data.py`; decisions S12-1 to S12-8
- Templates for field operators, to be reviewed by a lawyer before use (decision P4-6): the site server's privacy notice in Portuguese and English (`docs/templates/privacy-notice-pt.md`, `privacy-notice-en.md`) and a data processing agreement for a hosted server (`docs/templates/dpa-template.md`)
- Core: geo utilities for node and field scripts (ADR-P4-9, phase-4-site-server plan phase 3; in software): `geo.distance(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2)` in metres (haversine on a 6,371 km sphere) and `geo.in_zone(lat, lon, zone)` for a circle (`lat`, `lon`, `radius_m`) or a polygon of 3 to 32 `{lat, lon}` points, a point on the edge counting as inside; wrong arguments are script errors (`core/include/atalaia/geo.h`, `core/src/geo.c`). The results are the same to the bit on every platform, so replays agree: only `+ - * /` and `sqrt`, its own sine, cosine and arc tangent instead of the C library's, compiled with `-ffp-contract=off` (decisions M3-1 to M3-3)
- Shared vectors `protocol/vectors/geo.json` (generated into `core/tests/geo_vectors.h` by `tools/protogen.py`): known answers from Rosetta Code (haversine, ray casting) and GeographicLib (a WGS 84 geodesic) with their tolerances, arcs along a meridian and the equator, antipodes, edge cases and argument errors, and every distance's exact bits; run by `core/tests/test_geo.c` and `simulator/tests/test_geo.py` (from Lua through the shared library). The same bits were checked on arm64 and armv7 (gcc and clang) and on the ESP32 and ESP32-S3 in QEMU; the Field Box image grows by 5.4 KB
- Site server accounts, sessions and roles (`server/`, phase-4-site-server plan phase 11; in software): registration with consent, the NIST SP 800-63B password policy (10 characters or more, no character-class rules, not the username, not one of the UK NCSC's 100,000 most breached passwords) and Argon2id hashes with ADR-P4-2's parameters (re-hashed at login when they change); login, then a TOTP step (RFC 6238, 6 digits, one step of drift, each code accepted once, seeds Fernet-encrypted at rest) for accounts that have it; 15-minute JWT access tokens whose session and roles are re-read from the database on every request, so a logout or a removed role counts at once; refresh tokens in an HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Strict cookie limited to the refresh path, stored only as SHA-256, rotated on every use, a replayed one ending the whole session; TOTP enrolment, confirmation and removal; lockouts (5 failed logins per address, 5 wrong codes per account, 15 minutes each), 10 registrations an hour and 100 requests a minute per address; fields and per-field roles (player, squad_leader, vip, marshal, gm, spectator, admin) with TOTP required, and a TOTP login, for admin, gm and marshal; the audit log for every login, failure, lockout, replay and role change; `python -m atalaia_server create-admin`; `GET /api/v1/metrics` for server administrators (or the optional metrics token); migration `0002_accounts`; new hash-pinned dependencies argon2-cffi 25.1.0, PyJWT 2.15.1 and pyotp 2.10.0; tests with the RFC 6238 and RFC 4226 test vectors; decisions S11-1 to S11-15
- The site server's skeleton (`server/`, phase-4-site-server plan phase 10; in software, no accounts yet): a FastAPI app factory (`atalaia_server.app:create_app`), settings from `ATALAIA_*` variables (a wrong value stops the start; the database password never logged), SQLAlchemy 2's async engine on asyncpg against PostgreSQL 16, the base models with UUIDv7 keys (RFC 9562, checked against its example), Alembic with the first reversible migration `0001_base` (fields, users, roles seeded with ADR-P4-1's seven, per-field roles, sessions, the audit log), `GET /api/v1/health` (200 healthy, or 503 when the database does not answer), `GET /api/v1/metrics` behind a bearer token until admin accounts exist, and security headers on every response (HSTS, `nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options`, a content security policy, the referrer policy); decisions S10-1 to S10-12
- The site server's image (`server/Dockerfile`): the core compiled in its own stage so a scenario's Lua can be checked as the controller checks it (P4R9), then `python:3.12.14-slim` pinned by digest with every Python package pinned to a release at least two weeks old and installed by its PyPI hashes (`server/requirements.txt`, `requirements-test.txt` for the tests), the Python shared with the controller, an unprivileged user and no compiler; `server/docker-compose.yml` (the server and `postgres:16-alpine`, for a local trial) and `server/docker-compose.test.yml` (an ephemeral PostgreSQL in a tmpfs)
- `python3 tools/docker_server.py`: `test [names]` and `migrations` (upgrade head, downgrade base, upgrade head, `alembic check`) in the test image next to an ephemeral PostgreSQL 16, removed afterwards; `build`; `up` and `down` for a local trial on `127.0.0.1:8000`
- Site server tests (`unittest` on PostgreSQL 16 only, decision P4R17; a fresh database per test class): health and metrics, security headers on errors too, the served app over real HTTP and its clean stop, every migration's downgrade restoring the schema exactly, the migrations building exactly the models, the shared Python (chrono, player tokens, images, the mode loader) importing without FastAPI, pydantic or SQLAlchemy, and every built-in mode loading in the image's core; the ones without a database also run in `tools/check.sh` and CI's `controller` job
- CI job `server`: builds the test image and runs the same `sh tests/run.sh test` and `migrations` against a `postgres:16-alpine` service container
- The scenario compiler and validator (`controller/atalaia_controller/scenarios/`, phase-4-site-server plan phase 7, ADR-P4-6 as amended, decisions P4-1, P4R7, P4R8; in software, not yet on radio hardware): a YAML (or JSON) scenario manifest with nodes named by the GM, teams, numbered squads and objectives of seven types (`capture_and_hold`, `defend`, `enter_code`, `scan_tags`, `destroy`, `escort` by `position.fix`, `survive` against Respawn's lives), each with an assignee, a visibility, dependencies, a time window and an effect (points, a scenario parameter's points, a win, reveals), compiles to a `kind: scenario` package (`mode.yaml` with the objectives' names, assignees and visibility; `field.lua`: a data table and the shared scenario runtime `runtime.lua`) over `org.atalaia.objectives`. The validator reports every problem with its path, a code and a reason: unique ids, references, a dependency graph without cycles, each type's target, role and parameters, teams and squads, time windows, destinations (a radius under 10 m warns, crossing polygon edges are an error), and sizes (every setup command fits a box's 127-byte command, every scenario event a LoRa packet). No manifest text is ever Lua code: every value is written as an escaped Lua literal. Command line `python3 -m atalaia_controller.scenarios validate|check|compile` (also `python3 -m atalaia_controller scenario ...`); `check` and `compile` load the generated Lua in the core as an upload does (P4R9). Plain Python (PyYAML only), importable by the site server without its web stack. Shared vectors `protocol/vectors/scenario.json` (5 valid manifests, 54 invalid ones with their problems). Tests: `controller/tests/test_scenarios.py` (every type played in the simulator, the multi-objective example's dependency chain, hostile names round-tripped through the core) and the hand-written Foxhound recompiled from `controller/tests/scenarios/foxhound.yaml`, passing the objectives package's six YAML tests that play it (decisions M7-1 to M7-9)
- Scenario packages and the objectives role package (phase-4-site-server plan phase 6, decision P4R7; in software, not yet on radio hardware): a new manifest kind, `scenario` (a `mode.yaml` and a `field.lua`, no `node.lua`, no roles), uploaded to a controller and never built in, leads a game's id over at least one role package (`org.club.foxhound+org.atalaia.objectives`, optionally with one main mode after it); its parameters and its id never reach a node's join, which names what the node runs. The built-in role package `org.atalaia.objectives` ("Objetivos"; roles `objective` and `bomb_site`, no parameters) waits for the scenario's mode command `setup` and then is a point to capture, a code to type, player tags to scan or a bomb site, reporting the standard `zone.captured`, `code.used` (without the code), `tag.scanned` and `bomb.*` and never scoring; after a reboot the scenario sets it up again (an armed bomb goes on with the fuse it had left). Screens `obj_status`, `obj_capture`, `obj_code`, `obj_tags`, `obj_bomb` in Portuguese and English (the language and the objective's name come in the setup). The composition rules changed in the simulator (`atalaia_sim.mode`, which the controller uses), the app's catalogue (a join never names a scenario) and the Dart test runner, all against the new shared vectors `protocol/vectors/composition.yaml`; the controller accepts scenario uploads and refuses a scenario in its built-in folders. A hand-written example scenario, `modes/objectives/tests/fixtures/foxhound` (capture, code, tags, a bomb site, a dependency, the scenario's own parameter), proves the runtime before the compiler (plan phase 7). Tests: 9 event sequences (in Python and in Dart through the core), `simulator/tests/test_scenario.py`, `controller/tests/test_scenario.py`, the real controller with two host boxes on the host modem (a box rebooted and set up again), a console browser test, the Field Box host tests, fifteen reference images and five app goldens (decisions M6-1 to M6-9)
- Events `scenario.objective.completed` (42), `scenario.objective.failed` (43), `scenario.objective.revealed` (44) and `scenario.started` (45), all critical, and data keys `objective` (34) and `scenario` (35) in `protocol/schema/events.yaml`, with four shared vectors built by hand from RFC 8949, the JSON Schema and an example (draft RFC 0000 Amendment 4); `objective` is `gm_only` on 42-44 until the player view routes objectives per team (M6-5, P4R24); the console's feed (which objective, for staff) and the players' feed in words
- The simulator's and the Dart runner's test step `reboot` (a node loses power and comes back in the running game, reporting `node.rebooted`) and test key `scenario:` (a scenario package leading the game); a `field.send` whose parameters do not fit a box's 128-byte CMD buffer is a problem that fails the test
- Field Box: the objectives package is the thirteenth built-in preset (the picker's second page, fourth entry), so boxes can play their part in any scenario
- VIP / Payload, "VIP / Carga" in Portuguese (`modes/vip_payload`, phase-4-site-server plan phase 5, decision P4R6; the first mode to use `geo.in_zone` and `geo.distance`): the `vip_team` escorts its VIP (a checked-in player with the role `vip` who shares their position) or a payload (an Android prop with GPS) to an extraction zone, `radius_m` (25 m) around `extraction` (`latitude,longitude`); the controller hands a VIP's GPS reports to `field.lua` only, as `position.fix`, and the payload checks the zone on the phone from `gps.fix`, so positions never become events; the extraction, once: `vip.extracted`, `vip_points` (100) and with `end_on_extract` the game won by the escort; checkpoint boxes captured as in Domination score `checkpoint_points` for every `tick_s` seconds held, counted by `field.lua`; screens `checkpoint_free`, `checkpoint_held` and `payload` (the metres left to the zone) in Portuguese and English; 8 event sequences (in Python and in Dart through the core), simulator tests, the controller (a VIP token's report ends the game; no coordinate on the stream, MQTT or the log), a prop session with fake fixes, Field Box host tests and five reference images, four app goldens, a browser test of the console; in software, not yet with real GPS in the field (HW3; decisions M5-3 to M5-7)
- Event `vip.extracted` (46, critical; `player`, key 14, when the VIP was extracted) in `protocol/schema/events.yaml`, with two shared vectors built by hand from RFC 8949, the JSON Schema and an example (draft RFC 0000 Amendment 3); the console's feed and the players' feed in words
- The simulator's and the Dart runner's test steps `position` (a player's position report for `field.lua`) and `gps` (a prop's own fix); an event whose data carries a position-like key (`lat`, `lon`, `position`, `gps`...) is a problem that fails the test (P4R6)
- Android prop: a role that uses `gps` gets the phone's fixes as `gps.fix` (one without a position when the game starts, then every 2 s); they stay on the phone
- Field Box: VIP / Payload is the twelfth built-in preset (a checkpoint; the picker's second page, third entry), so checkpoint boxes can join a controller's game (decision M5-6, amending P4R23)
- `gm_only` data and parameters (decision M5-1): an event's fields listed in `gm_only` in `protocol/schema/events.yaml` (generated by `tools/protogen.py`) and a mode's parameters marked `gm_only: true` in `mode.yaml` reach staff only (the GM token, marshals, gm player tokens)
- The Hacking Terminal, "Terminal de Hacking" in Portuguese (`modes/hacking_terminal`, phase-4-site-server plan phase 4, decision P4R5): a box or an Android prop with a keypad sets a puzzle of `digits` (3-6, default 4): crack a code of different digits from the right-place and wrong-place counts of the last three guesses (`code`, the default), cut numbered wires in the right order (`wires`), or repeat a sequence of keys shown one a second (`sequence`); a wrong answer locks it for `lockout_s` (10 s) and the puzzle stays; puzzles come from `rand()`, seeded with the game's seed and the node's id, so a replay sets the same ones; the team that pressed its button last, or the terminal's `team`, solves it: `hacking.solved`, `points` (10) for the team, the `reveal_code` on the screen, and with `capture` the terminal becomes the team's zone and sets a new puzzle for the other teams; the GM's "New puzzle" command; screens `hack_code`, `hack_wires`, `hack_sequence` and `hack_solved` in Portuguese and English (`lang`); 13 event sequences (in Python and in Dart through the core), the real controller with a host box on the host modem (the sequence read off the box's screen and typed back), Field Box host tests and ten reference images, five app goldens, a browser test of the console; in software, not yet on a phone in the field (HW3; decisions M4-1 to M4-9)
- Event `hacking.solved` (47, critical) and data key `puzzle` (36) in `protocol/schema/events.yaml`, with a shared vector, the JSON Schema and an example (draft RFC 0000 Amendment 2); the GM console's feed in words with the revealed code, the app's players' feed without it
- Field Box: the Hacking Terminal is the eleventh built-in preset (the picker's second page, second entry); a game picked on the box gets a new seed for `rand()` from `esp_random()` (it was always 42); the host box prints the mode's screens (`SCREEN <template> <data>`) as it already printed the shell's
- Android prop: a role that requires the keypad shows the on-screen keypad from the start
- Target Windows, "Janelas de Alvo" in Portuguese (`modes/target_windows`, phase-4-site-server plan phase 2, design specification section 15): a target (role `target`, requires `hit` and `led`) stays dark for `interval_s` (20 s), then shows a random team's colour for `window_s` (5 s), the team picked from `game.teams()` with the game's seeded `rand()`; a hit of at least `min_strength` (40 of 255) in the window scores `hit_points` (5) for that team and logs `target.hit` with `window_open: true`, the first hit closing the window unless `multi_hit`; a hit while dark logs `window_open: false` and scores nothing; owner overrides are ignored; screens `standby` and `window`; 7 tests in the simulator and in Dart through the core, three Field Box reference images and three app goldens. The logic only, in software: the target addon and its driver are hardware work (HW2), so it is not a Field Box preset and no Android prop offers it (decisions P4R4, P4R23, M2-2 to M2-5)
- Core: `game.teams()`, the game's team names in order, for node and field scripts (decision P4R26), with C, simulator and Dart tests
- The `hit` capability (design specification section 7) for scripts and the capability proxy: the input `hit.hit` with `strength` 0-255, the commands `arm` and `disarm`, as draft RFC 0000 Amendment 1 and a dated refinement of `protocol/capability-proxy.md`; three new shared vectors (a target addon's IDENT, a hit EVT, an arm CMD) through the C, Python and Dart codecs; the app's pod client turns a lent sensor's hits into `hit.hit`
- Field Box: the keypad picker shows nine presets a page, `0` turns to the next page (`CHOOSE A GAME 1/2`, `0 more` in the footer), so every built-in preset, Master and Satellites included, can be picked on the box; nothing changes with nine presets or fewer; host tests and two reference images (decision M2-1, closing M1-4)
- Master and Satellites, "Mestre e Satélites" in Portuguese (`modes/master_satellites`, phase-4-site-server plan phase 1, design specification section 15): boxes with the role `master` or `satellite` capture as in Domination and do not score; the controller's `field.lua` scores the master's holder every `tick_s` (10 s) with `points_per_tick` (2) plus `satellite_bonus_pct` (50) % of it for every satellite the same team holds, rounded down (whole points), nothing below `min_satellites` (0), and frees every satellite with `field.send(id, "set_owner", { team = "none" })` when the master changes hands; no new events (satellites send `zone.captured` and `zone.neutralized`); screens `standby`, `captured` and `master_status` (the master's satellites and points per tick, told by the controller with the mode command `status`); a built-in Field Box preset (the tenth: on the picker's second page since plan phase 2, decisions M1-4 and M2-1); tests in the simulator (8 event sequences, in Python and in Dart through the core), the real controller with two host boxes on the host modem, the Field Box host tests and reference images, and app goldens; in software, not yet on radio hardware (decisions M1-1 to M1-5)
- The I/O pod firmware (ADR-P3-3, phase-3-android-players plan phase F): a third image of the ESP32 project (`CONFIG_ATALAIA_POD_MODE`, `sdkconfig.pod`, `python tools/docker_fw.py pod`, about 650 KB in a 2 MB slot) that lends a Heltec V4's team buttons, PCF8574 keypad, LED ring, amplifier, PN532 NFC reader, enclosure switch and LoRa radio to an Android phone over USB (the board's USB-Serial-JTAG port) and Bluetooth LE (a NimBLE GATT server: the pod service with Pod TX, Pod RX and Pod Status, one phone, a 247-byte MTU, an encrypted and bonded link required); IDENT, EVT with ACKs and resends, CMD acted on once, and the radio passthrough with the USB modem's own code (AES-CCM, the channel's duty cycle and power, MEASURE); the game key forgotten when its phone goes (`firmware/esp32/main/pod/`, decisions PF1-PF15)
- The capabilities a pod lends, listed in IDENT only when fitted: `btn.1`-`btn.4` (button N is the game's N-th team, as on a Field Box), `keypad`, `ring`, `audio` (the mode's clips as tones), `nfc` (a player tag's record; join cards not forwarded) and `case`, a new `tamper` type whose `opened` the phone reports as `node.tamper` (`pod_opened`); the app sends `team_display` and `audio` commands to the pod's parts by type (decisions PF6-PF8; `protocol/capability-proxy.md`)
- An Android prop on the field's radio through a pod (decision PF9): `app/lib/radio/pod_radio_link.dart` (a box's field link in Dart over the pod's RADIO_* frames: CONFIGURE with a stored new epoch, HELLO until acknowledged, events with ACKs and resends, STATE at most every 15 s, TIME and commands from the controller it follows) with Dart codecs for the packet header, HELLO, STATE, ACK, CMD and TIME checked against the shared vectors (`rf_codec.dart`); `DualLink` keeps the radio for when the Wi-Fi link is down, and HELLO's `transports` gets the LoRa bit with a pod; a joined game without the controller's addresses runs on the radio alone; the prop screen shows the radio link's state
- RADIO_NOISE (`0x0B`) in the capability proxy, the modem's NOISE: MEASURE works through a pod (C, Python and Dart codecs, two new shared vectors; decision PF2)
- The host pod (`firmware/esp32/test/pod_host`): the pod's own C code with mbedtls on Linux, USB and Bluetooth links as TCP sockets, the radio as UDP, the hardware on stdin, built by `tools/check.sh`, `tools/docker_app.py` and CI; tests of the pod client over both links, of the radio link and `DualLink` against the host pod and the host modem, and of a prop session on the pod's radio (`app/test/pod/pod_host_test.dart`, `app/test/radio/`, `app/test/prop/prop_pod_session_test.dart`); the controller with its modem and a pod on one air (`controller/tests/test_pod_e2e.py`); the pod's proxy and capabilities (`firmware/esp32/test/test_pod.c`) and its GATT service with the Bluetooth stack stubbed (`test_ble_gatt_stub.c`, `ctest -R ble_gatt_stub`)
- CI job `esp32-pod-build` (the pod image, failing on any warning); `flutter-test` and `controller` build the host pod
- `firmware/esp32/docs/BRING_UP.md`: the I/O pod's checks on a board with a phone (USB, pairing, inputs and outputs, the enclosure switch, the radio, losing the phone, hours on battery)
- Field and team branding (maintainer request, decisions BR1-BR24): a field or club running its own controller sets its name (the check-in poster's), a logo, an optional wallpaper and an accent colour in the GM console's *Settings* (GM token), kept by the controller across restarts and readable without a token (`GET /api/v1/field/branding` with an ETag; `/api/v1/field/branding/logo` and `/wallpaper` with the image's SHA-256 as ETag and 304s; a `branding` stream message); teams get a display name and a crest per game next to their colour, which stays the team's name for the boxes and the radio (`team_info` on `POST /api/v1/games`, `PUT /api/v1/games/{id}/teams/{team}` until the game ends, `POST /api/v1/crests`, `GET /api/v1/crests/{id}`, `GET /api/v1/games/{id}/teams`), and saved teams keep a club's name, crest and usual colour (`/api/v1/team-presets`); shown in the app (the logo in the header, the wallpaper dimmed behind the check-in's page, the player's view and an Android prop's idle screens but never behind a mode's screen, the accent on the primary buttons made readable at 4.5:1, crests and names in the player's view and on a prop's team buttons; kept per field id; the default look without branding), the GM console's header and scores, the scoreboard and the check-in poster (the logo above the field's name, outside the QR code)
- Uploaded images are PNG, JPEG or WebP only (SVG and GIF refused, the type read from the file itself): logo up to 1 MiB and 16-2048 px a side, wallpaper up to 4 MiB and 320-4096 px, crest up to 512 KiB and 16-1024 px, animated images refused; the controller checks each file's structure and strips its metadata (EXIF, XMP, text chunks, comments) with the standard library alone (`controller/atalaia_controller/images.py`; no image library added, decision BR7), stores it once by its hash and serves it with `nosniff` and a sandboxing CSP; the console reminds the uploader that they are responsible for the image's rights
- Tests: the controller (`test_branding.py`: real and broken images, every endpoint, ETags, persistence, crests, team presets, clean-up), the GM console in Chromium (settings to header, scoreboard and poster, whose QR code still reads back with `zbarimg`; saved teams and a game with crests; the read token), Dart unit, widget and golden tests (`app/test/branding/`, `app/test/goldens/images/branding/`), and the on-device check-in test with the branding over the pinned HTTPS
